Chapter 51: The Grounded Ritualistic Magic

Use magic to settle an IOU?

Was Old Neil planning to curse his creditor to death? Or create counterfeit banknotes?

I may not be able to solve the problem, but I can eliminate the person demanding payment?

All manner of alarming possibilities crossed Klein's mind as he stared strangely at Old Neil.

He seriously considered calling the police—or rather, reporting the matter to the Nighthawks.

Old Neil glanced at him irritably.

"I can see ignorance, stupidity, weakness, and shameless disbelief in your eyes. Didn't Dunn tell you the maxim of the Mystery Pryers? 'Do as you wish, but do no harm!'"

Although the maxim originated with the secretive and now-corrupted Moses Ascetic Order, generations of Beyonders following its pathway had confirmed its value. Strict observance, combined with proper fear and reverence, significantly reduced the risk of losing control. The consequences of violating it had been demonstrated just as thoroughly.

"Your suspicion is an insult to every Mystery Pryer!"

"I'm sorry." Klein apologized without hesitation.

He truly had forgotten Dunn's mention of the maxim.

Old Neil wasn't genuinely angry. A moment later, he chuckled.

"A pity so few Beyonders choose Seer. Your pathway has no corresponding maxim to guide you."

But I have Emperor Roselle's diary. And strict adherence to a maxim already resembles "acting"...

The thought occurred to Klein, and he nodded contemplatively.

Old Neil said no more. He removed the vase and other ornaments from the round table and placed them in a corner.

Then he took one crimson candle and one black candle from the silver chest.

"When ordinary people attempt ritualistic magic, they must consult astromantic calculations or the appropriate manuals to select a suitable date and hour. They might choose a day symbolizing the Goddess, for example, and a period governed by the moon.

"We Beyonders—particularly those skilled in this field—don't require such assistance. Our keen spiritual perception and powerful Astral Projections are the essential factors.

"Of course, if you lack confidence in a ritual, selecting the appropriate date and time will improve its chance of success.

"But all of this rests upon one premise. Remember it well and obey the rule without exception."

Old Neil placed the candles on the table, then turned toward Klein with an unusually solemn expression.

"Low-Sequence Beyonders aren't powerful enough to perform much magic independently. Nearly every ritual available to us seeks outside assistance. Therefore, appeal only to an orthodox deity such as the Goddess or the Lord of Storms.

"Never—absolutely never—attempt to communicate with an unknown or unpredictable existence, no matter how many people worship it or how tempting the promises recorded in some ancient text may appear.

"Believe me, you must never take that chance. A single attempt places you on a slope leading into an inescapable abyss. Every struggle afterward can only slow the descent. Nothing can reverse it."

"I'll remember," Klein answered gravely, though fear stirred within him.

My luck-enhancement ritual seems to seek power from an unknown and unpredictable existence.

And it gave me the ability to pull people above the gray fog—something even an experienced Beyonder like The Hanged Man finds inconceivable. At least, I assume he's experienced.

Fortunately, I haven't gone mad or shown signs of losing control.

Uneasy, Klein deliberately changed the subject.

"Then Nighthawks should always seek help from the Goddess?"

"No one will stop you from praying to the Lord of Storms," Old Neil replied jokingly. "But we have no way to tell whether He answers with malicious intent, and the result may be distorted in entirely unpredictable ways."

The answer immediately discouraged Klein from experimenting.

There was no universally "best" choice—only the one required by the circumstances.

After delivering his warning, Old Neil lifted the crimson candle.

"A candle made from moon flowers and dark-red sandalwood represents the Goddess in Her aspect as the Lady of Crimson."

He pointed to the black candle.

"One made from night vanilla and slumber flowers represents Evernight."

He placed the black candle at the upper-left side of the round table and the red one at the upper right.

Klein asked, "Why represent the Goddess with only two candles? She is also the Mother of Concealment, Empress of Misfortune and Horror, and Mistress of Repose and Silence."

Old Neil chuckled. "Excellent. That's precisely the question I wanted you to ask.

"Before its corruption, the Moses Ascetic Order maintained an excellent relationship with the Church. Its beliefs and achievements in ritualistic magic profoundly influenced our own traditions.

"The order taught that all things could be expressed through numbers and that every number possessed spirituality. In ritualistic magic, zero represents the unknown or chaos—the universe before creation. One represents the beginning, the original Creator. Two represents the world and the deities born from His body. Three represents the contact between divinity and matter, from which all things arose.

"Thus, these two candles represent the Goddess, while the third represents you. Which aspects and symbols the first two candles embody depends upon the ritual's intended result."

Three gives rise to all things?[1] Everything arises from three?

Klein couldn't help remembering a saying from his previous life.

Seeing him listen attentively, Old Neil removed a third candle.

"This represents me. It's an ordinary candle containing only a little mint. Remember that roses, lemons, mint, moon flowers, night vanilla, and slumber flowers are all cherished by the Goddess.

"Viewed another way, the three candles may also symbolize each person's body, spirituality, and divinity."

After finishing his explanation, Old Neil placed the third candle in the center of the round table.

From the chest, he then removed a bottle of prepared Full Moon Essence Oil, a large cauldron engraved with the Dark Sacred Emblem, an ornate silver knife, a cup of water, and a saucer of coarse salt.

"Beyonders without talent for ritualistic magic require bells, crystal balls, silver cups, incense, and other aids. Mystery Pryers and Seers have no need of so many objects. This equipment is sufficient for us."

Old Neil placed the imitation goatskin IOU below the cauldron and held down one corner with a special quill.

Turning to Klein, he continued, "Ritualistic magic requires a pure spiritual environment free from disturbance. We must create that environment ourselves. First, enter Cogitation and focus the mind. Then use an appropriate tool to draw out your power and construct a barrier.

"The apartment at Ray Bieber's home was sealed using Holy Night Powder. Today I'll use a ritual silver dagger.

"Every symbol and incantation must correspond to the desired result. Hermes is generally the best language for incantations. Ancient Hermes arose from the forces of nature, much like ancient Dragonese and ancient Elvish. Its effects are extremely direct, but it lacks the concealment and protection introduced by later revisions, making it dangerous to the caster. It is also more powerful.

"Now I need to concentrate. I won't explain further while performing the ritual. Watch and listen carefully, remember your questions, and ask them afterward."

"Understood."

Klein stepped back twice and fixed his attention on Old Neil.

The old man's eyes rapidly darkened as an invisible wind began circling him.

After a moment of silence, Old Neil moved from left to right, then top to bottom, using his spirituality to create friction and ignite the three candles one after another.

He picked up the silver knife and thrust it into the coarse salt. Then he chanted in Hermes:

"I sanctify you, blade of pure silver.

"I cleanse and purify you, that you may serve me in this ritual.

"In the name of the Evernight Goddess, the Lady of Crimson...

"You are sanctified!"

After the final short, forceful syllable, Old Neil removed the knife and dipped it into the cup of clear water. Then he raised it and pointed beyond the edge of the table.

Keeping the blade directed outward, he walked a complete circle around the table. With every step, Klein felt invisible energy pour from the silver knife. Rich with spirituality, it joined with the surrounding air and formed a perfectly sealed wall.

Once Old Neil completed the circuit, the altar was isolated from the outside world.

He stood before the table, set down the knife, and picked up the Full Moon Essence Oil. He placed three drops on each candle—the black, the crimson, and the ordinary one.

Hiss!

Thin mist rose, veiling the scene in mystery.

Old Neil returned the bottle to the table and silently contemplated the imitation goatskin IOU for two minutes. Then he lifted the quill and drew a symbol of his own devising: a square surrounding the entire document, signifying that he controlled the debt.

Next he drew a cross through it, symbolizing cancellation.

At that point, Old Neil picked up the parchment in one hand and tapped his glabella with the other, activating Spirit Vision.

Another surge of invisible, vigorous energy bloomed as he whispered in Hermes:

"I pray for the power of the dark night.

"I pray for the power of crimson.

"I pray for the Goddess's loving grace.

"Please provide me with the funds to pay this debt.

"Night vanilla, herb belonging to the red moon, bestow your power upon my incantation.

"Moon flower, herb belonging to the red moon, bestow your power upon my incantation."

Listening from the side, Klein was utterly dumbfounded.

That incantation actually works?

Even if it is spoken in Hermes...

Isn't it far too plain and practical?

What if the Goddess becomes angry and multiplies the debt instead?

The candle flames suddenly flared.

After completing the incantation, Old Neil closed his eyes for two minutes. Then he picked up the Full Moon Essence Oil and placed one more drop on each candle.

He brought the parchment to the candle representing himself. As soon as it caught fire, he threw it into the cauldron.

Old Neil closed his eyes once more, apparently sensing the IOU as it burned.

When he opened them, only ash remained inside the cauldron beneath the Dark Sacred Emblem.

"Praise the Lady!"

Old Neil touched four points on his chest to form the crimson moon. Then he extinguished the candles in the reverse order from which he had lit them.

Finally, he used the silver knife to cut open the invisible wall surrounding the altar.

A powerful gust swept through the room. Old Neil released a breath of relief.

"It's finished."

"That's all?" Klein asked blankly. "The debt is settled? How?"

"I don't know." Old Neil spread his hands with a smile. "Some reasonable means of repayment will present itself."

Klein couldn't decide what expression—or words—could properly answer that.

Isn't this terribly unreliable?

[1] A Daoist saying states: "The Dao gives birth to One; One gives birth to Two; Two gives birth to Three; Three gives birth to all things."

Chapter 52: Spectator

"Stop thinking about the damned IOU. Let's discuss ritualistic magic."

With a relaxed expression, Old Neil began packing away the candles, cauldron, silver knife, and other equipment.

Klein wanted to shrug like an American from his previous life but couldn't bring himself to make such an ungentlemanly gesture.

Instead, he concentrated on the ritual and asked detailed questions about everything that had puzzled him. Old Neil answered each in turn.

Incantations, for example, followed a particular structure. As long as that structure was respected and the essential meaning expressed in Hermes, the remaining language could be composed freely. Naturally, blasphemous or disrespectful descriptions were strictly forbidden.

The lesson continued until noon, when Old Neil coughed twice.

"We should return to Zouteland Street."

Then he muttered indistinctly, "Collecting those damned materials cost me my beloved breakfast."

Both amused and puzzled, Klein looked around.

"Mr. Neil, don't you employ a cook—or at least a maid responsible for meals?"

A salary of twelve pounds a week could support several servants.

According to newspaper advertisements, an ordinary cook receiving room and board cost between twelve and fifteen soli per week—not even one pound. A general maid was cheaper still, earning from three soli six pence to six soli weekly, though no one could reasonably expect culinary skill at that price.

Wait. Old Neil owes thirty pounds. Of course he doesn't keep servants.

It seems I've asked another question I shouldn't have.

While Klein regretted speaking, Old Neil shook his head without offense.

"I regularly perform rituals, study extraordinary objects, and read related documents at home. I neither wish nor am able to employ ordinary cooks, valets, or maids. Someone comes to clean periodically, nothing more. And if the servants weren't ordinary, do you imagine they would accept such work?"

"It was a foolish question," Klein admitted self-deprecatingly. "Perhaps I asked because I never conduct mystical work at home."

Old Neil had already risen and put on his round felt hat. As he walked toward the door, he rambled hungrily.

"I can almost smell fried foie gras. Once that IOU is settled, I'll certainly order some. For lunch, I'll have roast pork glazed with apple juice. No, that won't be enough. I must add sausage with mashed potatoes..."

You're making me hungry too.

Klein swallowed, caught up with him, and headed toward the nearest public carriage stop.

After they returned to Zouteland Street and stepped from the carriage, Old Neil suddenly grunted.

"What is that? Goddess, what am I seeing?"

He became as nimble as a seventeen-year-old, rushing to the curb and picking something up.

Curious, Klein leaned closer. It was a finely made wallet.

His inexperience kept him from identifying whether the dark-brown leather came from buffalo or sheep, but he noticed a small pale-blue emblem embroidered on one side: a white dove spreading its wings in preparation for flight.

That was his first impression. His second was of the thick stack of banknotes bulging from within.

More than twenty gray notes printed in black ink were visible—gold pounds.

Old Neil opened the wallet and withdrew the money. After examining it carefully, he chuckled.

"Ten-pound notes bearing the honorable Founder and Protector, William I. Goddess above, there are thirty of them. And several five-pound, one-pound, and five-soli notes besides."

More than three hundred pounds? That's an enormous fortune by any standard. I might not earn that much in ten years.

Klein's breathing grew heavy despite himself.

Finding such a wallet was equivalent to discovering a briefcase filled with cash in his previous life.

"I wonder what gentleman lost it. He certainly isn't an ordinary person," Klein observed calmly.

The wallet clearly didn't belong to a woman.

"His identity doesn't matter." Old Neil chuckled. "We didn't perform a divination to steal money belonging to someone else. We'll wait here. Whoever lost this will return very soon. No one, no matter how wealthy, casually abandons such a sum."

Klein released a breath of relief and gained an entirely new appreciation of Old Neil's principles.

He had worried that the old man might call the discovery a gift from the Goddess and use it to repay his debt. Klein had already been wondering how to stop and persuade him.

Is this what "Do as you wish, but do no harm" truly means?

The two waited at the roadside for less than a minute before a luxurious four-wheeled carriage raced toward them. Its side bore the same pale-blue emblem of a white dove with outstretched wings.

The carriage stopped. A middle-aged man in a black formal suit and matching bow tie climbed down. Looking at the wallet, he removed his hat and spoke politely.

"Gentlemen, I believe that belongs to my master."

"The emblem supports your claim, but I must conduct an additional verification for everyone's protection. How much money does the wallet contain?" Old Neil replied with equal courtesy.

The man hesitated, then immediately gave a self-deprecating smile.

"As a butler, I don't know how much cash my master carried. My apologies. Please allow me to ask him."

"Of course." Old Neil gestured toward the carriage.

The butler returned to its side and conversed through the window with the passenger within.

Then he approached Klein and Old Neil again.

"More than three hundred pounds but fewer than three hundred fifty. My master doesn't recall the exact sum."

He doesn't remember? He truly is obscenely wealthy. If I carried that much, I would count it again and again.

Klein was filled with envy.

Old Neil nodded and handed over the wallet.

"With the Goddess as witness, it belongs to you."

The butler accepted it, made a quick estimate of the contents, and withdrew three ten-pound notes.

"My master is Sir Deweyville. He wishes to honor your integrity. This is the reward an honest man deserves. Please don't refuse."

Sir Deweyville? The founder of the Deweyville Trust, which provides inexpensive housing for working-class families?

Klein immediately remembered the name. Benson respected the knight but considered his charitable rules detached from reality.

"Please convey our gratitude to Sir Deweyville. He is a kind and generous gentleman."

Old Neil accepted the three notes without false reluctance.

After watching Sir Deweyville's carriage depart and making certain no one stood nearby, he turned toward Klein, flicked the banknotes, and chuckled.

"Thirty pounds. The IOU is settled.

"I told you the debt would be resolved through reasonable means.

"This is the power of magic."

What kind of damned magical power is this? It actually worked?

Klein was dumbfounded yet again.

Several minutes later, as they climbed the stairs toward the security company, he asked in confusion, "Mr. Neil, why didn't you request more money?"

"Never be greedy. Restraint is an essential quality for every Mystery Pryer who wishes to live a long life," Old Neil explained happily.

...

In a vast ballroom, candles burned in several chandeliers, releasing a fragrance that soothed the mind. Their sheer number produced illumination equal to gaslights.

Long tables displayed fried foie gras, grilled steaks, roast chicken, fried tonguefish, Desi oysters, lamb stew, cream soup, and countless other delicacies. Bottles of Mist Champagne, Aurmir grape wine, and Southville red wine gleamed temptingly beneath the lights.

Servants in red vests moved among elegantly and lavishly dressed guests, carrying trays filled with crystal glasses.

Audrey Hall wore a pale-white gown with a high collar, high waist, and engageantes. A tightly fitted corset shaped her figure, while a cage crinoline supported the perfectly arranged volume of her skirts.

Her long blond hair had been gathered into an elegant bun. Earrings, necklace, and rings sparkled beneath the chandeliers. White dancing shoes embroidered with roses and diamonds covered her feet.

How many petticoats am I wearing? Five? Six?

Wearing white silk gloves, Audrey lightly touched her crinoline with her right hand. Her left held a glass of clear champagne.

Unlike her usual self, she made no attempt to occupy the center of the banquet or attract every eye. She avoided the bustle and stood quietly in the shadows of the drapes beside a French window.

She sipped her champagne and observed the crowd as though she didn't belong among them.

Earl Wolf's youngest son is speaking with Viscount Conrad's daughter. He moves his forearm whenever he wants to emphasize a point. The larger the gesture, the less believable the statement. That must be a habit developed through experience.

He constantly elevates himself by belittling others, yet he feels guilty about it. I can see that in both his words and body language.

Duchess Della has covered her mouth with her left hand every time she laughs tonight. Ah, I understand. She wants everyone to notice that flawless ocean-blue sapphire.

Her husband, Duke Negan, is discussing current affairs with several Conservative nobles. Since the banquet began, he has looked for Duchess Della only once.

They almost never meet each other's eyes. Perhaps they aren't nearly as affectionate as they pretend.

Baron Larry has made Madam Parnes laugh seven times. That alone is perfectly ordinary, but why does she glance at her husband with such guilt?

Now they're leaving in separate directions... No. Both paths lead to the garden.

Within the magnificent banquet, Audrey perceived countless details she had never noticed before.

For a moment, she almost believed she was watching an opera.

Everyone is an excellent actor...

She sighed inwardly, her eyes remaining clear and detached.

Then she sensed something and turned toward a dark corner of the broad balcony outside.

A large golden retriever sat silently in the shadows, half its body hidden by darkness, staring into the ballroom at Audrey.

Susie...

The corner of Audrey's mouth twitched, and her expression changed instantly.

She could no longer maintain the composure of a Spectator.

Chapter 53: Listener

An ancient three-masted sailing ship crossed a storm-torn sea.

It wasn't fast, nor did it displace much water. Against the apocalyptic sky and waves, the vessel looked like a dead leaf torn from its branch. Yet no matter how violently the gale raged or how high the waves rose, the ship continued forward in perfect peace, never once listing.

Alger Wilson stood alone on the open deck, gazing at breakers as massive as mountains. No one could have guessed what occupied his thoughts.

Monday is approaching again...

Monday belonged to the Earth Mother, marking the beginning of another cycle of waxing and waning.

For Alger, however, it possessed another meaning. It belonged to the mysterious existence forever shrouded in gray-white fog.

At least I haven't been reduced to a madman.

He stopped studying the storm and laughed quietly at himself.

One of his few sailors approached and bowed reverently.

"My lord, what course shall we set this time?"

Alger surveyed the raging sea and answered calmly, "We pursue the Listener of the Aurora Order."

...

The storm faded, giving way to spreading mist.

On a peculiar, cannon-equipped sailing ship, a boy of eight or nine with soft yellow hair looked fearfully at the pirates surrounding him.

The crew behaved without discipline. Some drank beer directly from barrels, others swung from ropes, several mocked one another, and two settled an argument with their fists.

The boy turned toward a black-robed man standing in the shadows. Lowering his voice, he asked, "Father, where are we going?"

He had first met this man—who claimed to be both his father and an adventurer—only five days ago.

If the oil portrait left by his dead mother hadn't confirmed the man's identity, and if the orphanage hadn't expelled him, the boy would never have left his hometown with his only living relative, who remained almost a stranger.

The man in the shadows lowered his head and looked at his son with an amiable expression.

"Jack, I'm taking you to a holy land—the sacred dwelling where the Creator once lived."

"The Kingdom of God? But mortals can enter only if He grants them grace..."

Jack's mother had educated him well. The prospect filled him with equal parts astonishment and fear.

The man's jawline was unforgettable, as though the finest sculptor had carved it from stone.

He raised one hand to his ear in a listening gesture and answered in a dreamlike voice.

"Jack, 'mortals' is an erroneous concept. The Creator made this world and exists everywhere, within every living thing. Thus, all beings possess divinity. When that divinity reaches a sufficient degree, they can become angels. The seven false gods worshiped today are merely powerful angels.

"Listen. I can hear the Creator's teachings now. Ah, what a magnificent revelation! Life is only a journey of the spirit. Once the spirit becomes sufficiently powerful and resilient, we can discover our own divinity and fuse it with still more divine nature..."

Jack couldn't understand the complicated explanation. He shook his head and asked something he had never before had the chance to raise.

"Father, Mother told me that after the Creator made the world, He divided Himself among all living things and ceased to exist as a separate being. If that's true, how can His sacred dwelling still exist?"

For a child of seven or eight, the question was remarkably logical.

The sculpted man froze. He turned away and seemed to listen to further whispers.

Without warning, he collapsed to his knees on the deck. Black fragments protruded from his exposed skin.

Clutching his head with both hands, his face twisted in agony.

"They are lying!" he screamed.

...

After lunch, Old Neil promised to take Klein along the next time he visited the underground market. Klein then returned slowly to the Blackthorn Security Company.

He considered two possibilities: remain in the civilian office to read documents and practice his abilities, or seize the opportunity to visit the Divination Club and act as a Seer before Captain Dunn found another task for him.

Before he could decide, Dunn Smith entered in his usual black trench coat and half top hat.

"Captain, is there any news?" Klein asked with concern, immediately thinking of the Antigonus family's notebook.

No fatigue showed in Dunn's gray eyes.

"The evidence confirms that Ray Bieber has the Antigonus notebook, but he has disappeared completely.

"I've notified the other Nighthawk teams by telegram and asked them to monitor ports and steam-locomotive stations. The first batch of printed portraits was mailed yesterday afternoon and will soon appear in the major newspapers."

How much easier this would be with telephones, fax machines, surveillance cameras, and large databases. What a pity. I know how to use all of them and even understand some of the underlying principles.

Klein sighed inwardly.

Dunn continued, "Regardless, we can regard the notebook as tentatively found, and the credit belongs to you. Naturally, one final confirmation is required. I've sent a telegram to the Backlund diocese asking them to escort Sealed Artifact 2-049 here. It once belonged to the Antigonus family and can help us determine whether Ray Bieber is one of their descendants."

A Grade 2 Sealed Artifact. Dangerous, but usable with care and restraint.

Curiosity made Klein want to ask about its abilities and hazards. Then he remembered that he lacked the necessary clearance and reluctantly abandoned the idea.

"May the Goddess bless us."

He touched four points on his chest, forming the crimson moon.

Dunn pushed open his office door and nodded slightly.

"The Goddess has always protected us. Klein, once the matter is verified, your contribution would have earned you full membership—if you hadn't already chosen Seer. You could have selected Sleepless, but unfortunately..."

He paused. "To be honest, your decision still puzzles me. Corpse Collector may be unpleasant, but you've met Daly and know how powerful a Spirit Medium can become. Mystery Pryer was also a sound choice. Old Neil could have served as your example and helped minimize the risk of losing control."

Klein had prepared an answer long ago but never had an opportunity to use it because Dunn hadn't asked directly. Now he could offer it in passing.

After organizing his words, he said, "I understood both Seer and Mystery Pryer to be support pathways that wouldn't require me to confront enemies constantly. Combat is far too dangerous.

"But you and Old Neil both warned me that curiosity and experimentation in mysticism often produce terrible consequences. Even the name 'Mystery Pryer' made me uneasy. You must remember that I was an ordinary university graduate only recently. Cowardice was the principal reason for my choice."

"I must admit, that's a far more reasonable answer than I expected." Dunn massaged his temples and chuckled.

Turning halfway, he examined Klein with his gray eyes.

"Continue going out for now. Don't restrict yourself to routes between Welch's residence and Iron Cross Street. You may sense the notebook again and help us confirm Ray Bieber's location."

"Understood."

Klein no longer needed to choose between his two plans.

He said goodbye and turned away, counting silently.

Three, two...

"Wait," Dunn called.

Klein turned back with a smile. "Yes, Captain?"

Dunn cleared his throat.

"Support Beyonders still have to fight occasionally. Although Seers may appear capable of avoiding battle, you cannot neglect the possibility. Maintain your shooting practice and continue improving your strength."

"That's exactly what I'm working toward." Klein pointed outside. "I'll be going now."

"Very good. Ah, wait another moment."

Dunn stopped him again and considered.

"Perhaps I should hire a combat instructor for you. Naturally, that assumes you become a full member."

Klein acknowledged the idea, then asked carefully, "Captain, is there anything else?"

"No." Seeing the disbelief in Klein's eyes, Dunn shook his head with a smile and emphasized, "Truly, nothing else."

Only then did Klein pass the partition. After saying goodbye to Rozanne and Mrs. Orianna, he went to the Shooting Club for practice.

When he finished, he continued to the Divination Club and found the attractive Angelica sitting behind the reception desk, leisurely reading a magazine.

Home...

Klein silently read the magazine's title, then approached with his cane.

"Good afternoon, Madam Angelica."

"Good afternoon, Mr. Moretti." Angelica calmly set down the magazine and stood. "Not long after you left yesterday, Mr. Glacis arrived. He has only just recovered from a serious illness."

Klein sighed in relief and smiled.

"That's certainly worth celebrating."

Angelica had been secretly watching his reaction. Now she lowered her voice and asked curiously, "Mr. Glacis said you're a very, very, very miraculous doctor. Is that true?"

What?

Klein stared at her, wondering whether he had misheard.

Why would Glacis think I'm a doctor?

Even I didn't know that...

Chapter 54: The First Divination Client

Seeing Klein's peculiar expression, Angelica immediately began doubting what she had heard.

"Isn't it true? Mr. Glacis said you identified an illness in his lungs merely by looking at him..."

Her voice softened until she trailed off entirely.

Looking at him? The darkened glabella?

Understanding dawned on Klein. He shook his head and chuckled.

"I believe Mr. Glacis misunderstood."

He initially intended to dismiss the matter with that. Then he remembered that not a single person had requested his services during the entire previous afternoon. Since the lack of clients interfered with his plan to act as a Seer, his mind began working rapidly.

"It was actually a form of divination," he explained.

"Divination? But Mr. Glacis said you only studied his face. Does that count as divination?" Angelica asked, both doubtful and astonished.

Klein smiled calmly.

"As an employee of the Divination Club, you're familiar with palmistry, aren't you?"

Palm reading was hardly unique to the Foodaholic Empire. Even on Earth, India and ancient Europe had developed similar principles—to say nothing of a world where Beyonder powers genuinely existed.

"Of course, but you didn't examine his hand. Were you secretly observing it?" Angelica asked curiously.

"I practice physiognomy—reading the face," Klein improvised. "Its fundamental principles are similar to those of palmistry."

"Truly?" Angelica remained visibly skeptical.

For the sake of establishing his career as a Seer, Klein chuckled and pretended to think, using the movement to tap his glabella twice.

He focused, and Angelica's aura emerged. Purple surrounded her head, red her limbs, blue her throat... Her health appeared normal. Several areas were slightly dim, but only from ordinary fatigue.

Her emotional colors consisted of orange mixed with red and blue, representing warmth combined with excitement and contemplation.

Fortunately, nothing is physically wrong.

Klein was about to deactivate Spirit Vision when he noticed dense darkness concealed deep within her emotional colors.

And there's very little white—the desire to improve or move forward.

He nodded thoughtfully.

"Mr. Moretti, are you reading my face?"

The young gentleman in black had abruptly fallen silent and begun studying her with grave attention. Angelica immediately noticed, her question carrying equal parts curiosity and unease.

Klein didn't answer at once. Instead, he tapped his glabella as though contemplating his findings.

Just as Angelica's discomfort became difficult to bear, he said gently, "Madam Angelica, some pain and sorrow shouldn't be sealed within the heart."

Her eyes widened, and her lips parted, but no words emerged.

She stared at Klein in his half top hat, his scholarly air unmistakable, while he continued in a deep, warm, reassuring voice.

"Climb a mountain, play a vigorous game of tennis, or attend a tragedy at the theater. Exhaust your body through activity. Let your tears flow freely. Cry and scream if you need to. Give those emotions a way out.

"It will be good for your health."

Angelica became as still as a statue.

She blinked repeatedly, then lowered her head in confusion.

"Thank you for the advice," she said softly.

"It seems the club is quite busy today."

Klein didn't press the matter. As though no divination had occurred, he turned toward the meeting room at the end of the corridor.

"Sunday afternoon... at least fifty members..."

Angelica's voice was initially hoarse, and she managed only the essential words.

After a pause, her pace and tone gradually returned to normal.

"Would you like tea or coffee?"

"Sibe black tea, please."

Klein nodded, removed his hat politely, and walked slowly toward the meeting room.

Only after he disappeared through the doorway did Angelica release a long breath.

The Divination Club's meeting room was nearly twice the size of Klein's classroom in high school.

With only five or six members present on ordinary days, the space looked enormous and empty. Now dozens of diviners occupied most of the room.

Sunlight entered through several oriel windows. Members held quiet discussions, asked Hanass Vincent questions, practiced various divination methods, or sat alone drinking coffee and reading newspapers.

The scene reminded Klein of his school days on Earth. The difference was that his old classroom had been far noisier, without the meeting room's peaceful atmosphere.

He looked around but saw neither Glacis nor Edward Steve. Selecting a divination textbook at random, he found a corner and began leafing through it leisurely.

Angelica soon arrived with his tea and placed it on the table.

As she quietly turned to leave, she saw Mr. Moretti draw an exquisitely crafted silver chain from his left sleeve. A clear piece of topaz hung from its end.

What is he doing?

Angelica unconsciously slowed and watched him.

Holding the chain in his left hand, Klein suspended the topaz above the Sibe black tea, stopping just short of the surface.

His expression became serene. As his eyes half closed, the atmosphere around him abruptly fell still.

The topaz began to move, carrying the unusual silver chain in a clockwise circle.

Watching, Angelica found Mr. Moretti profoundly mysterious.

Klein opened his eyes and smiled.

"The black tea you serve is excellent."

He had deliberately performed the entire display for Angelica.

If he wanted clients to select him, her recommendation would be crucial.

Since his goal was to act as a Seer, Klein set aside his reservations and fully embraced the role.

"Yes. Mr. Vannas is extremely particular about the quality of our tea," Angelica answered distractedly.

Klein carefully wound and put away the spirit pendulum. Then he raised the floral porcelain cup and gestured to her with a polite smile.

Angelica returned to the reception hall, but she could no longer concentrate on her magazine. She sat staring into the distance, lost in thought.

She remained that way until someone knocked on the door.

Startled back to awareness, she looked toward the entrance and saw a woman in a pale-blue dress.

The woman removed a veiled hat decorated with a powder-blue ribbon. She appeared composed but melancholy.

"Good afternoon, madam. Would you like to join the Divination Club, or are you seeking a reading?" Angelica asked automatically.

"A reading."

Sorrow hid in the woman's beautiful eyes, and she bit her lower lip as she spoke.

Angelica guided her to a sofa and carefully explained how the club operated. Then she offered her the album.

"You may choose any diviner listed here."

The troubled woman examined the pages seriously. With so many members present that day, the number of available choices soon became overwhelming.

"Could you recommend someone from these pages?" She indicated the middle portion, excluding both the diviners charging more than two soli and those asking fewer than four pence.

Angelica accepted the album and considered for several minutes.

"I recommend this gentleman."

The melancholy woman looked down and saw the name Klein Moretti.

"Mr. Moretti only recently joined. Is he reliable?" she asked uneasily.

Angelica nodded with complete certainty.

"Another club member and I both believe Mr. Moretti is an outstanding diviner. If he weren't new, his fee would be considerably higher."

"I understand. Then I'll choose Mr. Moretti."

"Very well. Please wait a moment."

Carrying the album, Angelica walked to the meeting room.

She approached Klein and spoke quietly.

"Mr. Moretti, a client has requested you. Which room would you like?"

That was effective. My first client has arrived.

Klein put down his teacup and answered with calm dignity.

"The Topaz Room."

"Certainly."

Angelica led the way, opened the wooden door to the Topaz Room, and left Klein to prepare.

He sat behind the table covered with divination tools. Less than a minute later, the woman in the pale-blue dress entered, her gaze lowered and her expression melancholy.

While she turned to close the door, Klein tapped his glabella twice.

The yellow aura around her stomach is slightly dim. Her emotional colors contain a great deal of darkness, primarily worry and anxiety.

Klein examined her carefully, leaned back, and raised one hand to deactivate Spirit Vision.

"Good afternoon, Mr. Moretti." The woman sat opposite him.

"Good afternoon. How may I address you?" Klein asked politely, without seriously expecting a genuine name.

From his experience online, he knew that many people preferred anonymity when seeking private advice.

"You may call me Anna." Setting aside her veiled hat, she looked at Klein with a mixture of doubt and hope.

"I want to learn what has happened to my fiancé. He traveled to the Southern Continent in March for business. On the third of last month, he sent a telegram to his family and me saying that he was about to sail home. Twenty days passed, and he didn't arrive.

"At first, I assumed weather in the Berserk Sea had delayed him. But more than a month has passed now, and his ship, the Alfalfa, still hasn't reached Enmat Harbor."

The ocean separating the Northern and Southern Continents was called the Berserk Sea, notorious for natural disasters and countless dangerous currents. Before Emperor Roselle sponsored expeditions that discovered several comparatively safe routes, the northern nations had been unable to begin their age of colonization—much less lay an undersea cable for transoceanic telegraphy.

Looking at the first client of his career as a Seer, Klein asked carefully, "Which form of divination would you prefer?"

Chapter 55: Revelation

Anna hesitated for more than ten seconds, uncertainty flickering in her beautiful eyes.

"You may choose whichever method you believe is most accurate. You're the fortune-teller, after all, not me. I've tried studying cards at home, including tarot, but they always felt more like toys than anything serious."

Klein considered her words. Resting his wrists against the edge of the table, he steepled his fingers before his face and said calmly, "Then we'll use an astrolabe."

He gestured toward the fountain pen and stack of white paper on the table.

"Write down your fiance's name, address, date of birth, and a description of his appearance. If you happen to know the exact time of his birth, include that as well."

Judging by Anna's clothing, makeup, and manner, Klein had no reason to doubt that she could read and write.

Without replying, she took a sheet of paper, dipped the pen into the ink, and began. She paused now and then to recall a detail.

Two minutes later, she pushed the paper across the table.

Klein turned it around and read:

Joyce Meyer. September 15, 1323, 2:00 p.m. Tingen City, East Borough, 8 Stevens Street. Short blond hair, aquiline nose...

After only a glance, he began calculating the man's spiritual numbers.

One plus five equals six.

In spirit numerology, adding together the digits of the day on which a person was born produced their Birth Day Path Number, which governed their life before the age of twenty-seven. The Birth Month Path Number influenced the years from twenty-seven to fifty-four, while the Birth Year Path Number governed everything thereafter.

It was July of 1349, and Joyce had not yet turned twenty-seven, so Klein began with the Birth Day Path Number.

Six represented balance and harmony, a generous nature, and a fortunate marriage or engagement.

Next, he calculated Joyce's Year Path Number.

To find it, one replaced the birth year with the current year, reduced its digits, then added the result to the subject's Birth Month and Birth Day Path Numbers. The final number offered a general picture of the person's fortune for that year.

One plus three plus four plus nine equals seventeen. One plus seven equals eight. Eight, plus nine for his birth month, plus six for his birth day, equals twenty-three. Two plus three equals five.

His Year Path Number is five. Change, upheaval, and the need to take risks...

After comparing that result with everything Anna had told him, Klein felt reasonably certain that her account was accurate.

He looked up from the paper. "Mr. Meyer began his return journey on June third?"

"If he didn't lie to me, then yes." Anna bit her lip.

"All right." Klein made a note beside the other information.

Then he met her gaze and said gently, "I'll begin constructing the astrolabe. It will take some time, and I need complete silence. Would you mind waiting outside? Angelica can bring you tea or coffee."

"Of course."

Anna knew that fortune-tellers often had their peculiarities. Without appearing surprised, she rose, picked up her hat with its pale-blue ribbon, and left the Topaz Room.

Klein locked the door behind her and returned to the table. Using the information she had provided, he drew the astrolabe, marking the relevant houses along with the positions of the corresponding planets and stars.

He completed the entire process from memory without once opening the Astromancy Manual.

Over the past few days of studying mysticism, Klein had discovered that he could understand and retain almost anything related to divination with astonishing ease. After only a little practice, the knowledge seemed to settle into his instincts.

Perhaps this is one of a Seer's abilities...

When the astrolabe was complete, a quiet sense of satisfaction filled him. His body relaxed, followed by his mind and spirit.

He traced the relationships among the houses, planets, and supporting signs. His preliminary interpretation was clear: Joyce Meyer had encountered a serious accident, but he would ultimately survive.

Strictly speaking, the divination was finished.

But this was Klein's first paid consultation, and he wanted to treat it with particular care. If he could establish a reputation here, it might help him secure future commissions.

He picked up the pen and wrote a sentence in Hermes:

Joyce Meyer's current situation.

He silently repeated the sentence while memorizing every detail Anna had written. After reciting it seven times, he took the sheet of paper in hand and leaned back in his chair.

He pictured the sphere of light. His eyes darkened as he swiftly entered Cogitation.

The room grew distant and unreal. Above him spread an intangible, boundless fog.

Holding the words on the paper in his mind, Klein relaxed and allowed himself to sink into sleep.

He was performing a dream divination.

By repeatedly contemplating a question, fixing the relevant details in his memory, and letting his Astral Projection wander through the spirit world during sleep, he could receive revelations in the form of dreams.

Ordinary people sometimes experienced something similar, but their dreams tended to be confused and symbolic, and they often forgot them upon waking. A Seer did not suffer from the same limitations. The images came to him directly and remained clear.

The world blurred as Klein slipped into the borderland between sleep and wakefulness.

Within the distorted dreamscape, he saw a young blond man with an aquiline nose. The man thrashed desperately through a sea of blood, nearly disappearing beneath the waves again and again. In the end, however, he escaped and dragged himself onto shore.

The scene shattered.

In its place stood a blue house with a toy windmill beside the door. The blond young man approached it slowly and stepped inside, his face bright with relief.

Then the dream changed once more.

Klein found himself standing inside a vast and magnificent palace.

Its walls had crumbled beyond repair, with moss and weeds spreading through the ruins. Through the gaping holes, he could see a mountain peak outside, wrapped in clouds.

At the far end of the palace stood an enormous throne carved from stone. Tarnished gold and dim gemstones adorned its surface, and its proportions made it clear that it had never been intended for a human being.

The throne was empty, mottled by the passage of countless years.

Klein looked around, bewildered. He couldn't understand why his dream had brought him here.

His muddled thoughts sharpened. Acting on instinct, he started toward the ruined palace's exit, hoping to discover where he was.

Then he felt it.

Someone was watching him from behind.

Klein whirled around and stared at the immense stone throne.

Countless translucent maggots writhed upon it, slowly twisting and growing.

Klein sucked in a breath.

His eyes flew open.

The crystal balls, tarot cards, and completed astrolabe filled his vision as reality rapidly displaced the nightmare.

The first part was clearly the result of my divination. But what was that final scene? It felt as though it was directed at me...

Klein set down the paper and rubbed his temples as he considered what he had seen.

He could rule out the possibility that it had been a projection of Anna's fears. He had performed the divination himself.

A palace built for something other than humans, standing atop a mountain... That silent gaze... Those twisted, unnatural maggots...

He sifted through the possibilities.

Did the luck-enhancement ritual connect me to that existence? Or is this related to the Antigonus family's notebook?

Then he remembered a crucial detail.

The notebook mentioned the Nation of the Evernight in the Hornacis mountain range. And the palace in my dream stood on a mountain peak!

As he reached that tentative conclusion, Klein felt a surge of relief that he had chosen the Seer pathway. According to Old Neil, Mystery Pryers could also use dream divination, but their results were not nearly as effective.

It really has no intention of letting me go... All I can do is hope Ray Bieber is captured soon.

After composing himself, Klein picked up the astrolabe and Anna's information, then walked to the door.

When he returned to the reception area, he found Anna standing by the window. Her cup of black tea sat untouched.

"Mr. Moretti!" She caught sight of him and hurried to her feet. "Do you have an answer?"

Rather than reply immediately, Klein asked a question based on the revelation in his dream.

"Is there a toy windmill outside either your home or Mr. Meyer's?"

Anna's eyes widened. For several seconds, she was too shocked to speak.

At last, she stammered, "It was a gift from him. I keep it beside my front door. How could you possibly know that?"

That can be divined?

Klein smiled and softened his voice.

"Congratulations, Miss Anna. Mr. Joyce Meyer is at your home right now. If you leave at once, you should still find him there. He has survived a terrible ordeal and an unimaginably painful journey. What he needs from you now isn't questions, but comfort and a warm embrace."

"Truly?" Anna stared at him in disbelief.

None of the fortune-tellers she had consulted before would have dared speak with such certainty or offer so definite a conclusion.

"You'll know as soon as you return home," Klein replied with a reassuring smile.

"Oh, Lord of Steam... Can it really be true? My poor Joyce has returned? Are you certain? No, I can't believe it..."

For a moment, Anna stood frozen, babbling incoherently. Then she pulled a one-soli note from her purse and placed it on the counter. Without waiting for her change, she rushed out of the Divination Club and hailed a carriage home.

"I suppose this includes the tip," Klein murmured, looking at the banknote with an amused shake of his head.

The two-wheeled carriage sped through the streets toward East Borough.

Anna watched the buildings sweep past, torn between hope, fear, and restless anticipation. Before long, the familiar toy windmill appeared in the distance.

She jumped down from the carriage with no thought for appearances, stumbled toward the house, and rang the bell.

The door creaked open.

A blond young man stood on the other side, dressed in a black formal suit. He looked exhausted and haggard, but joy shone in his eyes above his aquiline nose.

"I was afraid I'd miss you today," Joyce said with a smile.

"Oh, Exalted Steam... You really are back!"

Anna rubbed her eyes, then cried out in delight.

Everything the fortune-teller said was true!

No. He wasn't merely a fortune-teller. He was a genuine Seer!

It was incredible!

The thoughts tumbled through her mind as tears filled her eyes. She threw herself into her fiance's arms and held him tightly.

They embraced in silence outside the gray-blue house. Beside them, the toy windmill turned slowly in the breeze, as though carrying all their hardships far away.

Chapter 56: Escape from the Sea

In the spacious living room, Anna and Joyce sat on separate sofas, with Anna's parents between them.

Joyce let out a long breath, his face filled with relief.

"Praise the Exalted Steam. I'm incredibly fortunate to have returned alive... and to be able to see Anna again."

"My poor Joyce, what happened to you?" Anna could no longer contain her concern.

Joyce glanced at his fiancee, and his expression grew grave.

"Even now, I'm still terrified. I wake from nightmares night after night. Five days after the Alfalfa departed Caesar Pier, we encountered pirates--terrifying pirates. The only stroke of luck was that their leader was Nast."

"The pirate who calls himself the King of the Five Seas?" Anna's father, Mr. Wayne, asked in astonishment.

Joyce had already been there for half an hour, but until now, he had volunteered nothing about his ordeal. He had appeared frightened and restless, as though part of him were still trapped aboard the ship. Only after Anna returned and embraced him did he finally seem able to leave the experience behind.

"Yes. Nast claims to be a descendant of the Solomon Empire, and the King of the Five Seas doesn't believe in killing captives. They robbed us, but spared our lives. His men even left us enough food to continue our voyage," Joyce said, his voice unsteady at the memory.

His body began to tremble, but he forced himself to continue recounting his darkest nightmare.

"I hadn't lost much money, so I thought the worst was over. But as we sailed on, a bitter conflict erupted among the Alfalfa's passengers and crew. Arguments became fistfights. Then people drew revolvers and raised swords against one another...

"After that, all I saw was blood. One person after another fell beside me, their eyes staring open in death. Severed limbs, hearts, and intestines littered the deck.

"Those of us who refused to become savage beasts--those who still had our reason--had nowhere to hide and no means of escape. We were surrounded by the boundless ocean and its deep-blue waves. Some people screamed. Some begged for mercy. Others offered their own bodies to survive. It made no difference. Their heads still ended up hanging from the mast.

"Anna, I was consumed by despair. I thought I would never see you again. But even in that nightmare, there was still a hero. The captain led us into hiding inside the ship's reinforced keel, where we survived on the food and water stored there until the madmen began to exhaust themselves. Then Mr. Tris rallied us and bravely led the attack against those murderers...

"We survived the battle, but I'll never forget it. By then, the Alfalfa had drifted far off course, and only a third of her original sailors remained."

As Joyce described the darkest and most horrifying side of human nature, he couldn't help recalling their hero--the young man who called himself Tris.

Tris had a round, amiable face. He was almost as shy as a girl and preferred keeping to himself in a corner. Only those who knew him well realized what an engaging conversationalist he could be.

Yet when their situation had been at its most desperate, that unremarkable young man had stood resolutely before them all.

"Oh, Exalted Steam, my poor Joyce... You endured something truly dreadful. Thank God. Praise be to Him for sparing us from an eternal separation."

Tears welled in Anna's eyes as she repeatedly traced three points in the shape of a triangle--the Sacred Emblem of Steam and Machinery.

Joyce gave her a faint, pale smile.

"This is the reward for our faith. Afterward, the Alfalfa passed through several storms and lost her course. We overcame one trial after another before finally reaching Enmat Harbor.

"Because of the bloodshed aboard the ship, the police detained every survivor and questioned us separately. None of us were allowed to send telegrams to our families. When they released us this morning, I borrowed money from a friend and took the steam train back at once. Thank God I was able to set foot in Tingen again and see all of you."

He looked at his fiancee, puzzled.

"Anna, when you saw me, I could tell you were delighted and surprised. But I don't understand why you ran toward the door so excitedly the instant you stepped out of the carriage." He chuckled. "I had hoped to give you an enormous surprise."

Remembering what had happened earlier, Anna still found it difficult to believe.

"There's no reason to hide it from you, Joyce. I was so worried that I visited Tingen's only divination club today. The fortune-teller--no, the Seer--told me that you had returned and were waiting inside the house with the windmill."

"What?" Joyce and Anna's parents exclaimed in unison.

Anna covered her face and shook her head.

"I can scarcely believe it myself, but that's exactly what happened. Exalted Steam... perhaps miracles truly do exist in this world.

"Joyce, the Seer asked for your name, appearance, address, and date of birth. He said he would perform an astrolabe divination. Afterward, he asked whether the house with the toy windmill belonged to you or to me. When I told him it was mine, he said, 'Congratulations, Miss Anna. Mr. Joyce Meyer is currently a guest at your home. What he needs now isn't questions, but comfort and a warm embrace.'"

"God..." Joyce stared at her, unable to comprehend what he had heard. "Does he know me? Did someone send him a telegram? Perhaps he's acquainted with the police in Enmat Harbor? No, even that wouldn't explain it. How could he have known I came here? How could he have known you would seek a divination? Had you made an appointment?"

"No. I only decided to go at the last moment," Anna replied, looking dazed.

"Perhaps a skilled Seer must gather enormous quantities of information, even when none of it appears immediately useful. Perhaps that's the true wonder of divination," Mr. Wayne said with a sigh. "Divination has existed throughout more than a thousand years of recorded history, and even during the uncertain Fourth Epoch. It has never disappeared. There must be a reason for that."

Joyce slowly shook his head. "What was this Seer's name?"

Anna thought for a moment. "Klein Moretti."

...

Back in the reception hall of the Divination Club, Angelica had kept her distance while Klein spoke quietly with Anna. Consequently, all she had seen was Anna leaving in a daze, shock and confusion written across her face.

Angelica walked briskly toward the sofa and asked curiously, "Was it a favorable result?"

She didn't dare ask what the actual result had been, afraid of violating one of the profession's unspoken rules.

"Yes." Klein nodded and took three copper coins from his pocket. "One-eighth of a soli is one and a half pence, correct?"

"That's right." Angelica looked at the coins and saw one penny and two halfpence. She immediately held one of the smaller coins out to him. "You've given me an extra halfpenny."

Klein smiled. "Thank you for looking after my client. She gave me a tip, so it's only fair that I give you one as well."

And it's also thanks for recommending me to her, he added inwardly.

"All right."

Angelica felt an inexplicable trace of fear toward Klein, but his reason was perfectly appropriate, so she accepted the money.

Certain that more clients would soon request his services, Klein returned to the meeting room.

However, by twenty minutes to six, not a single additional customer had come to him.

It wasn't that the Divination Club lacked business. Most visitors simply arrived with a particular fortune-teller already in mind.

They were probably referred by friends and decided who to consult before they ever arrived... In other words, my reputation is still too low.

Klein quietly mocked himself for thinking in terms borrowed from a game.

After finishing his third cup of Sibe black tea, he picked up his top hat and silver-inlaid cane and strolled out of the meeting room.

Angelica suddenly remembered Glacis's request and hurried to intercept him.

"Mr. Moretti, when will you be visiting the club again? Mr. Glacis would like to thank you in person."

"I'll return whenever I have the time. If fate permits, he and I will certainly meet," Klein replied in the mysterious tones of a stage charlatan, fully inhabiting his role.

Before Angelica could respond, he left the club and took a public carriage home.

When Klein stepped through the front door, he found Benson reading the newspaper while Melissa assembled an assortment of gears, bearings, and springs in the evening sunlight.

"Good afternoon. Did Mrs. Shaud visit?" he asked casually.

Benson raised his head without lowering the newspaper.

"Mrs. Shaud stayed for fifteen minutes. She brought us gifts and was delighted with the muffins and lemon cake we prepared. She invited us to visit whenever we have the opportunity. She's a friendly, well-mannered lady and an excellent conversationalist."

"The only problem is that her family believes in the Lord of Storms," Melissa complained. "They think girls shouldn't attend school and ought to be educated at home instead."

She was clearly offended by the idea.

"Don't worry about that. As long as she doesn't interfere with our lives, she can still be a good neighbor," Klein said, smiling reassuringly at his sister.

The Loen Kingdom was home to several religions. It was unlike the Feysac Empire in the north, where only the God of Combat was worshiped, or the Feynapotter Kingdom in the south, which venerated only Earth Mother.

Conflicts in custom and doctrine among the three principal churches--those of the Lord of Storms, the Evernight Goddess, and the God of Steam and Machinery--were unavoidable. Yet after a thousand years of mutual restraint, they had found a way to coexist.

"All right." Melissa pursed her lips and turned her attention back to the pile of mechanical parts.

After dinner, Klein resumed reviewing history. Only once Melissa and Benson had bathed and retired to their rooms did he wash up, enter his bedroom, and lock the door behind him.

He needed to organize and summarize everything he had learned, as well as the problems he had encountered. Otherwise, he might forget or overlook some critical point. Only by maintaining a clear grasp of the situation could he respond properly to whatever came next.

Klein opened his notebook, uncapped his pen, and began writing in Mandarin.

"Why is acting the key to digesting a potion?"

Chapter 57: Organizing and Summarizing

After a brief pause, Klein continued writing.

"The solution to the problem posed by potions lies in digestion, not mere control. The distinction is easy to understand.

"Simply controlling a potion is like treating its power as an external tool. No matter how thoroughly a wild beast is tamed, it never truly becomes part of its master, and the danger of it turning on him always remains. Digestion, on the other hand, means treating the potion one has consumed as part of oneself--breaking it down, merging with it, absorbing it, and incorporating it into a unified whole.

"I am now reasonably certain of this. The more important question is how 'acting' aids the process of digestion.

"Based on today's experience as a Seer, I can propose two hypotheses to be tested in the future.

"First: acting in accordance with the potion's name changes the state of one's body, mind, and spirit, bringing them closer to the stubborn remnant psyche within the potion's core. This creates a resonance that allows the psyche to be gradually assimilated and absorbed.

"Second: the remnant psyche inside the potion might resemble a computer system protected by a complete set of defenses. To infiltrate and dismantle it, one would need to discover a flaw, vulnerability, or key. The potion's name provides the necessary clue. Through acting, we disguise our body, mind, and spirit as part of the system, deceiving its defenses. This resembles Emperor Roselle's explanation.

"Whichever hypothesis proves correct, body, mind, and spirit must be involved, because they form the only bridge between acting and the potion's power."

Klein set down his pen and studied the passage. For a moment, he almost felt grateful for the education he had received in the Foodaholic Empire.

Whether one pursued science or engineering at university, the curriculum at least taught the fundamentals of logical reasoning. Without that training, he could never have become a proper keyboard warrior, much less analyzed his present circumstances.

Acting seems to work, but I'll need more time to determine exactly how effective it is, Klein concluded.

He moved on to his second question.

"Why does a Seer, despite possessing broader and more specialized knowledge of mysticism, lack effective means of direct combat? Shouldn't superior knowledge and expertise make a Seer more powerful by allowing them to discover ways to defeat their enemies?

"Possible explanations:

"First: as in the web novels I once read, I may have transmigrated into a game world that became reality. Different 'classes' therefore possess different specialties and must remain balanced against one another. So far, however, I have seen no evidence that this world is a game, nor have events unfolded like assigned quests. I will retain the possibility, but it seems highly unlikely.

"Second: balance is a fundamental law of this world. The Creator fashioned existence around that central principle.

"Third: potions of the same Sequence possess roughly equivalent levels of power. This represents the optimal balance discovered and refined by our predecessors. A potion exceeding that limit would greatly increase the risk of collapse and loss of control, while one beneath it would fail to bestow the intended Beyonder abilities. Given a fixed total amount of power, greater strength in one field must naturally be offset by weakness in another.

"Fourth: everything in this world originated from a single source and was formed from the Creator's remains. Thus, all things are fragments of the Creator. Since those fragments must complement one another, every individual fragment necessarily possesses inherent deficiencies.

"I favor the third and fourth explanations. The fourth, however, rests upon an unverified creation myth and can serve only as a general reference.

"Therefore, I will use the third explanation as my working theory and attempt to verify it through my current knowledge and future study."

Klein had already filled two pages, but he did not stop. Instead, he wrote down another question.

"Based on what I learned today, my luck-enhancement ritual belongs to the category of classical ritualistic magic.

"Ritualistic magic of this kind generally contains three parts. First, an offering is used to attract the interest of a corresponding existence. Second, an incantation precisely identifies and describes that existence. Third, a simple format and set of symbols communicates the ritualist's request.

"When I analyze the luck-enhancement ritual according to that standard, one problem becomes obvious.

"There is no third part!

"The placement of staple foods and the four counterclockwise steps around a square constitute the offering. The incantation also clearly identifies its target with phrases such as 'The Immortal Lord of Heaven and Earth for Blessings.'

"But after completing those steps, all I did was close my eyes and wait. Nothing in the ritual expressed any desire to improve my luck.

"In other words, the corresponding existence had no way of knowing what the so-called luck-enhancement ritual was meant to request. It could only act as it pleased...

"Act as it pleased...

"What a scam! Wasn't that damned Quintessential Divination and Arcane Arts of the Qin and Han Dynasty nothing but a colossal scam?

"I must have had rocks in my head when I decided to try it..."

Klein stopped and took two deep breaths, struggling to calm himself.

After slowly exhaling, he resumed writing.

"I could redesign the ritual to make it complete. Its stated objective would be to return me to Earth--to the world where my parents and friends live.

"That raises another question: did the entity truly act on a whim, or was there some deeper purpose?

"Furthermore, is the existence identified by the incantation in this world the same one the words referred to on Earth?

"If it is, the difference between the first and second rituals could be explained by the entity acting however it wished. Yet the second and third attempts produced essentially identical results: I appeared above the gray fog and was able to connect with Justice and The Hanged Man. Why were those results so consistent?

"If tomorrow afternoon's fourth attempt produces the same stable outcome, it will demonstrate a fixed pattern. That may mean the unknown entity has an agenda I do not understand. If so, adding new descriptions and requests probably won't produce a clear response. It may instead complicate the ritual and cause dangerous side effects.

"Assuming the same entity answered every time, could the difference between the first ritual and the later ones be caused by my performing them in different worlds? It might be similar to accessing the same system through different interfaces...

"Then how can I design a ritual that will produce the result I want?

"If the first ritual and the later ones reached different entities, several questions become much easier to answer. But the consistent results of the second and third attempts would still suggest that the entity I am invoking has a specific agenda--one I currently have no means of changing.

"The most important question is the identity of the existence targeted by the ritual. Where is He, and why has He offered neither clues nor guidance?

"Could He be somewhere deep within the world of fog?

"Could I regard Him as a dormant entity--one that produces a fixed response when subjected to a particular stimulus, but otherwise does not interfere with my actions?

"If so, I could introduce different rituals as stimuli and determine whether the feedback follows a consistent pattern. From that, I might eventually discover the correct method of returning home.

"The danger is that He may not be asleep. If that's the case, such experiments could provoke terrifying consequences.

"My first test must be conducted with extreme caution. The design must not anger that being...

"What a headache. I need more knowledge."

Klein sighed and brought that section to a close.

Finally, he recorded several miscellaneous observations.

"Formless voices repeatedly echo in my ears, crying, 'Hornacis and...' Was the second word Frygrea? Feygrea?

"The Hornacis mountain range forms the border between the Loen Kingdom and the Intis Republic. Its highest peak rises six thousand meters above sea level.

"According to the Antigonus family's diary, a Nation of the Evernight existed during the Fourth Epoch. Was that nation connected to the Evernight Goddess? If so, what was their relationship? Were they allies or enemies? Did the Church of the Evernight Goddess destroy the Antigonus family because of the Nation of the Evernight?

"Were the murmurs I heard coming from the diary--the cries of the Antigonus family echoing across one or two thousand years?

"And what does Frygrea--no, Flegrea--mean?

"Another interesting question: the fact that the Antigonus family left behind both this diary and Sealed Artifact 2-049 implies that they possessed a relatively powerful Beyonder pathway. Which Sequence did it correspond to? Did they possess the complete pathway or only part of it?

"My discovery that Ray Bieber possesses the diary involved a degree of coincidence. Although I have found no obvious evidence of manipulation, could my fate truly be entangled with that diary?"

Line after line filled the paper as Klein recorded the events he had experienced and every theory he could devise to explain them.

By the time he finished, he had covered both sides of four sheets.

Rip!

Klein abruptly tore the pages from his notebook. He read through them from beginning to end, marking certain passages and adding sentences to others.

Time passed quickly. Dark clouds temporarily veiled the crimson moon. Klein picked up the pocket watch on his desk, snapped it open, and checked the hour.

He set it down, removed a box of matches from the drawer, and struck one. The tiny flame touched the edges of his notes.

Orange fire caught and spread rapidly across the paper.

Klein held the burning sheets over the wooden wastebasket and watched flakes of ash drift downward. Then he released them. Within ten seconds, the notes had vanished, leaving only curling ash and black scorch marks at the bottom of the bin.

Because Emperor Roselle's secret diary existed in this world, Klein dared not leave behind evidence that he could write Chinese. If Old Neil or any of the other Nighthawks discovered those four pages, he would have no way to explain them.

At the same time, whenever he considered sensitive matters, he feared that the mysterious existence observing his dreams might be able to see and decipher his writing regardless of whether he used Loenese, ancient Feysac, or Hermes. Chinese was therefore useful only as a temporary means of organizing his thoughts. Once he finished, he had to destroy every trace.

And because he couldn't preserve his notes, he established a rule for himself: he would repeat this process once a week to ensure that he forgot nothing important.

As the last ashes settled, Klein took out a fresh sheet of white paper and wrote a heading:

"To my respected mentor,"

He intended to write to Senior Associate Professor Quentin Cohen and ask whether he possessed any historical information concerning the highest peak of the Hornacis mountain range.

Chapter 58: A Train of Thought

The following morning was Monday.

Since it was his day off, Klein remained at home. He gave Melissa the letter addressed to his mentor, Cohen Quentin, along with more than enough money for postage, and asked her to mail it at the post office near Tingen Technical School.

After breakfast, he went back to bed to make up for the sleep he had lost to the previous night's "work." It was nearly noon when the protests of his empty stomach finally woke him.

He reheated some leftovers from dinner and ate them with a loaf of rye bread. Then, carrying a newspaper, Klein entered the second-floor bathroom.

Whenever he did so, he couldn't help lamenting the absence of smartphones in this world.

Seven or eight minutes later, he emerged feeling refreshed, washed his hands, and returned to his bedroom, locking the door behind him.

Klein drew the curtains, lit the gas lamp, and spent half an hour in Cogitation. He devoted another half hour to practicing Spiritual Vision, spirit dowsing, and pendulum divination, followed by an hour mentally reviewing the mysticism knowledge he had recently acquired.

Afterward, he tore up the old newspaper and crumpled the scraps into several balls. On them, he wrote labels such as "Moon Flower Candle" and "Full Moon Essence Oil." Using the paper balls as substitutes for ingredients, he rehearsed every prescribed step of ritualistic magic in his mind.

He had no intention of attempting a genuine ritual until every detail had become second nature. Not only would failure waste valuable materials, but an improperly performed ritual could easily attract danger.

He repeated the process again and again. Eventually, he picked up his silver pocket watch with its vine-leaf pattern and checked the time.

It was a quarter to three.

After a moment's consideration, he carried the scraps of newspaper downstairs and burned them in the kitchen. Then he composed himself, preparing for the afternoon's Tarot Gathering.

Back in his bedroom, Klein locked the door once more. Rather than wait for the clock to strike three, he intended to enter the realm above the gray fog early.

He wanted to use the opportunity to explore that mysterious place thoroughly.

Standing in an open area of the room, Klein prepared to take his four counterclockwise steps. Then a concern suddenly occurred to him.

What if Justice or The Hanged Man hadn't yet found a suitable place for the Gathering?

Could they be interrupted--or even discovered?

He had previously told them that if some unexpected circumstance prevented them from being alone, they could request a leave of absence in advance.

In the past, that would have posed an almost insoluble problem. He could hardly build an entire server-based internet by himself in another world. Any technology more advanced than the telegram would expose him at once.

Now, however, ritualistic magic had given him an idea.

Ritualistic magic borrows power from another source by requesting the aid of a particular existence. The opening lines of the incantation identify the intended recipient--the Evernight Goddess or the Lady of Crimson, for example. For unknown and concealed beings, the ritual instead uses a precise description.

Then could I alter an incantation so that its opening description points to me?

To me...

If that works, Justice and The Hanged Man could perform the ritual wherever they happen to be, and I would receive their messages.

Excitement stirred in Klein as he analyzed whether the idea was feasible.

There are two obstacles. First, I'm not a powerful high-Sequence Beyonder. Even if the incantation identifies me correctly, I might be incapable of receiving the request.

Second, how can I ensure that the description points precisely to me rather than straying toward some unknown existence that happens to fit it? That could be extraordinarily dangerous.

Klein paced silently back and forth, searching for a workable solution.

Round and round he went, until his thoughts naturally turned to the mysterious world above the gray fog.

Even if I can't receive the message, that doesn't mean the gray fog can't. Its connection to the crimson stars can drag people directly into that space regardless of where they are in the physical world.

Perhaps the description could bind me and that mysterious space together...

Following that line of reasoning, even if I don't receive their requests at the moment the rituals are performed, I should be able to see the messages the next time I enter the gray fog.

Put simply, it's the difference between receiving an instant message while online and finding it waiting after logging in.

The more Klein considered it, the more promising the idea seemed.

Hmm... What sort of description could direct the message precisely to both me and the world above the gray fog?

He already knew an incantation that would certainly work: the Loenese translation of "The Celestial Worthy of Heaven and Earth for Blessings." The problem was that using it would surrender his control over the gray fog and deprive him of his dominant position. He had no choice but to reject it.

"The Fool from another world"? No. It would be extremely precise, and there can hardly be another existence that meets those conditions, but it would expose my greatest secret...

Klein considered one phrase after another, only to discard each in turn.

After seven or eight minutes, he finally settled upon the first line of the description that would identify him.

"The Fool that doesn't belong to this era."

That alone was obviously insufficient, so Klein promptly added a second line:

"The mysterious ruler above the gray fog."

Together, the two statements narrowed the target almost exclusively to him while firmly linking him to the gray fog.

It still isn't quite enough. I can't rule out the existence of other gray-fog realms with other rulers. Nor can I exclude the possibility that the description might be diverted into the spirit world...

Klein frowned and tried to make the identification even more precise.

After a full minute of thought, he chose the final line.

"The King of Yellow and Black who wields good luck!"

It carried a meaning similar to "The Celestial Worthy of Heaven and Earth for Blessings." Used on its own, that line might point elsewhere and provoke some unknown, dangerous existence. But constrained by the first two descriptions--and reinforced by his experience of reaching the gray fog through a similar incantation--Klein believed the three lines together would lock perfectly onto the intended target.

He couldn't be certain that ritualistic magic performed with those three descriptions would work. He was, however, confident that it wouldn't draw the attention of some other existence or place Justice and The Hanged Man in danger.

Klein exhaled slowly and recited the honorific name he had devised.

"The Fool that doesn't belong to this era;

"The mysterious ruler above the gray fog;

"The King of Yellow and Black who wields good luck..."

He nodded slightly, then took out his pocket watch to check the time.

It's already 2:58...
