Without further delay, Klein put the watch away and entered Cogitation. He recited the incantation and took four counterclockwise steps, tracing the shape of a square.

The familiar savage howls and soul-shaking roars erupted around him. A headache seized him, even more difficult to endure than the pain of consuming the Seer potion.

It wasn't a sharp agony that tore through his skull, but a violent throbbing that drove him toward mania and irrationality, drowning his thoughts in chaos.

Klein maintained Cogitation and did his utmost to ignore the voices.

The whispers and murmurs gradually receded like an ebbing tide. His body became weightless and insubstantial, as did his spirituality. Everything seemed to float upward.

The boundless gray fog appeared before him, with crimson stars scattered near and far like countless watching eyes.

Above the fog stood the towering palace, immense as a giant's residence. It seemed to have endured unchanged for millions of years.

With a single thought, Klein vanished from where he stood and reappeared in the seat of honor at the long bronze table, surrounded by twenty-two high-backed chairs.

"So the ritual's effect really is fixed..." he murmured.

He lightly tapped his glabella and caused the fog around him to thicken. According to The Hanged Man, if Justice had become a Spectator, it would be best to reveal none of his unconscious habits or subtle reactions in her presence.

There was no longer time to explore. Klein extended his right hand and formed invisible connections with the two familiar crimson stars.

...

Upon the surging blue waters of the Sonia Sea, an ancient sailboat raced before the wind.

Alger Wilson had locked himself inside the captain's cabin and ordered the ghost ship to afford him its strongest protection.

He opened the pocket watch before him and placed it beside a brass sextant. Its hands ticked onward without emotion, filling the cabin with mounting tension.

The instant the hour, minute, and second hands aligned, crimson light exploded before Alger. It ignored every layer of protection he had arranged and swallowed him whole.

A sigh echoed through the empty cabin.

...

Backlund, Empress Borough.

Audrey Hall reclined against a down pillow, glancing at the yellow sheet of paper in her hand. Within her jewel-like eyes, there seemed to be two souls slowly revolving around each other.

Her gaze was cool and tranquil, like that of a spectator waiting for a performance to begin.

When crimson light burst into existence, she watched herself being engulfed with perfect detachment.

...

Above the gray fog, within the magnificent palace, figures appeared beside the ancient, mottled bronze table.

Klein had already activated his Spiritual Vision. As Audrey Hall's form took shape, he immediately noticed that the colors deep within her aura had blended together. They were now pure and tranquil, like the clear surface of a reflective lake.

She really did become a Beyonder...

Klein was about to look away when he noticed a change in Miss Justice's chair.

The brilliant stars across its back shifted rapidly, arranging themselves into an illusory constellation that did not exist in the real world.

Klein recognized it. It was one of mysticism's symbolic signs.

The symbol of a Giant Dragon!

Spectator... Giant Dragon...

Suppressing the urge to shake his head, Klein turned his attention to the back of The Hanged Man's chair.

Ordinarily, it would have been impossible to see it from his position. But this was his realm, and everything revealed itself according to his will.

The constellation on the chair hadn't changed. Now that Klein possessed a foundation in mysticism, however, he was no longer as ignorant as before. He recognized it as the symbol of a Storm.

Sailor... Keeper of the Sea... Storm... That makes sense. The color deep within The Hanged Man's aura is much purer as well... Has he advanced?

Wait--what symbol is on the back of my chair?

Klein restrained the impulse to look. As before, he tapped the edge of the long table three times, then smiled.

"Congratulations, Miss Justice. You are a Beyonder now."

He could tell immediately?

Audrey was startled, but quickly gave him a faint smile.

"Thank you, Mr. Fool. And thank you, Mr. Hanged Man."

"That was much faster than I expected," Alger Wilson admitted.

Klein didn't pursue the subject. He tapped his glabella and asked with a smile, "Lady, gentleman, have either of you found any pages from Roselle's diary?"

[1] In Chinese cosmology, yellow represents the earth, while black represents the heavens.

Chapter 59: Roselle's Origins

Upon hearing The Fool's question, Audrey did not answer immediately as she had in the past. Instead, her crystalline eyes widened slightly as she regarded The Hanged Man with a scrutinizing gaze.

Alger instinctively restrained his movements. After several seconds of silence, he said, "I found two pages from Emperor Roselle's diary and memorized their contents."

"I have one page," Audrey said. Shrouded in the gray fog, her detached tone made her sound almost removed from the conversation.

"Very good." Klein allowed neither his pleasure nor his disappointment to enter his voice.

He was pleased to receive three complete pages, but disappointed that there were only three. Their first search for the diary had naturally been the easiest: all they needed to do was inquire through contacts and channels they already knew. As time passed, finding additional pages would become increasingly difficult and require access to unfamiliar circles.

"Should we reproduce them now?" Audrey asked calmly.

"Yes." Klein gave a simple nod.

He kept his posture perfectly unchanged. In front of a Spectator, he couldn't afford to be careless.

At his command, sheets of yellow-brown goatskin parchment and dark-red fountain pens appeared before Audrey and Alger.

They picked up their pens, recalled the symbols they had memorized, and focused on expressing them. Lines of text silently materialized across the parchment. Some characters were neat, some delicate, and others slanted awkwardly.

Within a minute, everything Audrey and Alger had committed to memory had been reproduced.

With a thought, Klein brought all three pages into his hands.

A cursory glance revealed errors in the grammar, along with missing and incorrectly reproduced characters.

Fortunately, experience had shown that even a disordered Chinese sentence generally remained understandable. Missing characters didn't worry him either. He had read more than enough web novels riddled with censoring asterisks.

"April 8th. I stood at the bow of the Black King, spread my arms, and told Grimm and Edwards, 'You want my treasure? You can have it! I left everything I own at the end of the Fog Sea!'

"Neither of them understood the joke. They even asked whether I truly possessed another treasure. How boring! If this continues, neither of you deserves to be one of my Four Horsemen of the Apocalypse!"

"April 11th. I discovered an unnamed island that lies beyond the safe sea routes. It is home to a large number of extraordinary animals--no, I prefer 'extraordinary creatures.' That sounds much more impressive. There are also several truly bizarre life-forms on the island. If Darwin had transmigrated here, I don't believe he could ever have written On the Origin of Species."

"April 15th. Grimm has suddenly begun acting strangely. Has something infected him?"

When did Emperor Roselle, who was born in the Kingdom of Intis, undertake this voyage? The Fog Sea should lie west of the Intis Republic... I'll need to compare this against the historical records in the library.

Klein quickly finished the first page and turned it over.

He no longer attempted to hide his ability to decipher Emperor Roselle's secret script. Such knowledge suited the identity and status of The Fool. Audrey and Alger waited in silence, showing no surprise at the revelation. In fact, they seemed to regard it as entirely natural.

"October 2nd. They actually intend to marry me to Matilda of the Abel family without even asking me first! Heavens, I haven't so much as met her! No, I absolutely refuse! Even if I must run away, fend for myself, and endure all the hardships of the world, I will resist this marriage!"

"October 5th. Miss Matilda is extremely beautiful."

"October 6th. Her personality and bearing are exactly to my taste. I'm beginning to look forward to the wedding."

Emperor, where did your principles go?

Klein leaned back, carefully preventing his reaction from showing through the fog.

He had noticed that, in his early years, Gustav hadn't written in his diary every day. Most entries appeared only when some event gave him a reason to complain, vent his feelings, or preserve a particular memory.

Klein lowered his gaze to the final line on the page.

"October 9th. They actually called me the Son of Steam. I rather like the sound of that."

The first two pages had offered little information of practical value, leaving Klein mildly disappointed. Nevertheless, his mood didn't visibly change. He placed the third page on top. This one was covered on both sides.

"May 21st. The Church of the God of Craftsmanship offered me two choices--two starting Sequence pathways. The first is Savant, for which the Church possesses a complete Sequence pathway. The other is Mystery Pryer, which they obtained from the Moses Ascetic Order, but its higher Sequences are missing."

"May 22nd. The choice was simple: Savant! It has a complete Sequence pathway!

"A deeper understanding of mysticism might help me return home, but if I lack sufficient strength, transmigration would require assistance from an external power. I have no way of knowing whether such an existence would be good or evil, benevolent or malicious. It would lie entirely beyond my control, making the process extremely dangerous.

"Why shouldn't I strengthen myself and return through my own power instead? Therefore, the completeness of the Sequence pathway was the decisive consideration!"

"May 23rd. I have become a Savant. The potion's power allowed me to recall everything I learned in the past--physics, chemistry, and so on...

"I haven't merely recovered the knowledge. I now understand its principles, applications, and implications far more deeply. Haha! This 'class' might as well have been designed specifically for a transmigrator like me. It will allow me to exploit my advantages to their fullest!

"If I returned to my third year of high school in my present state, I would unquestionably become the top scorer. If I specialized further in a particular field, becoming a scientist wouldn't be especially difficult either."

"May 26th. I am thoroughly enjoying life as a Savant. There is one peculiar phenomenon worth recording. Whenever I think of myself as a Savant and behave in accordance with that identity, the maddening murmurs grow noticeably quieter. I am also better able to control my occasional fits of rage. This reminded me of what was said about the diary.

"Is this the 'acting' mentioned by the mysterious Mr. Zaratul? Could it be the key to resolving a potion's side effects?"

As Klein read, he became keenly aware of a fundamental difference between his own nature and Emperor Roselle's.

When considering how to return home, Klein wanted to acquire a thorough understanding of mysticism so he could avoid unnecessary risks and achieve his goal safely. Emperor Roselle, by contrast, had preferred relying on his own strength and confronting the danger directly.

I have to admit, sometimes I envy people like him. Perhaps everyone longs for qualities they lack... Of course, I need to strengthen myself as well. Both approaches are important.

Klein sighed inwardly.

More importantly, Roselle's description of acting gave him confidence that the conclusions he had reached the previous night were essentially correct.

He set down the three diary pages and looked at Justice and The Hanged Man.

"My apologies," he said with a smile. "I became absorbed in the material."

Audrey suppressed the envy in her heart and returned a faint smile.

"I understand. I hope that one day I'll be able to exchange something for information about the diary's contents."

"That would require an appropriate price." Klein smiled at Justice, then turned his gaze toward the silent Hanged Man.

Audrey brought her hands together before her.

"Mr. Fool, Mr. Hanged Man, I have three questions. If you believe the answers merit a substantial payment, tell me what you require. I'll do my best to obtain it."

"Very well," Alger replied succinctly.

Klein nodded and leaned farther back in his chair, making himself comfortable.

After thinking for several seconds, Audrey asked, "My first question is this: what does 'acting' truly mean? I've noticed that the remnant psyche in the potion has had very little effect on me. Is that because I've been behaving as a Spectator all this time?"

Alger said nothing. Instead, he looked toward The Fool, apparently waiting for him to answer.

Klein ran a finger along the edge of the table and spoke in a relaxed tone.

"Allow me to explain through an analogy. Imagine the potion's core power as a heavily guarded castle. The remnant psyche capable of causing a backlash resides within it. Our objective is to eliminate that psyche and become the castle's true master.

"There are two ways to accomplish this. The first is to storm the castle by force. Success isn't guaranteed, and unless you possess overwhelming power, you will almost certainly be injured. Naturally, none of us currently possesses that kind of strength.

"The second method is to have the castle's owner send you an invitation. That invitation allows you to pass the guards' inspection and enter the castle without resistance, after which you can easily dispose of the enemy within. The difficulty is that the invitation specifies its guest's appearance and distinguishing characteristics. Therefore, we must disguise ourselves and act like the invited guest. Do you understand?"

As though he had anticipated the answer, Alger immediately asked, "Then this so-called invitation is the name of the potion's Sequence?"

"Correct," Klein replied with complete confidence.

Audrey froze for a moment. Suddenly, she felt that she understood the true meaning of acting.

Her excitement caused her to slip out of her Spectator state.

"What an extraordinary method! I think... I think it suits your title perfectly. Its nature is remarkably compatible with The Fool. I would never have imagined that acting could have such an effect. Fortunately, I've spent the past few days behaving as a Spectator."

She paused before continuing.

"I believe that answer is extremely valuable. I wouldn't feel comfortable accepting it without payment. Mr. Fool, what would you like in exchange? Of course, I haven't forgotten that I still owe you a page from Emperor Roselle's diary."

"More pages from Roselle's diary, or..." Klein paused.

He had intended to request information about the Seer pathway's later Sequences. However, such a low-level request might undermine The Fool's mysterious and exalted image. He abandoned the idea, planning to inquire more discreetly in the future.

I only advanced recently and haven't finished digesting the Seer potion yet...

After consoling himself, he continued without expression, "Any information concerning the Antigonus family--even if it happens to be something I already know."

Alger remained silent for several seconds. His gaze lingered on the head of the long bronze table before he finally spoke.

"Mr. Fool... I believe I can repay you immediately with the information you just requested."

Chapter 60: The Second Blasphemy Slate

"Very well." Klein did his best to keep his deep voice unchanged.

He rested his left elbow on the armrest and lightly supported his forehead with his fingers, assuming the posture of someone listening with calm interest.

Alger considered his words before saying, "The Antigonus family is extremely ancient. Its history can be traced back to the Cataclysm Epoch, before the Fourth Epoch, and it is connected to the second Blasphemy Slate."

The second Blasphemy Slate? There's more than one? How many are there?

Klein's pupils contracted, and he nearly shifted in his seat.

According to what The Hanged Man and Justice had previously said, the Blasphemy Slate contained the profound mysteries of the twenty-two paths of the divine.

There are two such important artifacts--perhaps even more?

Twenty-two paths of the divine... Sequences and pathways... Could the terms refer to the same thing? Does every complete Sequence pathway lead directly to the throne of a god?

The mere mention of a second Blasphemy Slate sent countless thoughts racing through Klein's mind. If the thick gray-white fog hadn't concealed him, Miss Spectator would almost certainly have detected his emotional response.

As a historian, he was quite familiar with the term Cataclysm Epoch. It referred to the Third Epoch.

Thanks to his recent review, Klein also knew that the Third Epoch was divided into two periods: the Glorious Era and the Cataclysm Era.

"A second Blasphemy Slate?" Audrey asked, openly revealing her ignorance.

After recovering from her initial surprise, she slipped back into her Spectator state.

Excellent question! Klein silently applauded Miss Justice.

It was precisely the sort of question he couldn't conveniently ask while playing The Fool.

Alger stole a glance at the head of the table. Seeing that The Fool's posture hadn't changed and that he offered no comment, Alger continued.

"The first Blasphemy Slate appeared during the Dark Epoch--the Second Epoch, when humanity struggled to survive beneath the protection of the gods. The second appeared near the end of the Third Epoch. Its appearance could even be said to mark the end of the Cataclysm Epoch.

"The seven major churches keep the contents of both Blasphemy Slates strictly secret. I know only scattered fragments. I know they concern the paths of the divine, but I cannot say how the two slates differ."

"Did Emperor Roselle see the first Blasphemy Slate or the second?" Audrey asked curiously.

Her question reminded Klein of something Alger had said during their first Gathering: the names of the Sequence potions came from the Blasphemy Slate.

Captain Dunn had likewise mentioned that the emergence of the Blasphemy Slate had allowed the potion system to take shape and become complete.

That indirectly confirms it. The paths of the divine are the Sequence pathways!

Klein silently answered his earlier question.

"The second," Alger replied simply.

The light in Audrey's eyes faded as she returned fully to the detached state of a Spectator. She asked nothing more and merely kept her gaze trained upon The Hanged Man.

Her scrutiny clearly made Alger uncomfortable, but he suppressed the feeling and continued in a low voice.

"During the Solomon Dynasty of the Fourth Epoch, the Antigonus family was part of the highest aristocracy, but accomplished little of lasting note. That changed when they supported the founding of the Tudor Empire. From then on, they stood at the very center of the Northern Continent's political stage.

"During that age, Antigonus, Amon, Abraham, Jacob, and several others were among the most illustrious names in the human world. But after the War of the Four Emperors, the Blood Emperor of the Tudor Empire perished. Those families fell from the summit of power and became targets of the seven deities.

"I don't know the exact sequence of events, but I do know that the Antigonus family was destroyed by the Church of the Evernight Goddess. Mr. Fool, if you wish to learn more, I'm afraid you'll have to obtain the information from that Church or from one of the few ancient secret organizations. You know which ones I mean."

I don't...

Feeling bitter inside, Klein nodded calmly.

"Indeed."

The Secret Order is one. Captain and Old Neil have mentioned the Moses Ascetic Order. I wonder whether the Psychology Alchemists count...

While he mentally reviewed the possibilities, Alger offered the last of his information.

"I also don't know which Sequence pathway the Antigonus family controlled. Only two adjectives recur throughout the accounts concerning them: 'strange' and 'terrifying.'"

Strange and terrifying... Considering the notebook, what happened to the original Klein and his classmates, and the fate of Ray Bieber's mother, those words are remarkably appropriate...

Klein tapped the edge of the long table several times with one fingertip before slowly speaking.

"Very good. I am satisfied with the payment."

He had deliberately repeated the gesture so Justice and The Hanged Man would come to regard it as one of his habitual mannerisms. That would conceal the fact that he used the same motion to activate and deactivate his Spiritual Vision.

"The pleasure is mine," Alger replied, saying nothing more.

Audrey glanced from The Hanged Man to The Fool. With a faint smile, she said, "Then I'll ask my second question. What is the potion following Spectator, and where might I find clues concerning it?"

I would like to ask just as directly, but different choices create different difficulties...

Klein remained silent and turned his gaze toward The Hanged Man.

Alger considered for several seconds.

"I'll answer without payment, since I was the one who guided you onto this pathway.

"The Sequence following Spectator is Sequence 8: Telepathist. The ancient name for Sequence 7 was Psyche Analyst, though it is now called Psychiatrist. I learned this from a member of the Psychology Alchemists. I believe they possess a considerable number of formulas belonging to the pathway."

Psychology Alchemists... Spirit Medium Daly thought rather highly of their theories, but Captain Dunn considers them evil and insane...

Klein listened thoughtfully.

Audrey's eyes brightened with delight. "Do you know where that member of the Psychology Alchemists can be found?"

Both Telepathist and Psychiatrist appealed greatly to her sense of aesthetics.

Alger gave a rare laugh.

"I do. He lies at the bottom of the sea near Sonia Island. I sank him with my own hands.

"If you intend to search for the Psychology Alchemists, then I must apologize. I severed that particular lead."

He wasn't concerned that the story might reveal his identity. He had acted alone, far from any witnesses.

"Sank..." Audrey didn't know how to respond or what expression to make.

She took a deep breath, once again losing her Spectator state. With visible embarrassment, she asked, "My third question is purely hypothetical. If--and I mean if--an ordinary animal drank a Sequence 9 potion, what would happen?"

What sort of question is that?

With the finger supporting his forehead, Klein subtly tapped his glabella.

His Spiritual Vision immediately revealed a frantic mixture of anxiety, nervousness, and shame in Audrey's emotional colors.

Did she actually do something that foolish?

Klein was astonished, though not entirely surprised. After the previous two Gatherings, he had already concluded that Miss Justice could be rather airheaded.

The Hanged Man appeared equally dumbfounded. It took Alger several seconds to answer.

"Ordinary animals don't possess human intelligence and would be unable to learn Cogitation in time. In most cases, they would die immediately or mutate into monsters. However, if an animal survived the initial effects of the potion, it would become an extraordinary creature. And if the potion enhanced intelligence, it might become considerably smarter."

"I see." Audrey quietly exhaled in relief, then said in a relaxed voice, "I have no further questions."

Alger thought for a moment. He chose not to mention the Aurora Order or the Listener and likewise shook his head.

"Neither do I."

"I have one matter," Klein said with a smile, his posture unchanged. "It will require your cooperation."

Because he had not deactivated Spiritual Vision, he immediately saw tension arise within The Hanged Man. Miss Justice, meanwhile, was too guileless to display either fear or caution.

Before either could respond, Klein reassured them.

"Don't worry. It's a trivial experiment. If it succeeds, you will benefit as well, so I won't offer additional compensation."

"Please continue." Audrey instinctively entered her Spectator state, but the dense gray fog surrounding The Fool remained impenetrable.

"As you wish," Alger replied, steadying himself.

Klein moved his fingers and smiled.

"I previously mentioned conducting experiments that would allow you to request a leave of absence. That way, you won't need to worry if circumstances prevent you from attending on a Monday afternoon."

"That would certainly be helpful." Audrey's tightly drawn brows relaxed.

Alger considered the proposal. "What do you require us to do?"

"When you have time, perform a simple ritual. It needn't be excessively formal, so long as you are somewhere you won't be disturbed.

"Place four new candles at the four corners of an altar, preferably candles scented with sandalwood. Beside the upper-left candle, place a loaf of white bread. Put a bowl of Feynapotter noodles beside the upper-right candle, paella beside the lower-left, and a Desi pie beside the lower-right. Then use a silver knife to create a sealed spiritual environment..."

Klein described his modified version of the luck-enhancement ritual. As part of the explanation, he taught Miss Justice how to create a spiritual wall without charging her.

In truth, because the ritual was directed at Klein himself, he suspected that the opening procedures intended to attract an existence's attention could be omitted entirely. Nevertheless, he took pains to make every step sound indispensable. Naturally, the arrangement did not conform to Old Neil's rule that the deity came second and the ritualist third.

"...Combine moon flowers, gold mint, slumber flowers, fingered citron, and rock rose, then distill the mixture into an essential oil. Place one drop on each candle..."

Audrey listened with keen interest, carefully recording every instruction. When he finished, she asked, "What about the incantation? Mr. Fool, what words should we recite?"

Alger also stopped writing and turned toward the head of the table.

Shrouded in gray fog, Klein lightly tapped the long table and spoke in calm, measured Hermes.

"The Fool that doesn't belong to this era;

"The mysterious ruler above the gray fog;

"The King of Yellow and Black who wields good luck..."

Chapter 61: A Strange Symbol

The Fool that doesn't belong to this era... The mysterious ruler above the gray fog... The King of Yellow and Black who wields good luck...

Audrey Hall silently repeated the three descriptions. A turbulent wave of emotion swept through her, instantly disrupting her Spectator state.

As an enthusiast of mysticism, she had learned the ritualistic form of Hermes. Even before being drawn above the gray fog and formally encountering Beyonder powers, she had experimented with rituals discussed by her fellow noble enthusiasts during private gatherings.

None of those rituals had produced any effect, but they had at least taught Audrey the basic structure of an incantation.

Thus, she understood perfectly what those three lines meant.

Descriptions of that form were normally reserved for one of the seven gods who watched over the world!

The Fool's incantation therefore claimed a status equal to that of the Lady of Crimson, the Mother of Concealment, and the Empress of Misfortune and Horror!

Is Mr. Fool one of the unknown, mysterious, godlike entities Glaint mentioned? One of the dangerous existences we were warned never to invoke during a ritual?

Audrey rapidly recalled the warnings exchanged during the occult gatherings she and her friends had once attended--warnings about rituals none of them had dared attempt. For a moment, she was completely at a loss for words.

Alger Wilson, whose knowledge and experience far exceeded Audrey's, felt a chill rise from the depths of his heart.

If the ritual Mr. Fool designed truly points to him and allows him to receive our requests, then w-we would have to address him as Him. The divine pronoun, reserved for gods...

How fortunate that I cooperated with him from the beginning and never did anything reckless. Even when I tested him, I stayed within acceptable bounds...

Could he be an ancient, mysterious, terrifying existence who simply chooses not to appear before us in his true form or under his real name? The Primordial Demoness? The Hidden Sage? Or the True Creator worshiped by so many secret cults?

Alger understood that the figure seated before him might bear no resemblance to The Fool's true form. The being might not even possess a gender or a humanoid body.

Supporting his forehead with one hand and tapping the table with the other, Klein keenly noticed the changes in Justice and The Hanged Man.

He behaved as though nothing unusual had occurred, as if their reactions were entirely within his expectations. In an unconcerned tone, he continued reciting the ritual.

"I pray for your aid.

"I pray for your loving grace.

"I pray that you grant me a good dream.

"Moon flower, herb of the red moon, bestow your power upon my incantation!

"Fingered citron, herb of the sun, bestow your power upon my incantation!"

After finishing the incantation for this alternate form of ritual, he smiled.

"Lady, gentleman, have you memorized it?"

"Ah..." Audrey exhaled, then quickly covered her mouth and concentrated on recalling his instructions.

With the enhanced memory of a Spectator, she swiftly organized the information and repeated the incantation to confirm it.

Alger behaved more normally. Whatever thoughts might have been running through his mind, his pen never stopped moving.

Once Klein confirmed that Audrey's repetition was correct, he said with a smile, "If this experiment succeeds, we can modify the ritual next time to achieve our intended purpose.

"I hope both of you can perform it by Wednesday at the latest."

Klein planned to return above the gray fog on Thursday night to determine whether the ritualistic magic had worked.

He hadn't asked Justice and The Hanged Man to request leave directly because the result might prove ambiguous. If a message appeared, he wouldn't know whether it was a genuine request for absence or merely evidence that the ritual had succeeded. In that situation, should he pull them into an unscheduled Gathering or not?

"As you wish," Audrey and Alger replied respectfully after composing themselves.

"In accordance with The Hanged Man's suggestion last week, we will reserve time for casual conversation after concluding official business. Who would like to begin?"

Klein gestured for either of them to speak.

Audrey breathed out and said, "Mr. Fool, your proposal concerning competitive examinations and the separation of administrative and political appointments has gained the approval of several members of Parliament. It may actually become reality. Of course, given the government's efficiency, the corresponding bill won't appear for at least another six months."

She wasn't worried that this information would allow The Hanged Man to identify her. In her conversations with the wives of influential men, she had offered intermittent hints and carefully guided those proud ladies into believing they had conceived the idea themselves. They had then rushed to present it to their husbands, fathers, and brothers.

At the time, Audrey had felt as though she were watching a flock of golden peacocks spreading their magnificent tails.

She was confident that the women would soon convince themselves completely that the proposal belonged to them. They would forget Audrey's contribution and quarrel over who deserved credit for originating it.

Influencing the institutions of a kingdom through such subtle means gave Audrey a peculiar sense of satisfaction. It was as if she had discovered how a Spectator could alter the plot of the play without ever stepping onto the stage.

"Let us hope so," Alger replied with a trace of sarcasm.

After several seconds, he glanced toward The Fool and chose his words carefully.

"Over the past few decades, the activities of various secret organizations have increased considerably. New organizations have also emerged, some of which have already grown large enough to include a substantial number of Beyonders."

Are you asking me to explain why? I haven't even been given access to the Nighthawks' information on illegal organizations...

Klein merely smiled, offering no comment on The Hanged Man's news. Instead, he changed the subject with a deliberately vague statement.

"An ancient power is about to awaken from its slumber."

For example, the power represented by the Antigonus family's notebook...

"Is that so..." Alger murmured, as though the words had reminded him of something.

Klein's gaze passed from The Hanged Man to Justice.

"If neither of you has anything else to share, then today's Gathering will end here."

"As you wish."

Audrey and Alger rose together.

Klein moved one finger and severed his connections to the dark-red stars. He watched as their figures vanished from the magnificent palace.

Then he stood and turned toward the back of his own chair--the seat of honor at the bronze table--to examine its symbol.

Radiant stars formed a strange design, one that corresponded to nothing in Klein's present knowledge of mysticism.

Upon closer inspection, he identified a Pupil-less Eye, the symbol of concealment, along with Contorted Lines representing change. Both symbols were incomplete, their missing portions overlapping to create an entirely new emblem.

Incomplete concealment combined with incomplete change... What does that produce?

Klein frowned and murmured to himself, but no answer came.

He looked away and began walking through the ancient, magnificent palace, carefully examining every corner.

When I casually imagined this place, I formed only a rough concept. I never specified the shape of the palace, the table, or the chairs... So where did these designs come from? Are they the optimal forms? Some original prototype? Or reflections of something that exists in reality?

As he surveyed the palace, Klein realized that he had overlooked this question until now.

Sigh. For all my experience as a keyboard warrior, there are still far too many areas in which I lack practical experience. My powers of observation aren't good enough either. How did it take me this long to notice?

After that moment of self-reflection, he conducted a serious inspection of the palace. He found neither other living creatures nor any additional anomalies.

Klein didn't dare venture into the seemingly boundless, illusory region beyond it. He feared that if he went too far, he might become hopelessly lost.

This place truly is filled with mysteries... I wonder whether it will change as I grow stronger.

With a sigh, Klein released his spirituality and wrapped it around himself. At once, he felt as though he were plummeting at tremendous speed.

Everything rushed past him. Layer after layer of illusion shattered. He tore through the gray-white fog and returned to reality, where his bedroom table, curtains, and clothes rack came into view.

...

Backlund, Empress Borough.

Audrey opened her eyes to the oil painting on the wall and the softness of the down pillow beneath her head.

She didn't rise immediately. Instead, she carefully reviewed the entire Gathering, as though watching a play for the second time.

Mr. Fool sounded completely confident when he asked us to try the ritual and gave us the descriptions of the mysterious ruler and the King of Yellow and Black... Completely confident...

Audrey analyzed the memory in silence, then exhaled as a slight tremor ran through her body.

Forget it. If I can't resist him, there's no point worrying endlessly. Mr. Fool has always behaved kindly. He should be an existence who respects order...

Her mood quickly improved. She turned her attention to her acting and the weakening influence of the potion's remnant psyche.

Humming a cheerful melody, Audrey climbed out of bed and walked toward the door. She adjusted her mental state and once more became a Spectator.

When she opened the door, she saw a maid passing in the corridor. Audrey noticed the old calluses on the woman's hands, the marks on her face, and numerous other small details from which she could infer a great deal.

Then Audrey experienced an odd sensation.

She abruptly turned toward the shaded corner of the balcony.

Her golden retriever, Susie, sat there in silence, observing Audrey in precisely the same way Audrey had observed the maid.

My Goddess...

Audrey's lips twitched. She desperately wanted to cover her face.

...

On the Sonia Sea, inside the heavily protected captain's cabin, Alger awakened to find everything unchanged, as though nothing had happened.

He sighed and thought, An ancient existence?

...

After leaving the ritual, Klein opened his curtains, took out his notebook, and began writing once more.

He reconstructed the contents of Emperor Roselle's diary, using the act of writing to reinforce the memories and prevent them from fading.

When he finished, Klein read through the notes several times. Then he tore them up and burned them.

Repeating this once a week should preserve the most important details. But as time passes and my assignments grow increasingly complicated... Unfortunately, I don't have a better solution yet. I haven't learned cryptography either.

Klein composed himself and rolled his neck, preparing to visit the Divination Club.

Different people held different definitions of what a Seer should be, and no one could definitively declare another person's approach incorrect. Since Klein didn't yet know which interpretation best matched the potion's requirements, he could only experiment, make adjustments, and gradually discover the proper way to act.

Chapter 62: The Seer's Suggestion

Before leaving home, Klein carefully brushed his suit and top hat and wiped away every speck of dust with a handkerchief. He then washed his white shirt and changed into a similar linen one, pairing it with the only other presentable--if inexpensive--coat he owned before setting out.

First Melissa's dress, then Benson's suit. Only after that can I consider buying myself a second suit. There is never enough money... We also need to save for porcelain tableware so we can properly receive guests. And I need funds for all sorts of mysticism materials...

Sitting in the public carriage, Klein reviewed the household finances. The more he calculated, the more helplessly he shook his head.

He estimated that it would take at least a year before he and his siblings could truly live like a middle-class family.

Of course, that estimate didn't account for promotions or salary increases.

The carriage rolled through the streets and stopped opposite the Divination Club on Howes Street.

Klein held down his black top hat as he hopped off. He followed the familiar road, climbed to the second floor, and entered the club, where he immediately saw the pretty brunette Angelica.

Her eyes were still slightly swollen and red, but she looked remarkably relaxed.

Klein raised a hand and lightly tapped his glabella. Studying her carefully through Spiritual Vision, he found that most of the gray within Angelica's emotional colors had dispersed, replaced by a warm, sunlight-like white.

After taking in the change, he approached, removed his hat, and smiled.

"Madam Angelica, it's a lovely day, isn't it?"

Angelica looked up in surprise. Then she beamed.

"You're just like Mr. Vincent's cat. You don't make a sound when you walk, do you? And you could tell at a glance?" She chuckled. "I nearly forgot that you're a fortune-teller skilled in physiognomy..."

She paused, lightly bit her lip, and bowed.

"Thank you. Thank you for your advice yesterday. I feel much better. I haven't been this relaxed, happy, or content in an entire year."

Her sincere gratitude was infectious. The corners of Klein's mouth lifted.

"I'm glad I could help."

As he spoke, he felt his spirituality relax and become more vibrant.

Is this what the Seer potion requires? A Seer who genuinely helps those seeking guidance?

Klein pinched the bridge of his nose as though considering something, then secretly tapped his glabella twice.

He had to admit that his method of activating and deactivating Spiritual Vision wasn't subtle enough for regular use. Unfortunately, he hadn't yet discovered a better one. He had only recently become a Seer; neither his spirituality nor his control had reached its limit. Few parts of his body could currently serve as a reliable switch, and the glabella remained by far the best option.

Once I fully digest the potion and become a true Seer, I should be able to devise a less conspicuous gesture...

Klein gave an imperceptible nod and started toward the half-open meeting room.

"Coffee or tea?" Angelica hurriedly asked.

"Desi coffee."

Klein intended to sample every beverage the Divination Club offered.

Looking into the meeting room, he saw six or seven members, but Hanass Vincent--who seemed to be present almost every time--was not among them.

"Mr. Vincent isn't here today?" Klein paused to ask casually.

Angelica looked slightly taken aback. "Mr. Vincent doesn't come every day. He was invited to give a lecture at a divination society in Enmat Harbor. Were you hoping to speak with him?"

"Not at all. I was merely curious, since I've seen him on every previous visit." Klein shook his head with a smile.

At the same time, he spotted a familiar face among the members.

Glacis, who had once performed a divination for him, was there.

Glacis sat reading through some papers with a monocle over one eye. As if sensing Klein's gaze, he raised his head and looked across the room.

Delight immediately spread across his face. Bracing both hands on the table, he rose and hurried over to Klein.

"Good afternoon, Mr. Moretti. I've been wondering whether you would come today.

"Angelica told me that you aren't a physician, but a fortune-teller skilled in physiognomy?"

Klein smiled.

"Physiognomy isn't my only specialty, Mr. Glacis. I see that your illness is no longer troubling you."

He pinched his forehead and tapped his glabella twice. Glacis's physical aura had returned to normal.

"Yes. I deeply regretted ignoring your warning. Fortunately, there's a remarkable apothecary near my home. He gave my wife a miraculous medicine that pulled me back from the brink of death," Glacis said with emotion.

As a provisional Nighthawk, Klein's professional instincts were immediately aroused.

"A remarkable apothecary? Miraculous medicine?"

Miraculous in what sense? Was it within the realm of Beyonder powers?

"He said it was a folk remedy from Lenburg. Whatever it was, it proved extremely effective against my illness," Glacis replied, noticing nothing unusual in the question.

A folk-medicine apothecary?

Klein tapped his glabella as though thinking.

"What is his name, and where can he be found? As you know, even a fortune-teller cannot guarantee perfect health forever. I may need to purchase medicine from him someday."

From his teachers and classmates, Klein knew that this world's medical system remained in its infancy. Many illnesses had no reliable treatment, leaving ample room in the market for miraculous medicines and mysterious apothecaries. Learning about this one could prove useful someday.

Glacis answered without hesitation.

"His name is Lawson Darkwill. He owns a tiny shop at 18 Vlad Street in East Borough called Lawson's Folk Herb Store."

"Thank you." Klein committed the details to memory and expressed his gratitude sincerely.

Glacis turned and invited him to sit nearby. At that moment, Angelica arrived with the freshly brewed coffee.

Compared with Southville coffee, Desi coffee has a richer aroma, but the flavor is considerably worse...

Klein took a sip and savored it for a moment.

As soon as he set down the white porcelain cup, Glacis carefully chose his words.

"Mr. Moretti, may I request a divination from you? I'll pay whatever fee you set."

Klein had been hoping for precisely such a request.

"Eight pence will be sufficient. I don't intend to raise my fee without warning. Shall we use one of the divination rooms?"

"Yes. The Topaz Room." Familiar with the club, Glacis led the way.

Once they were inside and the door was locked, Klein took his place behind the long table.

"Mr. Glacis," he asked seriously, "what matter would you like me to divine?"

"I've been offered an investment opportunity, but it requires a considerable sum. If it fails, my family and I will suffer a severe financial blow. I want to know whether the investment will succeed," Glacis explained. "I already performed a tarot divination after purifying my spirit. The result was quite favorable. I interpreted the cards myself, but I followed the established symbolism faithfully."

After some thought, Klein replied, "Please describe the entire situation again and give me your personal information. If possible, provide information about the other party as well. We'll perform an astrolabe divination."

"Very well." Glacis organized his thoughts. "While surveying the Hornacis mountain range, Mr. Lanevus discovered an enormous deposit of high-quality iron ore. He spent all his savings purchasing the land and hired a professional company to survey it. The findings were extremely promising.

"He lacks the funds required for development, so he has established a steel company and intends to use the project as collateral for a bank loan. At the same time, he will issue shares to raise the initial capital. The venture is still in preparation, but the projected returns are substantial."

As a regular newspaper reader and something of a history expert, Klein knew that shares existed in this world. He also knew who had introduced the concept.

Emperor Roselle. Again.

During the colonization of the Southern Continent, Roselle had founded the West Balam Company. By issuing shares and raising money from the public, he had successfully resolved the nation's funding problem and secured the first-mover advantage in colonial development.

The enormous returns ensured that the system continued. Railway shares, mining shares, steam-development shares, and countless other securities followed. Some ventures succeeded and others collapsed, ultimately giving rise to institutions such as the Backlund Stock Exchange.

Roselle had also introduced government bonds, unit trusts, and other financial products. Government bonds had become the most reliable investment available, yielding four to six percent annually.

Klein remembered Benson once saying that if he inherited three thousand pounds, he would never need to work again. At a stable return of approximately five percent, that sum would produce an annual income of 150 pounds--roughly equal to Klein's current salary.

Rentier capitalism at its finest...

Klein sighed inwardly and asked carefully, "Are you certain everything is legitimate? Is Lanevus trustworthy?"

"I've seen the property deeds and survey report. They bear the seal of the Sivellaus County government and the endorsement of a professional firm. In addition, Mr. Lanevus keeps a photograph in his office showing him with Sir Deweyville and the mayor," Glacis replied with a nod.

A photograph proves nothing...

Having lived through an age of information overload, Klein had encountered far too many similar scams to find that persuasive.

His personal opinion was irrelevant, however. Using the important dates and information Glacis supplied, he picked up his pen and constructed the corresponding astrolabe.

After a long period of calculation and interpretation, Klein pointed to the chart.

"As you can see, this undertaking is destined to fail. Beneath its prosperous surface lies a cliff--a bottomless chasm. My divination advises you to go around it and stay far away."

Glacis fell silent. His mouth opened and closed several times without producing a sound.

Several minutes later, he gave a bitter smile.

"I'll give the matter careful consideration when I return home."

Hearing that answer, Klein could only shake his head and sigh inwardly. He once again felt the helplessness inherent in being a Seer.

A Seer could offer advice, but could never make another person's decisions.

Just as they left the Topaz Room, Angelica approached.

"Mr. Moretti, someone else would like a divination from you." Lowering her voice, she added, "He didn't ask me for a recommendation, and he didn't look through the album."

Has my reputation already begun to spread?

Puzzled, Klein turned toward the reception hall.

Chapter 63: Dream Interpretation

Klein walked forward a few steps and saw the waiting client.

The man wore a formal black suit and half top hat and carried a wooden cane inlaid with gold. His short blond hair flared out at the sides, and his nose curved sharply like a hawk's beak.

Anna's fiance... Joyce Meyer, the man who survived that terrifying voyage.

Having seen him during the dream divination, Klein recognized him immediately.

"Good afternoon, Mr. Meyer," he greeted with a smile.

"Good afternoon, Mr. Moretti." Joyce removed his hat and bowed. "Thank you for the advice you gave Anna. She hasn't stopped talking about how miraculous you are."

Klein chuckled.

"I didn't change anything. You should thank yourself. Without your determination and hope for a better tomorrow, you could never have survived such an ordeal."

After exchanging these pleasantries, Klein couldn't resist making a private joke.

Does this count as two professionals flattering each other?

"To be honest, my survival still feels like a dream. I can scarcely believe I endured one terrifying ordeal after another and made it home alive." Joyce shook his head wistfully.

Before Klein could reply, he asked with curiosity, "You knew who I was the moment you saw me. Was it my distinctive nose, or did you divine that I would visit today?"

"I possessed detailed information about you. For a Seer, that is sufficient," Klein replied vaguely, adopting the manner of an accomplished charlatan.

Joyce was visibly taken aback. More than ten seconds passed before he managed a smile.

"Mr. Moretti, I would like to request a divination."

As soon as he said it, a detail suddenly struck him.

Klein Moretti had called himself a Seer, not merely a fortune-teller.

A Seer!

"Very well. We'll use the Topaz Room." Klein gestured toward the hallway.

At that moment, he felt that he ought to be wearing a long black robe. He deliberately kept his words brief, hoping to enhance the mystique of a true Seer.

After entering the divination room, Joyce Meyer locked the door behind him. While the man examined his surroundings, Klein seized the opportunity to tap his glabella twice and activate Spiritual Vision.

Joyce sat down, placed his cane beside him, and tugged nervously at his black bow tie.

"Mr. Moretti," he said hoarsely, "I would like you to interpret a dream."

"A dream?" Klein asked for confirmation while behaving as though he had expected precisely that.

The colors representing Joyce's physical condition appeared dim, but none suggested an impending illness. His emotional aura was dominated by a deep blue whose intensity made his tension obvious.

Joyce nodded solemnly.

"Ever since the Alfalfa reached Enmat Harbor, I've had the same horrible dream every night. I understand that it may be trauma from what I experienced and that I should consult a psychiatrist. But I suspect this isn't an ordinary nightmare.

"Even when normal dreams recur, some details change from one night to the next. This dream--at least the parts I can remember--is exactly the same every time."

"To a Seer, dreams of that kind are revelations granted by the divine," Klein said, partly explaining and partly reassuring him. "Please describe it to me."

Joyce clenched his fists and held them against his mouth. After thinking deeply for a moment, he began.

"I dream that I'm falling from the Alfalfa into the ocean. The water is dark red, as though the sea is filled with decaying blood.

"As I fall, someone aboard the ship catches me. I can't identify him, but I know he's extremely strong.

"At the same time, I'm holding another man, trying to keep him from falling into the sea. I recognize him. He was a passenger aboard the Alfalfa named Younis Kim.

"He's too heavy, and he struggles so violently that I can't hold on. I have no choice but to release him and watch the sea of blood swallow him.

"At that moment, the person above me also lets go. I flail my arms and try to seize something, but there's nothing within reach. I can only plummet toward the water.

"Then I wake in terror, with sweat covering my forehead and back."

Klein touched his forehead and tapped it gently, as though deep in thought. After organizing his ideas, he said, "Mr. Meyer, nightmares--whether similar or identical, whether occasional or recurring--generally arise from a psychological source. When exactly the same nightmare repeats night after night, it is a warning from your spirituality and a revelation from the divine."

Seeing Joyce's confusion, he elaborated.

"Don't doubt it. Even an ordinary person's spirituality can issue warnings.

"I don't know precisely what happened aboard the Alfalfa, but I can tell it was a tragedy of blood and steel, one that left a deep wound in your mind."

When Joyce nodded slightly, Klein continued.

"You must have been consumed by fear and horror aboard the ship. Under the pressure of such intense emotions, people often lose their powers of observation and overlook signs they would normally notice.

"That doesn't mean you never saw those signs. It means you disregarded them. Do you understand? You saw them, but dismissed them.

"Your subconscious mind--your spirituality--still retains those overlooked details. If what they point toward is important enough, your spirituality will remind you through dreams."

Something similar happened to me. I disregarded a strange feeling and only later realized that Ray Bieber possessed the notebook... But my spirituality is stronger, I'm more sensitive, and I know more about mysticism, so I reached the conclusion sooner.

Klein paused, then looked directly into Joyce Meyer's eyes.

"Did Younis Kim--the man you released into the sea of blood--ask something of you aboard the ship, only to meet his death anyway?"

Joyce shifted uncomfortably. His mouth opened several times before he finally answered.

"Yes, but I don't pity him. In a few days--perhaps a week--you'll probably read in the newspaper how cruel and evil he was. He raped and murdered at least three women and threw an infant into the Berserk Sea. He also led a band of savages who had lost all reason and massacred the ship's passengers and crew.

"He was cunning, powerful, and vicious. I couldn't stop him, and I didn't dare try. I would only have thrown away my own life."

"I'm not questioning your actions," Klein said, making his position clear. "But the dream tells me that you feel regret and guilt. If you truly believe letting him die was an act of justice, why do you regret releasing him so deeply that the moment returns in your dreams every night?"

"I don't know..." Joyce shook his head in confusion.

Klein interlaced his fingers and rested them beneath his chin, attempting to analyze the situation.

"Based on what I've explained, you must have overlooked certain details. Perhaps something Younis Kim said, the words of his plea, the way he behaved, or some similar sign. I cannot recover the memory for you. You must think back carefully."

"There was nothing... All he said was, 'Spare me. I surrender,'" Joyce murmured, bewildered.

Since Klein didn't know exactly what had happened aboard the ship, he could only guide Joyce according to the dream's symbolism.

"Perhaps you believed Younis Kim would be more useful alive--that he could prove or explain something?"

Joyce knitted his brows. A long time passed before he replied.

"Perhaps... The conflict aboard the Alfalfa began too suddenly and escalated too quickly. It was as if all the hidden evil in everyone's heart erupted at once, completely beyond control. It was abnormal--deeply abnormal...

"Perhaps I wanted to interrogate Younis Kim and discover why he had behaved as though possessed by a devil..."

Joyce's dreamlike description gave Klein a sudden flash of inspiration. He spoke in the mysterious, confident tones of a professional charlatan.

"No. That isn't the only reason."

"What?" Joyce looked startled.

Klein kept his hands interlaced beneath his chin and stared directly into Joyce's eyes. His words emerged slowly, each one carrying force.

"You didn't merely find the incident abnormal. You witnessed certain things and then disregarded them. Taken together, those forgotten details lead to a terrifying conclusion.

"Your spirituality is warning you that one person deserves the greatest suspicion. In the dream, that person is the one who caught you and later let you fall.

"Your conscious mind cannot identify him because you don't wish to suspect him. He was your companion. He once held your fate in his hands--perhaps he even saved your life."

Joyce recoiled and struck the back of the chair with a dull thud.

Sweat gradually beaded across his forehead, and confusion filled his eyes.

"I... I can see him..."

He suddenly surged to his feet. The chair rocked and nearly toppled behind him.

"Mr. Tris..." Joyce forced the name from his throat with all his strength.

The friendly, bashful young man with the round face. The hero who had saved the survivors...

Klein didn't interrupt. He leaned back slightly and waited while Joyce wrestled with the realization.

The man's expression shifted again and again before finally settling into something approaching normal, though his face remained pale.

He gave a bitter smile.

"I understand now. Thank you for interpreting my dream. Perhaps it's time I paid a visit to the police station."

He took out his leather wallet and removed a one-soli banknote.

"I don't believe money can fully represent the value of what you've done, but I can at least pay the price you requested. Please accept this."

I wouldn't have objected to ten pounds... One soli. You really are well matched with your fiancee.

Maintaining the mysterious bearing of a Seer, Klein said nothing. He merely smiled and pressed a hand against the banknote.

Joyce took a deep breath, put on his hat, and walked toward the door.

As he unlocked it, he suddenly turned back.

"Thank you, Master Moretti," he said with complete sincerity.

Master?

Klein laughed inwardly and watched Joyce leave the divination room.

Whatever happened aboard the Alfalfa was clearly extraordinary... If only Captain Dunn were here. He could probably understand everything hidden within Joyce Meyer's dream.

...

At dawn on Tuesday, in Backlund's Empress Borough, Audrey rose earlier than usual and beckoned her golden retriever closer.

"Susie, you're a Beyonder now too. We're the same kind--ugh, no, that isn't what I mean." Audrey adopted a serious tone. "We need to help each other. Guard the door for me and don't let anyone interrupt. I have to perform a ritual."

Susie looked at her mistress and wagged her tail with evident exasperation.

Chapter 64: Instigator

After giving instructions to her golden retriever, Susie, Audrey paced restlessly around the room. She, too, was uncertain whether the ritualistic magic might produce some unexpected phenomenon.

Let's approach this differently...

Her eyes grew calm as she adopted the detached perspective of a Spectator and mentally observed the process she was about to perform. Before long, she devised a new arrangement.

Audrey unlocked her bedroom door and said, "Susie, sit here. If Annie or any of the others tries to enter, come to the bathroom and warn me immediately."

Her personal maid possessed a key to the room in case of emergencies.

Susie gave her an inscrutable look and wagged her tail three times.

"Excellent. You may choose anything you want for lunch today!" Audrey made a small fist of encouragement.

After reminding Susie of her duty, she entered the bathroom. The square bathtub measured three or four meters on each side. Clear water rippled gently inside it, releasing clouds of steam that gave the room a dreamlike quality.

Audrey cleared a rectangular table crowded with bottles, then returned to the bedroom and carried in the candles, offerings, and a white ceremonial robe.

Once everything was ready, she closed the bathroom door.

Audrey breathed a sigh of relief and picked up a translucent pale-blue bottle beside the four candles.

The cylindrical glass shimmered dreamily in the light. It contained the essential oil she had distilled from yesterday's mixture. As a longtime enthusiast of mysticism, Audrey had extensively studied such materials. She already possessed numerous extracts, floral essences, perfumes, essential oils, and varieties of incense that she had prepared herself. Consequently, she had been able to complete all the preliminary work in accordance with The Fool's instructions.

"Moon flowers, gold mint, slumber flowers, fingered citron, and rock rose... What an unusual combination," Audrey murmured. "Oh, before performing ritualistic magic, one must cleanse the body and calm the mind as a gesture of reverence toward the deity--or rather, the intended recipient."

After reviewing the entire process in her mind, she placed the ritual oil beside the bathtub and began removing the clothes she wore at home.

One piece of silk after another fell into the laundry basket. Audrey twisted her hair into a bun and tested the temperature with one hand. Then she carefully stepped into the tub and allowed herself to sink into the warm embrace of the water.

"Phew..."

She exhaled in contentment. Warmth spread through her entire body, leaving her extraordinarily relaxed.

I don't even want to move a finger...

Audrey forced herself to summon some energy. She reached for the pale-blue bottle and added several drops to the bathwater.

A wave of fresh fragrance drifted through the quiet bathroom. Audrey inhaled several times and nodded with satisfaction.

"Not bad. It smells wonderful.

"So soothing... so comfortable...

"I don't want to move at all. I only want to lie here quietly...

"Quietly... in silence... si... lence..."

After an indeterminate length of time, barking suddenly pierced the haze.

Audrey's eyes flew open. Still dazed, she looked around and discovered that Susie had somehow opened the door and entered. The golden retriever sat beside the bathtub, regarding her with exasperation.

Audrey rubbed the corners of her eyes and realized that the water had grown considerably cooler.

I-I fell asleep?

Susie stared at her without barking or wagging her tail.

"Haha... That bottle of ritual oil is certainly effective. Yes, remarkably effective!" Audrey explained with a dry laugh, forcing cheerfulness into her voice.

She stood, took a towel, and began drying herself.

"Susie, please continue standing guard. Don't let Annie or anyone else come in!"

Only after the golden retriever left did Audrey furtively stick out her tongue. She tossed the towel aside and put on the clean white robe.

With the bathroom door once again closed, she recalled the ritual she had memorized.

Audrey placed one candle at each corner of the table.

A loaf of white bread at the upper left. A bowl of Feynapotter noodles at the upper right. They smell delicious, though they've gone a little cold... No! This isn't the time to think about food. Paella at the lower left, and a Desi pie at the lower right...

She solemnly arranged the altar according to The Fool's instructions, shaking her head at herself twice during the process.

Once the preparations were complete, she lit all four candles. Then she picked up a silver knife and plunged it into a pile of coarse salt.

After reciting the sacred incantation in Hermes, Audrey raised the beautifully patterned knife and dipped it into a cup of clear water.

She focused her thoughts, withdrew the silver sacred blade, and used Cogitation to channel her spirituality through its tip.

Invisible energy flowed outward as she carried the knife around the altar. When she sensed that a complete wall of spirituality had formed, sealing the interior and excluding all impurities and distractions, she stopped.

Audrey maintained her Spectator state, preventing excitement and anticipation from interfering with the ritual.

She set down the silver knife, picked up the small bottle of pale-blue crystal, and placed a drop of oil on each candle.

Sizzle!

A faint fragrance spread through the room, and Audrey's body, mind, and spirit settled into tranquility.

She drew a breath, bowed her head reverently, and began chanting in Hermes.

"The Fool that doesn't belong to this era;

"The mysterious ruler above the gray fog;

"The King of Yellow and Black who wields good luck.

"I pray for your aid.

"I pray for your loving grace.

"I pray that you grant me a good dream.

"Moon flower, herb of the red moon, bestow your power upon my incantation!

"Fingered citron, herb of the sun, bestow your power upon my incantation!"

The instant Audrey completed the words and prepared to focus on her request through Cogitation, something stirred within the wall of spirituality. A dark-red star began revolving upon the back of her hand.

Her heart leaped. She hurriedly closed her eyes, calmed herself, and offered her plea with complete sincerity.

When the ritual ended, she looked around in wonder but found nothing else out of the ordinary.

"Is that all?" Audrey whispered with a slight frown.

...

The King of Yellow and Black who wields good luck... The Fool that doesn't belong to this era...

Inside the captain's cabin of the Blue Avenger, Alger Wilson sat in his storm robes, silently repeating the three descriptions he had heard the previous afternoon. He appeared to be searching them for some clue to The Fool's identity.

Eventually, he shook his head and rose in visible frustration, but made no attempt to perform the ritual.

Alger didn't feel entirely secure aboard the Blue Avenger, an ancient vessel dating back to the Tudor Dynasty. Although he controlled the ship, instinct told him that many secrets remained hidden within it--secrets as deep as those surrounding the Blood Emperor.

He intended to use the vessel to test The Fool's power, but he had no desire to perform unknown ritualistic magic aboard it.

After several minutes of consideration, Alger left the captain's cabin and stepped onto the deck.

"We'll reach the Rorsted Archipelago soon," he told the handful of sailors. "We'll anchor there for one day."

The sailors immediately cheered.

"Thank you, my lord!"

Because the ghost ship required no crew to sail, only a few sailors lived aboard. Supplies presented no difficulty, and they enjoyed fresh food and clean water. Nevertheless, day after day upon the open sea, surrounded by seemingly endless horizons, exhausted them both physically and mentally. The accumulated pressure felt like something they had to endure until they finally lost control.

The Rorsted Archipelago, by contrast, was a famous colony in the Sonia Sea, with thriving trade and every kind of entertainment.

"I can't wait!" One crewman gyrated his hips and gave a suggestive laugh whose meaning every man present understood.

...

In a public carriage bound for Zouteland Street, Klein was leisurely reading the newspaper when his body suddenly jolted.

He seemed to hear a distant, ethereal voice calling to him.

Formless murmurs echoed in his mind, and his forehead throbbed uncontrollably.

The unintelligible summons vanished as quickly as it had arrived. Within ten seconds, it was gone. Klein pinched his brow, enduring the lingering ache deep inside his head.

Are those the murmurs of unknown existences Old Neil warned me about? Is this a consequence of heightened spiritual perception?

Thoughts flashed through Klein's mind. Then he saw four black dots emerge on the back of his right hand, like tiny, inconspicuous moles.

The marks originating from the luck-enhancement ritual quickly sank beneath his skin, dimmed, and disappeared.

Klein stared in surprise as a new possibility occurred to him.

Did Justice or The Hanged Man perform the ritual I taught them?

Was my reasoning correct?

Did those three descriptions point precisely to me through the mysterious space above the gray fog?

But I'm nowhere near powerful enough. I couldn't make out the content of the request... I wonder whether the message has been stored above the gray fog.

Yes. I'll enter tonight and confirm it.

Agitated and uneasy, Klein quickly raised the newspaper to hide his face so the other passengers wouldn't see his changing expression.

Soon afterward, he reached Zouteland Street and entered the Blackthorn Security Company.

Before Klein could greet Rozanne, Captain Dunn Smith emerged from the interior. He carried a sheet of paper bearing a sketched portrait.

"Take a look at this internal arrest warrant. An exceptionally cruel and dangerous Beyonder has entered Tingen."

Dressed in his black trench coat but wearing no hat, Dunn swept his gaze over Klein and handed him the paper in passing.

The first thing Klein saw was the portrait.

It depicted a round-faced young man with an amiable, slightly bashful appearance. He looked no older than eighteen or nineteen.

"Tris. Suspected Beyonder, initially assessed as a Sequence 8 Instigator. The possibility of the Theosophy Order's involvement has not been ruled out. Responsible for the massacre aboard the Alfalfa... According to witness testimony, he traveled to Tingen after leaving Enmat Harbor. Current whereabouts unknown..."

Tris... The Alfalfa... So the massacre truly was the work of a Beyonder?

Klein immediately recalled yesterday afternoon's dream interpretation and Joyce Meyer's account.

"Captain, I know one of the witnesses. He may be particularly important."

"I know. Joyce Meyer." Dunn's gray eyes remained calm as he chuckled. "The Machinery Hivemind requested my assistance last night. I saw you in Joyce's dream. Numerous details allowed us to confirm that Tris caused the Alfalfa tragedy."

How disappointing, Captain... Fortunately, yesterday was my day off. At least you didn't catch me acting as a Seer during working hours.

Klein silently mocked the situation, narrowly escaping the horror of being caught slacking by his direct superior.

Instead, he asked, "Which pathway does Instigator belong to? And what sort of organization is the Theosophy Order?"

Had Tris incited the passengers to murder one another as a means of digesting his potion, or was the massacre a requirement for advancing to the next Sequence?

Dunn thought for several seconds.

"As it happens, it's time you learned the relevant information about Beyonders and secret organizations. Old Neil shouldn't keep you buried in historical records forever."

Captain, wasn't I recruited specifically to serve as the team's history expert?

Klein didn't dare point out the contradiction. He merely nodded solemnly.

"All right."

Chapter 65: Beyonder Information

Carrying a document signed by Dunn, Klein descended underground and turned toward the armory.

"Dunn is right. It's time you gained a proper understanding of the various Beyonder pathways and secret organizations."

Old Neil, dressed in his classical black robe, read the note without surprise and readily approved the captain's decision.

With a smile, he immediately added, "After all, you'll be accompanying me to the underground market tomorrow night."

"Tomorrow night?" Klein made no attempt to conceal his delighted surprise.

Old Neil nodded and sighed.

"I'm the sort of person who can't sleep while he owes a debt. I always want to settle it as quickly as possible."

That isn't how you behaved before. You waited until the last possible moment before using ritualistic magic to solve the problem... So I'm not the only one who procrastinates.

Wait, is "can't sleep while in debt" merely a polite way of saying you hate parting with the money needed to repay it?

Klein didn't expose him. Instead, he urged, "Mr. Neil, please retrieve the relevant documents from behind Chanis Gate."

The armory primarily held archaeological and historical records. It did contain documents concerning Beyonders and mysticism organizations, but only a small number, most of which covered basic knowledge.

Old Neil slowly sipped his freshly ground coffee and smacked his lips. Then, carrying the signed and stamped authorization, he left Klein to watch the armory in his absence.

Approximately ten minutes later, he returned with an enormous stack of documents.

"These may only be read here. You are not permitted to take them away," he warned as he placed them on the desk.

"Understood." Klein nodded firmly.

He reached for the stack and quickly leafed through it to gain an overview.

So detailed... Exactly what one would expect from the Nighthawks' internal archives--and from a Church with at least four thousand years of history.

The documents not only introduced the major secret organizations, but also cataloged many Sequence pathways. The information varied in completeness. Some entries listed only the potion name for a given Sequence, while others described its Beyonder abilities without identifying the potion. A number of entries were entirely blank.

Suppressing his excitement, Klein searched first for the pathway beginning with Seer.

He rapidly turned the pages until a familiar word appeared.

His delighted expression immediately stiffened.

The two Sequences following Seer--Sequences 8 and 7--had no potion names!

At least their abilities are recorded...

Klein quietly exhaled, calmed himself, and read the descriptions with great care.

"Sequence 8: Potion name unknown. The corresponding Beyonder excels at fighting through cunning and artifice and possesses an exceptionally crafty nature."

Fighting through cunning and artifice? This is Seer's next advancement? Why does that sound so strange? I'm not a Hunter... Am I supposed to become some kind of close-combat mage? And what does "crafty" imply? Greater intelligence, allowing me to deceive opponents more effectively?

Klein was baffled. He even began to suspect that the Nighthawks' information might be inaccurate.

Several case studies followed the description, but even after reading them repeatedly, he couldn't derive a satisfying explanation.

He lowered his gaze to the entry for Sequence 7.

"Potion name unknown. The corresponding Beyonder commands numerous spells that can be cast with exceptional speed, seamlessly combining physical skill with supernatural power."

Now that sounds right! That's what an advancement from Seer ought to be like.

Klein breathed a sigh of relief. After reviewing the associated cases, he moved on to the pathway's general description.

"This Sequence pathway was first controlled by the Zaratul family of the Solomon Empire. The family was not completely destroyed during the conflicts of the Fourth Epoch, and its name has occasionally surfaced throughout the history of the Fifth Epoch... Suspected connection to the ancient organization known as the Secret Order."

Zaratul?

Klein's eyes narrowed at the name.

He had seen it only yesterday afternoon in the surviving pages of Emperor Roselle's diary.

Roselle had learned about acting from a mysterious man named Zaratul!

Was it because of Zaratul that Emperor Roselle regretted not choosing Seer? That regret influenced me indirectly and led me to become a Seer myself. And now the knowledge of acting has returned to the Seer pathway...

It almost feels predestined.

Klein frowned, sensing that the situation wasn't as simple as it appeared.

Viewed as a chain of ordinary cause and effect, every link seemed reasonable. But in the realm of mysticism, apparent predestination often revealed deeper forces and created new questions.

And transmigration itself is incomprehensible... To make matters worse, the body I now inhabit attempted suicide because of a missing notebook connected to the Secret Order...

Klein considered numerous possibilities, but lacked the evidence needed to confirm any of them.

Phew...

After rereading the passage several times, he suppressed his speculation and continued through the records.

He searched next for the Sailor pathway and confirmed that it belonged to the Lord of Storms.

Despite dealing with an old rival the Nighthawks might not have confronted directly in two or three thousand years, their internal records were surprisingly thorough.

"Sequence 8: Folk of Rage. Ancient name: Guardian of the Storm. When enraged, the corresponding Beyonder can unleash several attacks exceeding ordinary human limits. Their strength and speed increase dramatically... Confronting one is like facing a storm...

"Sequence 7: Seafarer. Ancient name: Storm Priest. The corresponding Beyonder is also a scholar of astronomy and geography, possessing an intuitive grasp of magnetic fields, ocean currents, wind direction, and cloud formations... A vessel carrying a Seafarer will never become lost at sea...

"The ocean favors them, greatly enhancing every aspect of their abilities while at sea. They are friends of water and can move freely underwater for more than half an hour...

"They can also perform a limited number of water-related spells. Some arise from their innate abilities, while others are gifts bestowed by the Lord of Storms. Examples include..."

A Sequence 7 Seafarer is extremely powerful...

Klein nodded thoughtfully.

He suspected that The Hanged Man was either a Guardian of the Storm or a Seafarer. Given the signs that Alger had recently advanced, the latter seemed more likely.

That also suggested The Hanged Man was either a member of the Mandated Punishers or a pirate secretly recruited by the Church of the Lord of Storms.

Impressive. Very impressive...

Klein flipped back several pages and found the advancements of Spectator. Their descriptions matched exactly what The Hanged Man had told Justice.

Like a Seer, a Sequence 9 Spectator lacked direct means of combat. Instead, they observed their targets to gather information and infer hidden thoughts. With subtle influence and guidance, they could steer events toward the outcome they desired.

Sequence 8, Telepathist, represented an advancement of the Spectator's abilities. Their perception extended beyond surface details into a person's aura, Ether Body, and other mystical domains. By combining those observations, they could understand another person's thoughts with an accuracy resembling mind reading. It was exceedingly difficult to keep secrets from them.

Sequence 7 was Psychiatrist, formerly known as Psyche Analyst. Building upon the abilities of Sequence 8, they could begin influencing a target directly. They might treat insanity and other mental disorders--or drive a person mad and strip away their reason.

"A Beyonder who would be extremely difficult to detect..." Klein murmured after considering the implications.

Once he had reviewed the pathways connected to his fellow Gathering members, he turned to the Church of the God of Steam and Machinery, since Emperor Roselle had begun with its Savant Sequence.

"Sequence 9: Savant. The corresponding Beyonder embodies the belief that knowledge is power.
