Although they possess a general understanding of the supernatural, their expertise lies more heavily in such subjects as aqua regia, nitroglycerin, and complex systems of gears and bolts. They appear to know something about everything."

No wonder Emperor Roselle said the potion suited him perfectly and allowed him to exploit his advantages to the fullest...

Klein understood completely. He moved his gaze down the page.

After several case studies, the entry for Sequence 8 appeared.

"Archaeologist. They possess extensive knowledge of history, wilderness survival, and the forbidden lore of ancient ruins. Their bodies and abilities are strong enough to confront the dangers associated with such exploration..."

"Sequence 7: Appraiser. They can intuitively discern the powers and hidden problems of most extraordinary items, allowing them to use those objects while minimizing the danger..."

Klein's security clearance gave him access only to information through Sequence 7. The restriction frustrated him, but there was nothing he could do about it.

He could only hope that Backlund would soon send Sealed Artifact 2-049 to Tingen and determine whether Ray Bieber was a descendant of the Antigonus family.

That would give Klein an opportunity to become a formal Nighthawk and obtain a higher clearance level.

After composing himself, he continued reading the documents from beginning to end.

He learned that the Sequences following Corpse Collector were Gravedigger and Spirit Medium. The Mystery Pryer pathway's Sequence 8 entry contained neither a potion name nor a description; both fields were entirely blank. Sequence 7, however, had a name: Warlock.

That sounds impressive...

Klein slowly turned the page and found Apprentice and Marauder, the two pathways Emperor Roselle had continued to regret passing over. The Nighthawks' records for both ended at Sequence 8.

"Sequence 9: Apprentice. Their abilities are exceptionally strange. It can only be confirmed that this is the originating pathway of a mage. They are rarely trapped and almost impossible to contain, frequently escaping and passing through obstacles that should stop them...

"Sequence 8: Trickmaster. They command a wide variety of bizarre but relatively weak spells..."

"Sequence 9: Marauder. These Beyonders are difficult to distinguish from ordinary thieves and bandits. Their methods may be more sophisticated, but they don't steal wealth merely for survival or enjoyment. It is more akin to answering a calling...

"Sequence 8: Swindler. Beyonders of this kind have also appeared in investigations of fraud. They derive pleasure from deceiving others..."

They derive pleasure from deception... Isn't that another form of acting? If I had been given the choice, perhaps I would have selected Apprentice...

As Klein silently reflected, his eyes suddenly landed on the potion corresponding to Tris, the culprit behind the Alfalfa massacre.

Instigator.

Chapter 66: The Demoness Sect

"Sequence 8: Instigator. They excel at arousing the evil desires hidden within the human heart, intensifying disputes and inciting conflict until the result is a bloody massacre..."

The description isn't detailed enough. It appears the Nighthawks don't fully understand this potion's abilities... Still, it matches everything that happened aboard the Alfalfa.

Klein looked upward to read the corresponding Sequence 9 entry.

"Sequence 9: Assassin. They can briefly transform their bodies, becoming as light as feathers, while gaining vision comparable to that of an eagle. Every Assassin is skilled at concealing themselves in shadows. Their movements are exceptionally agile, and they can concentrate all their strength into a single attack..."

After reading the description, Klein was thoroughly confused.

Assassin... advances into Instigator?

That progression is every bit as strange as a Seer advancing into a class skilled at combat through cunning and artifice...

Some pathways advance in an entirely intuitive manner, such as Spectator. Yet others seem to violate all ordinary logic.

Well, perhaps that isn't entirely fair. Maybe I simply haven't discovered the hidden principle connecting them.

For example, both Assassins and Instigators bring catastrophe to others...

But I still can't make sense of Seer's advancement! Could it belong to the Gandalf school of development?[1] Learn a few support spells, then pour all the remaining attribute points into strength and combat technique?

After silently mocking the idea, Klein shook his head and turned to the section concerning the secret organization associated with Instigators: the Theosophy Order.

"Theosophy Order. A secret organization founded during the early Fifth Epoch, the current epoch. Its members believe that the mind is the foundation of the individual, while the physical body is a prison that confines it. Human evil, in their view, arises from the influence of the flesh.

"Through spirituality, one must acquire knowledge and gradually free the mind from the body. After enduring the trials of the stars, the mind will eventually separate from the material world and return to its purest and truest state, thereby attaining eternal redemption.

"Consequently, many extremist members of the Theosophy Order seek to destroy the bodies of others, a belief that has produced numerous massacres...

"The organization demonstrably controls two Sequence pathways. The first consists of Apprentice and Trickmaster, which are relatively common among its members. The second, beginning with Assassin and Instigator, appears far more rarely...

"There is currently no evidence proving that the Theosophy Order possesses formulas for Sequence 7 or above.

"The origins of the Theosophy Order remain unknown, but its two pathways suggest several possibilities. Apprentice and Trickmaster naturally call to mind the Abraham family of the Fourth Epoch's Tudor Dynasty. The Tamara family, which maintained a long-standing marriage alliance with the Abrahams, must also be considered.

"Assassin and Instigator, meanwhile, point toward the Demoness Sect."

The Abraham, Tamara, Antigonus, and Zaratul families. The Black Emperor of the Solomon Empire. The Blood Emperor of the Tudor Dynasty. The Trunsoest Empire, along with the Jacob and Amon families mentioned by The Hanged Man...

The history of the Fourth Epoch buried an astonishing number of secrets--and perhaps an equally astonishing number of truths.

Klein was struck again by how completely the era remained shrouded in fog.

Even the vague silhouettes visible through that fog made him shudder. He could almost imagine an age in which Beyonders flourished, where blood and strangeness danced together and horror joined distortion in a grand symphony.

He drew a silent breath and searched through the documents, but found no entry describing the Demoness Sect itself.

Looking up, he saw Old Neil struggling with filter paper and his freshly ground coffee.

"Mr. Neil, what sort of organization is the Demoness Sect? I can't find any information about it in these documents."

Old Neil took his time answering. Only after settling the dispute with his coffee did he chuckle.

"Your security clearance isn't high enough. Even with Dunn's authorization, you can't access the relevant records. In fact, much of that information may exist only at the Holy Cathedral rather than behind Tingen's Chanis Gate.

"Perhaps when you become a Nighthawk captain, you'll be sent to the Holy Cathedral for training and gain access to it.

"I don't know much about the Demoness Sect myself. I only know that its members worship the Primordial Demoness. They believe this concealed existence is the Creator's true successor. According to their doctrine, She was born from Chaos, the first being to emerge from the Creator's body, and She will ultimately bring an end to all things.

"Their Sequence pathway is related to this belief. To receive the Primordial Demoness's favor and draw closer to Her, every high-ranking member is female. That is why the organization is called the Demoness Sect.

"Anything beyond that lies outside the knowledge of an ordinary member like me. I have heard that the demonesses regard spreading catastrophe as their sacred mission."

Spreading catastrophe... That does match the common theme between Assassin and Instigator.

But Mr. Tris's prospects seem bleak. The later potions sound far better suited to women...

Klein gave a slight nod and resumed reading.

By the time he finished, he had discovered far more secret organizations than he had imagined. Upon reflection, however, their sheer number seemed perfectly reasonable. This world possessed thousands of years of history, including an age when Beyonder powers had been exceptionally active.

Based on the records, Klein divided the organizations into three broad categories according to when they had appeared.

The first consisted of ancient organizations originating in the Fourth Epoch. These included, but were not limited to, the Moses Ascetic Order, the Secret Order, and the Blood Sanctify Sect, whose members worshiped the Devil. The Demoness Sect was mentioned only in passing.

The second category contained organizations founded during the early Fifth Epoch. Among them were the Theosophy Order; the death-worshiping Numinous Episcopate; the Life School of Thought, which transmitted its teachings from master to apprentice; and the Rose School of Thought, notorious among Beyonders for its bloody sacrifices.

The third category comprised organizations that had emerged within the past century or two: the Aurora Order, the Iron and Blood Cross Order, Element Dawn, and the Psychology Alchemists, which Klein had learned about earlier.

There were also numerous smaller groups that had never caused any major incidents.

"Benson and Melissa could never imagine how dangerous this world truly is... And the danger isn't limited to war."

Klein shook his head with a bitter smile, neatly stacked the classified documents, and pushed them toward Old Neil.

At the same time, he silently added a prayer.

Please don't let my Tarot Club end up on that list...

Old Neil never suspected that the leader of a secret organization was sitting directly across from him. He chuckled, took the documents, and headed toward Chanis Gate.

Klein remained in the armory, wondering whether he should attempt to divine the location of Instigator Tris. He abandoned the idea in less than twenty seconds.

After all, he possessed only a rough description of Tris's appearance and couldn't even be certain the name was genuine. If Klein could locate someone using so little information, he wouldn't merely be a Seer--he would be a Prophet.

By the time Old Neil returned, Klein had organized his thoughts and resumed studying mysticism, focusing on mastering additional forms of ritualistic magic.

He spent the remainder of the day studying and reviewing. He did not participate in the joint operation to capture Tris, though he heard that the delivery of Sealed Artifact 2-049 from Backlund had been delayed for unspecified reasons. Its new arrival time remained uncertain.

Because the previous day's divinations had earned him nearly two soli, Klein spent ten pence on a two-liter barrel of Enmat beer for Benson on his way home. He also bought freshly baked lemon cakes for Melissa.

When Benson saw the little barrel, he considered his words before speaking.

"Klein, I know you care deeply about us, but this really isn't necessary. You don't need to keep spending money on such things."

Melissa stood beside him and nodded in agreement.

This must be the difference between our attitudes toward consumption...

Klein sighed inwardly, amused.

"Benson, Melissa, don't worry. I paid for these with additional compensation. I earn another two to four soli each week."

I can't exactly tell them I earned it by performing divinations for strangers...

"Your job is even better than I imagined," Benson said after a moment of objective assessment.

That's right. It even taught me how to divine...

Klein silently agreed and turned toward the kitchen.

Through the combined efforts of all three siblings, they soon prepared a sumptuous dinner.

After eating their fill, Klein, Benson, and Melissa slumped in the living room. It took quite some time before they recovered enough to clean up, converse, and return to their studies.

Once Benson and Melissa are asleep, I'll go above the gray fog and examine the ritual's results...

While reviewing his history textbook, Klein glanced at his siblings.

...

West Borough, Lower Iron Cross Street.

A three-story apartment building stood submerged in darkness, untouched by streetlamps or any other light.

Without warning, a figure leaped from a third-floor window. He landed as gently as a feather, making scarcely a sound.

He crouched and seemed to disappear, merging with the surrounding shadows until only the faintest outline remained.

Moving swiftly, the figure reached the harbor and approached an isolated corner containing nothing but stacked cargo.

He carefully surveyed the area and circled it twice before leaving the darkness and stepping into the open space.

His round, amiable face became visible.

It was Instigator Tris, the man who had single-handedly caused the Alfalfa tragedy.

"How did it feel?"

A mysterious figure in a black hooded robe emerged from the shadows. The hoarse voice unmistakably belonged to a woman.

Tris gave her a friendly, satisfied smile.

"Wonderful. It was a spectacle I had dreamed of and longed to witness.

"I believe I completed the assignment properly and made all the necessary preparations for advancement."

The black-robed woman gave an almost imperceptible nod.

"Very good. As promised, I'll give you the Sequence 7 formula and its three main ingredients. You must gather the remaining supplementary ingredients yourself."

"That won't be a problem," Tris replied, clearly prepared.

The mysterious woman raised her hand and passed him an object shaped like a book.

Its cover was made of ancient, mottled bronze, and a peculiar star-shaped lock adorned one edge.

Tris knew that the formula and ingredients were sealed inside. Excitement surged through him.

Struggling to remain composed, he curiously examined the potion name written in ancient Hermes upon the bronze cover.

"Witch!"

Tris exclaimed in disbelief.

Witch? I'm supposed to advance into a Witch? What kind of joke is this?

The mysterious woman covered her mouth and chuckled. It took her several moments to stop laughing.

"Weren't you always curious why every high-ranking member of our organization is a woman?

"That is your answer."

[1] Gandalf is a wizard and major character in J. R. R. Tolkien's novels The Hobbit and The Lord of the Rings.

Chapter 67: A Response

High above, the crimson moon hung silently in the darkness. Its light washed over Tingen, the City of Universities, as the streets gradually fell quiet.

Standing before his desk, Klein looked through the oriel window at the empty stretch of Daffodil Street. Somewhere in the distance, a carriage sped past without disturbing the stillness.

He picked up his silver pocket watch with its vine-leaf pattern and snapped it open. After checking the time, he closed the curtains, causing the yellow light of the gas lamp to reflect back into the bedroom.

Klein turned at an unhurried pace, locked the door, and shut off the gas valve.

Darkness immediately enveloped the room. Only a sliver of crimson moonlight slipped through the curtains, creating precisely the atmosphere in which late-night folktales ought to unfold.

He took out the silver knife he had requisitioned, imagined the sphere of light, and entered a partial state of Cogitation.

Following his previous practice, Klein focused his mind and allowed spirituality to flow from the blade's tip. The energy blended mysteriously with his surroundings, sealing the room within a spiritual wall.

He wanted to prevent any unusual disturbance from waking Benson or Melissa.

After setting down the knife, Klein took four counterclockwise steps. With each step, he recited one line of the incantation from Earth.

The familiar roars and murmurs flooded over him. Mania and pain assaulted his mind, but he struggled to maintain control and endured the most grueling and dangerous stage in a half-conscious state.

The endless gray-white fog appeared. Dark-red stars floated at varying distances, while the towering divine palace stood like the corpse of a giant. At first, nothing seemed different. The silence and antiquity of countless millennia pressed in upon him.

No--something has changed!

Klein's attention fixed upon a nearby dark-red star.

It was the star representing Justice.

Its deep-red light pulsed continuously, neither violently nor faintly.

With great care, Klein extended his spirituality toward it.

The instant they touched, a humming vibration filled his head. A blurry, distorted scene appeared before him, accompanied by overlapping, illusory prayers.

"The Fool that doesn't belong to this era;

"The mysterious ruler above the gray fog;

"The King of Yellow and Black who wields good luck.

"I pray for your aid.

"I pray for your loving grace.

"I pray that you grant me a good dream.

"I pray that you grant me a good dream.

"I pray that you grant me a good dream..."

The woman's voice echoed again and again in intermittent waves. Klein's thoughts grew irritable and chaotic, as though he had just fallen asleep only for someone in the apartment above to begin pounding on the floor.

He suppressed his agitation and used Cogitation to restore his calm. Then he carefully studied the hazy image before him.

A young woman in a white robe stood before four flickering flames. Beautiful golden hair framed her bowed head as she chanted without end.

Despite the distorted image, Klein could just recognize Miss Justice.

At last, he had confirmation: the ritual and honorific name he had designed could point precisely toward the gray fog--and toward him.

The success gave him the profound satisfaction of creating something from nothing.

I won't go so far as to praise my own brilliance...

Klein's mood improved enough that the incessant prayer buzzing around his ears became tolerable.

With a thought, he attempted to form a response and transmit it through the mysterious connection to the dark-red star.

"I am aware."

Gray fog spread before him. In its deepest reaches sat a blurry, distorted figure.

Dark-red light revolved where the figure's eyes should have been, while a voice echoed repeatedly through the vast and empty world.

"I am aware."

"I am aware."

"I am aware..."

...

Audrey Hall jolted awake and sat upright, clutching the blanket around herself. Her mind was filled with the images from her dream.

She knew with absolute certainty that she had dreamed of The Fool--the mysterious being who dwelled above the gray fog.

"Was that a response to my prayer this morning?"

Audrey quickly entered her Spectator state, calmed herself, and began analyzing the experience.

She didn't understand why The Fool had waited until night rather than responding immediately. Nevertheless, the fact that a ritual based on only a few lines of incantation had actually worked left her deeply shaken.

She had prayed to the Evernight Goddess many times in the past, but had never once received a direct response!

Even if Mr. Fool isn't truly a god, he cannot be far from that level...

Audrey inhaled slowly, then released the breath.

Since this was a powerful existence she had no means of resisting, she quickly set aside her worries and turned her attention to what she should do next.

First, I must completely digest the Spectator potion... My acting has been quite effective so far.

Second, I need to locate the Psychology Alchemists.

Third, I should try to obtain the Telepathist formula from Mr. Fool--or find another source of information about the Psychology Alchemists.

But every godlike existence should control a complete Sequence pathway of its own. Mr. Fool may not know formulas from other pathways... And a recently founded Beyonder organization like the Psychology Alchemists may be too insignificant to attract his notice.

...

Once the connection ended, Klein remained in the seat of honor at the bronze table, feeling rather pleased.

Completely shrouded in gray fog, he leaned back and raised a fist to his mouth while reviewing the experiment.

He was the only living presence in the entire gray-fog realm. Apart from him, there was absolute silence.

It appears I can transmit information, but I can't channel this place's power outward... In that case, my plan to exploit the mechanism won't work.

Klein continued pressing his fist against his mouth as he reached a conclusion.

If the incantation and ritual proved effective, he had intended to bind his physical body to the gray-fog world in the same manner. He hoped that such a connection would allow him to leverage all the power contained within this mysterious realm.

He might then pray to himself and use that circular process to bypass the limitations, mysteries, and dangers preventing him from fully employing the gray fog.

For example, he could perform a ritual in the physical world and ask "himself" to grant a spell. Afterward, he would enter the space above the fog, answer his own request, and bestow the desired power.

It seems I was far too optimistic... My understanding and control of this realm are nowhere near sufficient.

Klein shook his head self-deprecatingly and prepared to leave.

At that moment, the dark-red star representing The Hanged Man began to pulse, and a distant, formless voice spread through the fog.

Did I happen to arrive just as The Hanged Man performed his ritual?

Klein nodded thoughtfully. Still seated at the head of the long bronze table, he reached out and touched the star.

His spirituality spread into the pulsating crimson light.

At once, he heard The Hanged Man's deep, repeated prayers and saw another indistinct scene.

The Hanged Man stood before four columns of flame, dressed in a pure-black robe. A wall of spirituality enclosed the area, isolating him from all outside interference.

Klein didn't respond immediately. He simply watched and listened.

"The King of Yellow and Black who wields good luck;

"I pray for your aid."

After completing his prayer, The Hanged Man waited for a while. When no response came, he began dismantling the spiritual wall, extinguishing the candles, and clearing the altar.

Finally, he swept one outstretched hand across the surface. Watery light spread outward, leaving the altar table perfectly clean.

A water spell... A gift of the Storm. The Hanged Man is indeed at least a Seafarer.

Klein nodded slightly. Just before the scene disappeared, he formed a response and sent it through the dark-red star.

...

Alger Wilson was staying in the City of Generosity in the Rorsted Archipelago.

Rather than accompany his sailors to the famous Red Theater, he had remained in his hotel room, sealed the doors and windows, and attempted the ritual described by The Fool.

After completing the prayer with practiced ease, Alger waited patiently, but received no response.

"The experiment doesn't appear to have been very successful... Mr. Fool may need to revise the method."

He felt relieved, yet faintly disappointed.

Once everything had been put away, Alger decided to go downstairs for a bottle of Lanti Proof, a strong liquor that helped a Folk of Rage unleash his abilities. Mandated Punishers of the Lord of Storms were particularly fond of it.

He opened the door and was about to step into the hall when his vision blurred.

Gray fog billowed through the corridor. In its deepest reaches, a hazy human figure sat as though upon a lofty throne.

"I am aware."

The familiar, profound voice echoed around Alger, freezing him in place as a dull ache throbbed inside his head.

His eyes suddenly darkened. When he looked around, nothing had changed. The same floorboards creaked beneath him, the same old wall-mounted candleholders lined the passage, and the corridor remained equally filthy.

I am aware...

The voice continued reverberating beside his ears.

Alger's expression grew solemn. He struck his chest lightly with one fist, but offered no words of reverence to the Lord of Storms.

After a long silence, his face returned to normal, though his gaze had grown noticeably deeper.

...

Klein didn't linger above the gray fog. Once the lingering voices subsided, he wrapped himself in spirituality and plunged through the fog toward the material world.

Lights streaked past him like scenes from a film played dozens of times faster than normal. Just as dizziness began to overwhelm him, the familiar outlines of his desk and bookshelf appeared, along with curtains admitting faint crimson moonlight.

Klein picked up the silver knife and dismantled the spiritual wall. A sudden gust swept the door open and rushed into the corridor.

Only after hearing no movement from Benson's or Melissa's room did he relax completely.

This luck-enhancement ritual really is indispensable for journeys like this.[1] Concealed and mysterious...

Klein silently sighed, closed the door again, and walked toward his bed.

Tomorrow, he would accompany Old Neil to the underground market for Beyonder materials.

[1] A reference to a line from Stephen Chow's film Flirting Scholar, commonly used as a Chinese internet joke.

Chapter 68: Monster

At dusk, the setting sun cast long shadows behind the passing horses and carriages.

After telling Benson and Melissa that he would be eating dinner at the Blackthorn Security Company, Klein boarded a public carriage with Old Neil and headed toward the harbor.

He had deliberately worn his inexpensive formal clothes. The harbor was a complicated place where a fight could easily break out, and if the tuxedo he cared for so meticulously were damaged, he might genuinely weep.

By the time the sunlight had turned fiery red, the carriage came to a stop.

Old Neil wore his usual classical black robe and a rounded black felt hat. Ignoring the looks of the other passengers, he crossed the street diagonally toward the Evil Dragon Bar.

Although the bar was still some distance away and its heavy doors were tightly shut, Klein could hear wave after wave of ecstatic shouting from within, as though a crowd were cheering for a hero.

As he drew closer, his spiritual perception suddenly stirred.

Klein turned toward the warehouse opposite the bar and saw a stocky uniformed man standing in a concealed corner of the roof.

The man carried a bulky gray-white mechanical pack on his back and held a thick rifle connected to it by several tubes.

"A steam-powered rifle?" Klein muttered in surprise. He looked toward Old Neil. "This bar can obtain military weapons like that?"

The weapon was under strict military control.

Although it used refined phlogiston,[1] the steam pack remained astonishingly large and heavy. Only a genuine warrior of blood and iron could bear its weight. The rifle possessed tremendous muzzle velocity and destructive power; fitted with an appropriate scope, it was nearly equivalent to a crude sniper rifle.

"What?" Old Neil squinted in the indicated direction and appeared equally confused. "Did something happen here?"

Something happened?

Klein surveyed the surroundings and spotted several more men armed with repeating rifles, apparently searching the area.

Old Neil approached the entrance and addressed the brawny guard outside.

"What happened?"

The guard clearly knew him and gave a bitter smile.

"The bar was nearly demolished earlier.

"Apparently, a wanted criminal came here to purchase materials and someone recognized him. Then all this happened. Oh Lord, what did that man do to deserve a response like this? My knees nearly gave out when I saw all those guns--worse than after an entire night with Ginger Sunny!"

He obviously knew neither the wanted man's identity nor the fact that several of the people purchasing materials in the market were Beyonders.

"A wanted criminal? Do you know his name?" Old Neil asked with interest.

"Tris, I think. Something like that," the guard replied uncertainly.

Instigator Tris?

Klein nodded as the situation became clear.

Tris didn't know Joyce Meyer had begun to suspect him. He had therefore strolled into the market to purchase materials without taking precautions. An informant belonging to either the Machinery Hivemind or the Nighthawks must have recognized him, leading to a violent confrontation.

"Did they capture him?" Klein asked, tapping his black, silver-inlaid cane.

Judging by all the searching, probably not...

The brawny man shook his head and gestured with his chin toward the warehouse roof.

"He escaped before those terrifying fellows arrived. Bloody hell, I've never seen anyone run so fast!"

You've never witnessed the true abilities of an Assassin. If you had, you might have been taken somewhere indescribable for a thorough reeducation...

Old Neil changed the subject.

"Is the market still open?"

"It just reopened," the guard confirmed.

"Excellent."

Old Neil quickened his pace, reached out, and pushed open the heavy door.

Klein followed close behind. The suffocating heat and overwhelming smell of alcohol nearly made him faint.

A boxing ring occupied the center of the Evil Dragon Bar. Two shirtless men were locked in a furious brawl, surrounded by dozens of customers who shouted encouragement and obscenities in support of their chosen fighter.

Old Neil paid them no attention. He led Klein around the ring and into a billiard room at the rear.

Two men stood inside holding cues and chatting casually. The instant Old Neil entered, both fell silent.

After confirming his identity, they stepped aside and allowed Old Neil and Klein through the concealed door behind them.

They passed through several rooms before the space suddenly opened up. Klein found himself in a chamber roughly the size of a university lecture hall.

Vendors had established makeshift stalls covered with bottles, jars, and assorted containers. Customers wandered among them, examining goods, comparing prices, and conversing in low voices.

"They pay Swain five percent of their profits," Old Neil explained conversationally. "He's the owner of the Evil Dragon Bar, a former captain of a Mandated Punisher squad, and older than I am. These days, he seems determined to drink himself to death."

Klein considered the arrangement and offered an honest assessment.

"A highly profitable business."

After all, Swain's only expenses were the premises and its protection.

"If you find something you want but don't have enough money, you can borrow from Swain. Of course, his interest rates are exceptionally high..." Old Neil's voice trailed off as he ground his teeth.

Just like a casino. They even provide usurious loans...

Holding his cane, Klein looked around curiously.

"Is Mr. Swain a Seafarer?"

A captain of a Mandated Punisher squad was likely a Sequence 7 Beyonder.

"No. He's only a Folk of Rage. Tingen isn't a coastal city, so the Church of the Evernight Goddess is far more powerful here than the Church of the Lord of Storms," Old Neil replied with a scoff. "Swain once had the opportunity to become a Seafarer, but he feared losing control and chose not to advance."

Klein was about to ask whether the bar's owner had previously come close to losing control when he sensed something strange to his left.

It was as though something hidden nearby were murmuring and recounting an endless tale.

Klein turned and saw a pale young man dressed in an old linen shirt and the blue trousers commonly worn by laborers. His eyes looked listless yet faintly insane, and he muttered continuously to himself.

"His spiritual perception is extremely strong... or perhaps distorted?" Klein whispered with a frown.

The young man's spiritual perception had actually triggered Klein's own.

Generally, when one person's spiritual perception detected another's, the interaction couldn't be completely concealed. But the "other person" in such cases normally had to be a Spirit Medium actively using their abilities, or a powerful individual with similar traits. A Beyonder at Klein's level should have found it difficult to notice unless the other party possessed extraordinarily heightened perception or suffered from some abnormal distortion.

Their gazes met.

The pale young man with disheveled black hair began walking toward Klein, looking half asleep and half insane.

He stopped directly in front of him and stared.

Then he burst into laughter.

"Haha! The smell of death... death--ah!"

Before he could finish, he screamed in agony. His eyes squeezed shut as blood-colored liquid streamed from them.

"Ah! Damn it!"

The young man clutched his eyes, wrapped his arms around his head, and writhed on the floor. After a while, he finally grew still and lay there panting.

Throughout the entire episode, not a single customer or vendor looked in their direction.

Klein pressed down his half top hat and stared at Old Neil, his mouth hanging open. His expression conveyed both shock and an urgent request for explanation.

"Ignore him. That's Ademisaul, an orphan everyone calls 'Monster.' He was born with exceptionally high spiritual perception and has always been able to see things he shouldn't and hear voices no one should hear. That's why he constantly raves and frequently injures himself," Old Neil explained with a shake of his head.

Could he tell that this body was once dead?

Klein frowned and lowered his voice.

"Have the Nighthawks, Mandated Punishers, or Machinery Hivemind never considered recruiting him?"

"No. None of us controls the Sequence potion suited to him," Old Neil replied with a sigh.

Right. He was effectively born halfway into a Sequence...

Klein asked curiously, "Which pathway suits him?"

"The appropriate Sequence 9 is called Monster. That's where his nickname came from. Unfortunately, only the Life School of Thought controls the beginning of that pathway," Old Neil answered softly.

He kept their conversation low enough that the surrounding mysticism enthusiasts couldn't overhear the confidential information.

The Life School of Thought?

Klein recalled the records he had recently read.

The secret organization had appeared near the beginning of the current epoch. Its precise origins remained unknown, and it passed down its teachings primarily from master to apprentice.

Little was known about its doctrine. Klein only remembered that the organization divided existence into three layers: the world of absolute rationality, also called the world of absolute truth; the spirit world; and the material world.

Rumor claimed that the organization had once produced a Soothsayer... Wouldn't that correspond to the Seer pathway? This is all terribly confusing.

Klein shook his head and watched Ademisaul struggle to his feet before wandering toward another corner.

After reorganizing his thoughts, he followed Old Neil past stall after stall. They displayed plants such as moon flowers, fingered citron, and night vanilla, along with minerals and gemstones including silver, topaz, and ruby.

"They really do have everything..." Klein murmured.

Mysticism enthusiasts of every age and gender moved around him, stopping occasionally to inspect an item, identify a material, or negotiate with a vendor. The market possessed a lively, bustling atmosphere.

"Look around on your own. I'm going to settle my debt." Old Neil pointed toward one of two rooms at the far end.

"All right." Klein nodded without hesitation.

He strolled onward with his black cane and stopped before a stall selling handmade charms. For a while, he examined them carefully.

Just as Klein prepared to question the seller, he heard someone at the stall behind him ask, "Is this powder made from ground cow-teeth paeonol?"

Cow-teeth paeonol? Isn't that one of the supplementary ingredients for the Spectator potion?

Klein turned to look at the customer.

Justice had repeated the formula several times, leaving every ingredient exceptionally clear in his memory.

[1] Phlogiston was once believed by eighteenth-century chemists to be a substance contained in combustible materials and released during burning.

Chapter 69: Protection Amulet

Klein turned and saw the person asking about the cow-teeth paeonol.

The man stood less than a meter away. He wore a black suit with a matching half top hat, carried a silver-adorned cane, and had gold-rimmed spectacles perched on his nose. His pale-yellow sideburns and composed manner gave him the air of a refined gentleman.

"Yes. Do you need it? This tin costs three soli," replied the stall owner, whose long black robe made his interest in mysticism unmistakable.

The bespectacled man considered the price.

"Could you lower it? I still need several other ingredients, including this bottle of white-edged sunflower petals."

After a few seconds of thought, the vendor reluctantly said, "Two soli and six pence. I doubt you'll find it any cheaper elsewhere."

Seeing that the gentleman was purchasing other ingredients as well, Klein felt that he had probably been overthinking the coincidence.

Nevertheless, out of caution, he tapped his glabella twice and examined the man with Spiritual Vision.

Nothing unusual. He's in excellent health, and his emotional state appears normal. Keep it up, sir...

Klein withdrew his gaze and returned his attention to the stall of handmade amulets.

The items were arranged neatly before him. Some had been fashioned from pure silver, others from steel or gold.

Only a handful emitted even a faint spiritual aura: crimson, pale white, or golden.

That meant a few of them contained traces of spirituality and would possess at least some effect.

After studying them carefully, Klein concluded that the craftsman had a basic foundation in mysticism.

He had correctly matched the various sources of power with their corresponding incantations and had chosen appropriate materials for each source.

Naturally, an ordinary mysticism enthusiast was bound to make mistakes. The vendor didn't fully understand how incantations were constructed. One couldn't simply translate the words of a prayer into Hermes and expect them to work; an incantation had to follow a particular form governed by unique rules.

The craftsman had also made errors of varying severity when selecting and arranging the symbols corresponding to each source of power. That explained why only two or three of the dozens of amulets displayed any spiritual light.

As for how effective those few might be, Klein could only say they were better than nothing.

To create an amulet with an obvious supernatural effect, the craftsman needed to channel spirituality through the engraving tool while carving the incantation and symbols.

For an even stronger result, the process had to be supplemented with ritualistic magic.

Neither technique was within the reach of an ordinary person.

Klein tapped his glabella twice to deactivate Spiritual Vision, then pointed his black cane toward the upper-left corner of the stall.

"How much for those two?"

He wasn't indicating the finished amulets with faint spiritual auras, but two unfinished pieces. Apart from their exterior designs, neither had been engraved with an incantation or symbol.

Klein had no reason to purchase amulets with negligible effects. He intended to turn the unfinished pieces into genuine protective charms himself.

Hmm... I'll make one for Benson and one for Melissa, something that can protect them from danger. As for mine, I can requisition the necessary materials from the Nighthawks.

Good grief, Old Neil must be influencing me. I don't even feel guilty about that anymore...

Klein's thoughts wandered as the stall owner picked up the two unfinished silver pieces.

The first was long and narrow, with a hollow center surrounded by delicate patterns resembling an angel's feathers. The workmanship was intricate and beautiful. The second was simple and almost entirely undecorated, bearing only a vertical line representing night and a circle symbolizing the crimson moon.

Klein, who placed considerable importance on appearance, liked both immediately.

"Six soli for this one," the taciturn middle-aged vendor said, pointing to the elaborate piece.

After a pause, he rubbed the simpler one with his thumb.

"Five soli and three pence for this."

"That's far too expensive. Neither is even a completed amulet."

Living with Benson and Melissa had gradually taught Klein the value of bargaining.

After a vigorous exchange, he purchased the elaborate silver piece for five soli and six pence, and the simpler one for four soli and nine pence.

For now, they can only be called silver ornaments...

The total of ten soli and three pence came from the reimbursement he had received for his Divination Club membership.

Klein slipped the two pieces into his pocket and was preparing to move to the next stall when a gentle voice addressed him.

"Sir, why didn't you buy one of the finished amulets?"

He turned and found a teenage girl standing nearby. She appeared to be about fifteen and wore a lacy yellow dress. In one hand, she held a veiled hat decorated with ribbon.

"Because I intend to make the amulets myself. As you know, every enthusiast of mysticism dreams of doing so," Klein replied carefully.

He didn't want the stall owner to think he was attempting to steal business, though he had briefly considered using his new skills to earn some extra money.

The girl had naturally curly brown hair and a round, endearing face still softened by baby fat. She looked at Klein with sincere, pale-blue eyes.

"Could I ask your advice about choosing an amulet? A friend introduced me to this market. I've visited several times and have become deeply interested in mysticism, but I still know very little.

"That same friend will turn sixteen soon, and I want to give her an amulet as a present. I didn't bring her because I want it to be a surprise. She explained some of the important principles to me before, but I can't remember most of them."

Klein offered her a gentlemanly smile.

"What sort of protection do you want the amulet to provide? Should it avert misfortune, prevent illness, or bring good luck? Different purposes draw upon different sources of power, which means appealing to different gods. Each god corresponds to particular constellations and materials.

"For example, an incantation meant to avert misfortune belongs to the domain of the Empress of Misfortune and Horror--the Evernight Goddess. Every mysticism enthusiast knows that the moon symbolizes the Goddess, and the corresponding metal is pure silver.

"Therefore, if you wish to ward off misfortune, your best choice is a pure-silver amulet engraved with the appropriate incantation."

One must also ensure that the language and structure of the incantation are correct. The Path Number, the spell's nature, the positions of the symbols associated with the Empress of Misfortune and Horror--all of it has to be accurate...

But that's far too complicated, and there is no need to explain it all to her.

The girl's eyes sparkled, though she still seemed uncertain.

"Can a follower of the Goddess wear an amulet belonging to another god?"

"That isn't a problem. The gods don't concern themselves with such minor matters," Klein reassured her.

The wearer faced no difficulty, but the person crafting the amulet had to exercise caution. If a believer in the Lord of Storms attempted to create an amulet invoking the Eternal Blazing Sun, the response would most likely be hostile.

Of course, that applied only to genuine amulets produced with ritualistic magic. A craftsman making an ordinary ornament had little to worry about.

The girl sighed in relief.

"I want to give her an amulet for good health. Which deity should it invoke? The Eternal Blazing Sun, Earth Mother, or the God of Knowledge and Wisdom?"

"Either the Eternal Blazing Sun or Earth Mother would be suitable. The former corresponds to the sun, while the latter is represented by the Brown Planet," Klein explained with a smile. "Gold is the metal of the sun, while lead symbolizes the Brown Planet. I would recommend the sun, though I don't know whether you brought enough money."

He recommended it because one of the three amulets possessing a rudimentary spiritual glow was a health charm belonging to the sun's domain.

"Isn't this..."

The girl stopped before finishing. She glanced warily at the vendor, who waited in silence.

After some thought, she asked, "Once I've chosen the material, how can I identify the proper incantation and symbols?"

"Do you know Hermes?" Klein asked in return.

"I've only just begun learning it," she admitted with some embarrassment.

"Then allow me to choose for you."

Klein pointed toward the golden health amulet.

"This one is properly made. Both the incantation and its corresponding symbols are correct."

The girl lifted the hem of her dress and crouched beside the stall. She picked up the health amulet, whose edges were decorated with rays of sunlight. As she held it, she seemed to feel a nourishing warmth that left her completely at ease.

"Thank you. Thank you very much."

She rose and curtsied gratefully.

Klein laughed.

"I'll leave the price to you and the vendor. I have other matters to attend to."

As he spoke, he glanced at the stall owner and noticed the man's peculiar expression, as though he were deciding whether Klein deserved a share of the profit.

Smiling, Klein left them to it and continued exploring the underground market. He didn't encounter any genuine Beyonder ingredients.

By then, Old Neil had finished paying his debt and was carrying a dark wooden box.

Noticing Klein's puzzled look, he pointed toward the other room at the far end.

"Anyone buying or selling Beyonder ingredients conducts the transaction in there. No one wants the entire market knowing which extraordinary materials they're purchasing."

"I understand." Klein nodded thoughtfully.

He had no reason to enter the room for now, so he joined Old Neil and headed toward the market's exit.

"How much for these elf flowers?"

The question suddenly reached Klein's ears.

Elf flowers... Those are also supplementary ingredients for the Spectator potion.

He glanced sideways and once again saw the refined gentleman with gold-rimmed spectacles.

"Is something wrong?" Old Neil asked curiously.

"Nothing important." Klein looked away.

Although he was a provisional Nighthawk, he didn't believe every independent Beyonder had to be absorbed by a Church or imprisoned. Each situation ought to be judged on its own merits. A Spectator posed little direct danger to society or the kingdom, and the risk of losing control at Sequence 9 was relatively low.

After leaving the Evil Dragon Bar, Klein and Old Neil boarded a public carriage out of the harbor. They parted ways in North Borough and continued toward their respective homes.

Klein's carriage entered Daffodil Street and stopped beside the road. As he prepared to get off, he saw a young woman in a gray-white dress waiting to board.

She had smooth black hair, a slightly round face, narrow eyes, and otherwise unremarkable features. Taken together, however, they gave her a sweet and gentle appearance.

It wasn't her beauty that attracted Klein's attention.

Her body was trembling slightly in an unnatural way.

"Miss, are you all right?" he asked with concern.

The young woman abruptly shook her head.

"No--I-I mean, I'm only tired."

The passengers behind Klein urged him to move, leaving him no choice but to step off the carriage.

Once his feet touched the road, he returned his attention to the young woman. He tapped his glabella twice, intending to determine whether she was truly well.

If she appeared to be on the verge of a serious medical attack, he planned to send her to a hospital.

As Spiritual Vision activated, the colors of the surrounding auras emerged.

Klein turned to examine the sweet, gentle-looking woman.

Chapter 70: The Arrival of 2-049

Clip-clop, clip-clop.

The horses lengthened their stride, and the carriage wheels began to turn.

Klein activated Spiritual Vision and spun around, hoping to examine the sweet-looking young woman. He was too late. Brown silhouettes swept across his field of view, blocking her from sight.

The other passengers had already boarded. The carriage door closed tightly, and the vehicle gradually pulled away.

Twenty or thirty people stood crowded together inside, their energy fields overlapping and obscuring one another. Through Spiritual Vision, the interior became an explosion of mingled colors, making it impossible for Klein to distinguish any individual aura.

He quietly shook his head and tapped his glabella to deactivate the ability.

He had only intended to offer help because he happened to notice something unusual. Now that the opportunity had passed and the situation remained unclear, there was no reason to dwell on it or delay his own affairs.

Bathed in crimson moonlight, Klein strolled home through the still-bustling Daffodil Street.

When he entered, he found Melissa seated at the dining table, doing her homework beneath the bright gas lamp. She bit the end of her fountain pen and frowned in concentration.

"Where's Benson?" Klein asked casually.

"Ah..." Melissa looked up and stared blankly for several seconds before answering. "He spent today visiting several boroughs and came home drenched in sweat. He's taking a long bath."

"I see." Klein chuckled.

Then he noticed that she was wearing a dress he had never seen before.

It was entirely beige, with fashionable engageantes at the sleeves and narrow frills around the collar and bodice. Otherwise, the design was simple and suited for everyday wear, perfectly accentuating the youth of a girl of sixteen or seventeen.

"Is that your new dress?" Klein asked with a smile.

He and Benson had insisted that she buy one.

Melissa gave a short affirmative reply.

"I just collected it from Mrs. Rochelle. Since I'll have to wash it eventually, I thought I might as well try it on first."

Klein looked puzzled.

"Mrs. Rochelle?"

Wasn't she one of our former neighbors?

Melissa nodded and explained with complete seriousness.

"Mrs. Rochelle is actually a seamstress, but she's had terrible luck and has been forced to mend and sew clothes for people from her home. Her life is very difficult. I knew she was skilled, and her prices are lower than those of a dress shop. She could also tailor it precisely to my measurements.

"This dress cost only nine soli and five pence, and she finished it within a few days. A similar design at Harrods Department Store would cost one and a half pounds!"

What a frugal girl... Though I know at least half the reason was that you felt sorry for Mrs. Rochelle.

Klein didn't criticize Melissa for making the arrangement herself. Instead, he asked with a smile, "When did you visit Harrods?"

The department store stood on Howes Street, near the Divination Club, and catered primarily to the middle class.

"..."

Melissa was momentarily speechless. After a long pause, she said, "Selena and Elizabeth insisted that I accompany them. I actually--well, I prefer gears. I like places filled with steam and machinery. Yes."

"There's nothing wrong with a girl occasionally enjoying a department store," Klein reassured her with a laugh.

After chatting for a while, he hurried upstairs, eager to wash away the unpleasant mixture of odors clinging to him from the bar.

Just as he was about to enter his bedroom for a change of clothes, he heard movement from the bathroom near the balcony.

A few seconds later, Benson emerged while rubbing his slowly receding hairline with a towel.

"Well? Did you compliment Melissa's new dress?" He glanced at Klein and smiled.

"I suppose I forgot. I only asked where she had it made," Klein admitted after thinking.

Benson immediately laughed and shook his head.

"What a disappointing elder brother. When Melissa brought the dress home, she could hardly bear to put it down. She rushed through cooking and washing the dishes, changed into it immediately, and hasn't taken it off since."

But didn't she say she planned to change after bathing so she could wash and starch it at the same time?

Klein instinctively recalled Melissa's explanation.

"Tsk." Benson sighed. "The weather has been scorching these past few days. She spent a long time working in the kitchen, so surely she would have felt more comfortable doing her homework after taking a bath."

That's true...

Understanding dawned on Klein, and he exchanged a knowing smile with his brother.

So that's how it is, Melissa. There is nothing wrong with caring about your appearance. You don't need to invent excuses...

The corners of his mouth rose. He gently shook his head and entered his bedroom.

While bathing, Klein faintly heard a knock downstairs.

Who could that be?

The worker who collects money from the gas meter only visits once every two weeks.

Could it be Mrs. Shaud from next door? Unlikely. She supposedly observes middle-class etiquette with great precision and would never call at an inappropriate hour.

Still puzzled, Klein dried himself, put on an old but comfortable shirt and trousers, and went downstairs.

He saw no strangers.

"Was someone at the door just now?"

Benson looked up from his newspaper with a smile.

"It was Bitsch Mountbatten, one of the police officers responsible for Iron Cross Street. He asked whether we'd seen an eighteen- or nineteen-year-old young man with a round face. He even showed us a sketch.

"Unfortunately, neither of us recognized him, or we might have earned a reward. What about you?"

"No, I haven't seen him." Klein already understood what had happened.

Instigator Tris had escaped from the Evil Dragon Bar in the harbor and fled toward the area around Lower Iron Cross Street and Daffodil Street. The police were therefore conducting a door-to-door search.

The fact that they had resorted to such measures made one thing obvious: the operation to capture the Instigator had failed completely.

Klein had no intention of involving himself. He had not yet begun formal combat training and possessed only basic shooting skills. Trying to confront a natural Assassin would be treating his own life as a joke.

Even so, he slept poorly that night. He kept imagining the Instigator breaking into their home to hide and causing another massacre.

Fortunately, Daffodil Street remained peaceful until morning, when sunlight dispersed the lingering fog.

Relieved, Klein changed into formal clothes, put on his top hat, picked up his cane, and traveled to Zouteland Street.

He greeted Rozanne in the reception hall.

"Good morning, Klein," she replied brightly. Then she lowered her voice. "I heard the enormous operation last night failed."

"The attempt to capture Instigator Tris?" Klein asked curiously.

"Yes!" Rozanne nodded emphatically and glanced toward the partition. "Apparently, an informant working for the Mandated Punishers discovered the Instigator at the harbor. They intended to wait until more Beyonders and another police Special Operations unit arrived, then capture him in a single strike without alarming ordinary people.

"Unfortunately, the Instigator was exceptionally alert. He sensed something wrong and broke through the encirclement before they were ready, successfully escaping."

"At times like that, they need a Beyonder skilled at tracking--someone like me," Klein joked.

"There was no shortage of trackers," Dunn Smith's voice said abruptly.

Rozanne spun around. The captain, dressed in his black trench coat, leaned against the frame of the partition and regarded her with his deep gray eyes.

She hurriedly covered her mouth with both hands and shook her head repeatedly in a futile display of innocence.

Dunn turned to Klein.

"There were six Beyonders in total from the Mandated Punishers, the Machinery Hivemind, and the Nighthawks. We followed the wounded Tris to Lower Iron Cross Street and located his temporary residence, but the trail ended there.

"Neither Beyonder methods nor ordinary investigative techniques produced anything. It was as though he evaporated into thin air."

"Would you like me to attempt a divination?" Klein asked tentatively.

Dunn gently shook his head.

"The Machinery Hivemind brought a Mystery Pryer. He's an experienced Beyonder, comparable to Old Neil. I even suspect he has reached Sequence 8, though I don't know the corresponding potion's name."

"The Theosophy Order has survived until the present day. It must possess methods ordinary organizations lack," Klein said consolingly.

For the rest of the morning, he followed his usual routine: studying mysticism, reading historical records, and practicing the techniques he had learned.

As lunchtime approached, his thoughts began to wander.

Several minutes later, he heard the insistent summons of his stomach and put away the documents.

At that moment, Dunn Smith entered the clerks' office.

"Klein, come with me to Chanis Gate," he said in his deep but gentle voice. "Sealed Artifact 2-049 has arrived. The operation that follows may require you to sense the notebook."

"All right." Klein rose at once.

His thoughts became chaotic as he imagined what the Sealed Artifact might look like and how dangerous the operation could become.

In tense silence, he followed Dunn downstairs and into the tunnel.

After they continued straight through the intersection, Dunn abruptly stopped and turned toward him with an exceptionally solemn expression.

"Copy this movement and continue without stopping. Remember--under no circumstances are you to stop. This is for your own safety."

As he spoke, Dunn repeatedly bent and extended one arm.

Klein watched the demonstration in bewilderment. Then realization struck.

"Is this related to the Sealed Artifact's unique properties?"

"Yes." Dunn nodded with unusual gravity. "As long as you repeat the movement, we'll immediately notice if something happens to you. If we intervene in time, your life shouldn't be in danger."

"Understood."

Without further hesitation, Klein began repeatedly bending and extending his arm.

"If that arm becomes tired, switch to the other," Dunn added.

Sealed Artifact 2-049 is certainly bizarre... What purpose does this movement serve? It sounds exceptionally dangerous.

The thoughts flashed through Klein's mind as he looked solemnly at the captain.

"All right."

Questions crowded his thoughts, but Chanis Gate had already come into view, so he could only suppress them.

Besides, with my current clearance, they probably won't tell me the full details. All I can do is follow orders.

Klein exhaled and accompanied Captain Dunn into the guardroom outside Chanis Gate.

Chapter 71: Sluggish Phenomenon

Klein continued bending and extending his arm as he watched Dunn turn sideways and push open the door to the guardroom.

The captain's caution and vigilance, combined with the absurd-looking "protective exercise," left Klein unusually tense. It reminded him of the tests of courage from his youth, when he and his friends had dared one another to walk through eerie graveyards at night.

A Grade 2 Sealed Artifact. Dangerous. Must be used carefully and in moderation... Even ordinary Nighthawks aren't permitted to know its exact properties. Just how dangerous is it?

With his nerves already taut, Klein couldn't stop his imagination from running wild.

Then his mind suddenly went numb, as though someone had switched off the power.

Everything in his vision slowed. His arm movements became sluggish as well.

He saw Captain Dunn stop, turn, and approach him in what appeared to be slow motion. The captain gradually extended a hand and pushed Klein's shoulder.

In an instant, Klein's thoughts and vision returned to normal, making the previous experience seem almost illusory.

"What happened?" he whispered, frightened and confused.

Dunn shook his head and replied in a deep voice, "Pay close attention."

He turned and entered the guardroom. Klein followed close behind and found four other people inside, some sitting and others standing.

One was the Midnight Poet, Leonard. Klein had never met the other three. All four, however, shared one peculiar behavior: they were solemnly and continuously bending and extending their arms.

"Klein Moretti has a mysterious connection to the Antigonus family's notebook," Dunn said by way of introduction.

He gestured toward the three strangers.

"These colleagues from the Backlund diocese escorted Sealed Artifact 2-049 to Tingen. This is Madam Lorotta, a Sequence 8 Gravedigger and expert marksman."

The black-haired woman, who appeared to be about thirty, gave Klein a friendly nod.

She was attractive and wore no hat. Her clothing resembled a man's: a black coat over a white shirt, fitted black trousers, and black leather boots. The corners of her mouth curved upward slightly.

After Klein returned her greeting, Dunn pointed toward a man seated behind the desk.

"Aiur Harson. Like me, he is--"

Before Dunn could finish, Klein saw the movement of Aiur Harson's arm become sluggish. The man in the gray trench coat moved as though his gears had lost their oil and every joint had rusted.

What's happening?

As Klein stared in confusion, Lorotta reached over and pushed Aiur Harson. His movements immediately returned to normal.

Was that what happened to me?

Klein first felt startled, then understood.

This was the danger posed by Sealed Artifact 2-049.

What would happen if no one woke the victim in time?

Would they become a zombie?

Questions filling his mind, Klein exchanged greetings with the handsome, middle-aged Aiur Harson.

"Borgia," Dunn said, indicating the final Nighthawk.

Borgia was a stern man with a knife scar along one side of his face. His sharp brown eyes resembled an eagle's, and he constantly observed everyone in the room.

"Let's depart," Aiur Harson said as he stood. Fine wrinkles showed at the corners of his eyes. "The sooner we finish, the sooner we can seal 2-049 again."

Where is 2-049?

Klein curiously surveyed the room but saw no trace of the artifact. Of course, without activating Spiritual Vision, he couldn't see anything concealed behind the desk.

"All right." Dunn turned toward Leonard Mitchell. "You'll drive. It's best not to involve Cesare in a matter like this."

Cesare was the clerk responsible for purchasing and collecting supplies for the Tingen Nighthawks, and he also served as their carriage driver. He had driven Klein to Welch's house when they met Spirit Medium Daly.

"No problem." Leonard abandoned his usual frivolous manner and nodded solemnly.

Aiur Harson bent down and lifted a black metal chest from behind the desk.

Brilliant stars and a crimson full moon had been carved into its surface. An invisible barrier seemed to surround the entire chest.

The Sealed Artifact must be inside. I wonder what 2-049 actually looks like...

Klein watched with fascination.

Thump!

Thump! Thump!

Violent pounding suddenly erupted from inside the black chest. Its metal surface bulged outward again and again.

Thump! Thump! Thump!

It sounded as though something horrifying had awakened and was frantically trying to escape. Every impact seemed to strike directly against the hearts of those present.

It's alive?

The thought had barely formed when Captain Dunn's arm movements began to slow, as though glue had filled his joints.

Borgia immediately pushed Dunn's shoulder, restoring him to normal.

Anyone affected by 2-049 looks like they're performing a mechanical dance... If it caught all of us at once, we'd resemble the world's most awkward dance troupe.

Fortunately, it appears capable of affecting only one person at a time.

Klein silently joked to relieve his tension, but didn't dare stop exercising his arm.

Following Dunn's example, he left his cane behind. Then he accompanied the five Nighthawks through the tunnel and up the stairs to the second floor of the Blackthorn Security Company.

Leonard had gone ahead to warn everyone in the front offices. Rozanne and the others had already moved to the third floor. Although they rarely became involved with incidents of this kind, they weren't entirely unfamiliar with the procedures. Another Nighthawk, Kenley, had taken Dunn's place guarding Chanis Gate.

Klein breathed a little easier when they reached the carriage. Glancing suspiciously out the window, he asked, "Won't 2-049 affect ordinary people in the street?"

During the short journey from underground to the carriage, the artifact had already caused six incidents of sluggishness. Two had targeted Klein, and Captain Dunn and Leonard Mitchell had respectively jolted him awake. The frequency was alarming.

"There's no need to worry," Lorotta explained lazily. "2-049 prioritizes humanoid creatures within five meters. The closer someone is, the more likely they are to be chosen. As long as at least three people remain around it, pedestrians passing the carriage won't be affected."

What a bizarre Sealed Artifact...

Klein continued bending and extending his arm.

During the journey to Ray Bieber's home, Dunn and the other Nighthawks spoke little, concentrating on one another's condition. Only Lorotta appeared unconcerned. Sometimes she watched Tingen's less-than-clean streets through the window; at other times, she praised Backlund's underground sewer system.

Before long, the familiar building appeared.

The six of them made their way to the third floor while carefully monitoring one another.

The door to Ray Bieber's residence bore the seal of the Tingen Police Department, warning unauthorized persons not to enter.

Without interrupting his arm exercise, Dunn took out a key and opened the newly installed lock. Then he stepped aside, allowing Aiur Harson and the black chest to enter first.

Thump!

Thump! Thump! Thump!

The artifact began hammering against the chest with even greater violence. Aiur Harson's arm swayed uncontrollably beneath the impacts, and Klein wondered whether the thing inside might eventually smash its way free.

Thump! Thump! Thump!

Klein noticed Captain Dunn's movements slowing. He was about to push him when a buzzing filled his own head.

His mind went numb, and the scene before him began unfolding in slow motion.

Didn't they say... it could affect only one... person at a time?

Even Klein's thoughts became sluggish.

Lorotta and Borgia, who had been prepared for precisely this development, pushed Dunn and Klein respectively.

His mind and vision snapped back to normal. Klein looked around with lingering fear and nearly blurted out his question.

"Didn't you say 2-049 could affect only one person at a time?"

Fortunately, I never stopped moving my arm!

"When Sealed Artifact 2-049 enters an agitated state, it can affect two people simultaneously," Aiur Harson explained in a mechanical tone. "We can now confirm that Ray Bieber is indeed a descendant of the Antigonus family."

Lorotta gave a soft laugh and looked at Klein.

"2-049 becomes extremely excited in the presence of an Antigonus descendant, even if only the person's scent remains. Its abilities also increase considerably. I'm sure you can understand its feelings."

No, I really can't...

Klein asked curiously, "Is it a living creature?"

Lorotta smiled without answering directly.

"You'll see soon enough. As long as Ray Bieber hasn't left Tingen, 2-049 will lead us to him."

Klein could only suppress his remaining questions as he followed the Nighthawks around the apartment.

With the chest still shaking under violent blows, they locked the door, descended the stairs, and returned to the carriage.

Aiur Harson looked out the window several times and confirmed that no pedestrians were within five meters. Then he placed the black chest on the floor and turned a mechanical switch, releasing the spiritual restraints.

The pounding stopped instantly.

Silence engulfed the carriage. Not even the breathing of the Nighthawks could be heard.

Klein held his breath as the chest slowly opened, producing a sharp creak that hurt his ears.

Creak!

The lid fell back, and a slender brown arm extended from within. It was only about as long as a child's finger.

A second arm emerged. Bracing themselves against the edge of the chest, they slowly lifted an object no larger than an ordinary human palm into view.

It had clearly articulated elbows, fingers, and knees. Oil-stained brown cloth covered its body, while red and yellow paint formed a clown's face.

It was a bizarre wooden puppet.

2-049 raised its head and stared directly at Klein with pure-black eyes.

Its rigid mouth slowly opened into a clownish smile.

Chapter 72: Tracking

The wooden puppet's face had been painted red and yellow like that of an ordinary clown. The corners of its mouth curved unnaturally high, forming an absurdly comical smile.

Its lips parted to reveal a dark, bottomless mouth.

The moment Klein met its gaze, every hair on his body stood on end. Intense terror surged uncontrollably from the depths of his heart.

The world before him dimmed, as though he were looking through a thick sheet of brown glass.

His thoughts gradually slowed. Instinctively, he tried to call for help, but it felt as though a rope had tightened around his neck. He couldn't make a sound. The single word remained trapped in his throat.

Then Dunn noticed Klein's arm movements becoming sluggish and shoved him hard.

The brown glass over his vision shattered instantly.

"Help!"

The word burst from Klein's throat and echoed sharply through the carriage, filled with panic.

"It's growing stronger," he said with certainty.

Remaining beside a strange Sealed Artifact like 2-049 was terrifying. A moment's carelessness could place them in mortal danger. No--ordinary caution wasn't enough to defend against it. They could only rely on strict countermeasures.

"That's normal," Aiur Harson replied evenly with a nod.

Lorotta chuckled.

"It seems to like you. Don't worry. Among Grade 2 Sealed Artifacts, it's considered relatively safe."

As she spoke in her naturally languid voice, the puppet rose on joints modeled unmistakably after those of a human and began tottering to its left.

Its movements were stiff and mechanical, like a steam engine rusted from years without oil.

A mechanical dance...

The phrase surfaced in Klein's mind, followed by a new theory about the artifact's danger.

Does it transform the living creatures it controls into things like itself?

If the others failed to wake me in time, would I become a human-sized puppet--a living doll?

While those thoughts crowded his mind, Aiur Harson began to slow and was promptly awakened by Dunn.

Still bending and extending his arm, Aiur pointed in the direction the puppet was attempting to walk.

"That way!" he called to Leonard, who was driving.

Since the carriage couldn't pass through the building ahead, Leonard had to take a detour. Throughout each turn, 2-049 continually adjusted its direction, behaving like a compass that always pointed toward the Antigonus family.

Despite the tension, Klein nearly laughed at the sight.

I heard 2-049 was created by the Antigonus family. Is this loyalty to its creators, or the perfect example of sabotaging one's own side?

Leonard steered according to Aiur Harson's intermittent directions.

Whenever the bizarre puppet reached the edge of the carriage, Aiur picked it up, returned it to the center, and forced it to begin walking again.

Each time he did, its mouth opened and its power simultaneously seized two people.

Gradually, Klein's taut nerves relaxed. Sealed Artifact 2-049 no longer seemed quite as horrifying. As long as at least three people remained nearby, continued their arm movements, and woke affected companions immediately, it was merely a puppet with peculiar abilities.

The carriage raced onward and soon reached the warehouse district near the harbor.

After circling the area several times, they confirmed that 2-049 was pointing toward the innermost gray-white warehouse.

Aiur Harson's expression turned solemn. He carefully seized the puppet and forced it back into the black chest.

Thump! Thump! Thump!

The savage pounding resumed at once.

With Borgia and Lorotta repeatedly waking him from bouts of sluggishness, Aiur struggled to activate the chest's mechanism. He infused it with spirituality, causing the engraved stars and crimson moon to light up.

When the invisible seal reappeared, Aiur Harson released a long, heavy breath.

"Let's disembark," Dunn Smith said in his deep, gentle voice. "Leonard, tie the horse here."

The six Beyonders, dressed variously in trench coats, suits, and shirts, left the carriage and approached the innermost warehouse. As they walked, every one of them continued rhythmically bending and extending an arm.

The absurdly synchronized movements lent a comic air to an otherwise tense situation.

The Nighthawks' Awkward Dance Troupe...

Klein could only make jokes inwardly to ease the pressure.

There was no better solution. From what he had observed, 2-049 affected the upper body first. To recognize its influence before the situation became dangerous, they needed to perform some continuous movement with their arms, necks, or torsos.

Shaking their necks or bodies would make them look like street hooligans. Blinking or raising their eyebrows was too easy to overlook, while more exaggerated facial movements were impractical.

This awkward dance troupe is still better than looking like a Causeway Bay gang.[1]

Klein sighed resignedly and followed Dunn and the others.

The closer they came to the warehouse door, the deeper his anxiety grew.

No one knew what effect the Antigonus notebook had produced in Ray Bieber.

If something terrible happened, Klein didn't dare place his hopes in transmigrating a second time.

He could still cut himself while preparing dinner, and he bled and healed at an ordinary human rate. He wasn't an immortal monster impervious to injury or death.

Without warning, Dunn lowered his free hand and made a pressing gesture, signaling everyone to stop about ten meters from the warehouse entrance.

He turned toward Klein. His gray eyes remained as deep and calm as ever, betraying no fear.

"Divine whether there is danger inside. If possible, determine how severe it is."

Klein gave a barely perceptible nod and stopped exercising one arm. He reached into his left cuff and removed the silver chain holding a topaz pendant.

Because Dunn continued moving, he immediately noticed when Klein began to slow and jolted him awake with a push.

Klein held the silver chain in his left hand, allowing the topaz to hang freely. He kept his right arm moving, though over a much smaller range.

Once the pendant became still, he half closed his eyes, visualized the sphere of light, and entered Cogitation.

"There is danger inside the warehouse."

"There is danger inside the warehouse."

After repeating the statement seven times, Klein opened his eyes.

The suspended topaz began rotating clockwise.

It spun faster and faster until Klein could feel it tugging at his left hand.

"There is danger--great danger," he reported honestly.

Clockwise rotation affirmed the divination statement, while counterclockwise motion denied it.

For most Beyonders, including Mystery Pryers, spirit dowsing could reveal whether danger existed but not its degree.

Klein, however, had discovered that his pendulum rotated at different speeds depending on the answer's intensity. The distinction was imprecise and vague, but it allowed him to make a rough assessment of the situation.

As expected of the Seer potion...

He felt quietly pleased with the result.

Just as Klein prepared to put away the pendulum, Leonard Mitchell, who had remained silent until then, spoke.

"Divine whether there is danger around us as well."

Dunn nodded in agreement.

"Yes. The Secret Order may not have abandoned the notebook. They could have kept Ray Bieber's home under surveillance, followed us here, and chosen a critical moment to interfere."

Klein inhaled deeply and once more entered a calm, ethereal state.

When the silver chain settled, he silently recited, "There is danger around us."

"There is danger around us."

After repeating the statement, he opened his eyes and watched the topaz.

At first, the pendulum laboriously rotated counterclockwise. Then it abruptly stopped and began turning clockwise instead.

"There is danger around us," Klein said cautiously as his heart tightened.

Someone had attempted to interfere with his divination--but had lost the invisible contest.

The instant he spoke, an orange-yellow fireball the size of a fist hurtled toward them.

It streaked directly toward the center of the group at blazing speed.

Dunn Smith had drawn his long-barreled revolver before Klein began the divination. He raised it immediately, aimed, and pulled the trigger.

Bang!

The bullet appeared to have no effect. The fireball continued along its original path, forcing everyone to scatter.

At first, Klein hadn't considered the hidden enemy much of a threat. Six Beyonders were present, including Sequence 8 and Sequence 7 experts. In a modest city like Tingen, their combined strength was almost overwhelming.

But as the fireball descended, realization struck him.

Their most dangerous enemy wasn't the pursuer outside, nor even Ray Bieber in his unknown condition within the warehouse.

It was Sealed Artifact 2-049.

If the attack forced them apart and prevented them from waking one another, they would be transformed into living puppets one after another.

Chaotic thoughts flashed through Klein's mind as Leonard grabbed him and pulled him aside.

With no time to worry about his clothes, Klein saw the Nighthawks divide into two groups in a disciplined, orderly maneuver.

Poof!

The orange-yellow fireball struck the ground without disturbing a single grain of dust. It vanished as though it had never existed.

An illusion?

As the thought occurred to Klein, Aiur Harson lifted the black chest and hurled it at least ten meters away.

"Keep your distance! Guard it!" Aiur shouted.

Before he finished, Leonard and Borgia had already moved into position on opposite sides. Each remained at least seven meters from the chest while preventing anyone from approaching it.

Dunn and Lorotta drew their guns and joined Aiur, who had unsheathed a slender silver sword. The three formed a crescent and rushed toward the source of the fireball while watching the surrounding area.

Still moving his arm and deprived of his cane, Klein sighed in relief.

He had overlooked one crucial fact.

2-049's influence had a limited range. As long as they stayed far enough away, they no longer needed to fear its power.

Klein rolled to his feet. With one hand, he stuffed the topaz pendulum into his pocket; with the other, he reached for the revolver in his holster.

[1] Causeway Bay is a district in Hong Kong frequently associated with triad imagery in popular films.

Chapter 73: First Battle

Beneath the afternoon sun, Klein stood with dust coating his clothes and swiftly rotated his revolver's cylinder, disengaging the safety mechanism he had devised.

Sunlight gleamed along the weapon's brass frame as he adopted a firing stance.

He held the revolver in one hand while keeping the other arm in constant motion, cautiously watching for changes in every direction.
