At the same time, he couldn't help worrying about Captain Dunn and Aiur Harson. Both were Nightmares who specialized in influencing enemies through concealed and indirect means. Klein didn't know how capable they were in a direct confrontation.

As the thought occurred to him, Aiur Harson slowed his movements. His expression became peaceful and serene.

He opened his mouth and recited a soothing poem that seemed to draw the listener into the embrace of night.

"When once the sun sinks in the west,

"And dewdrops pearl the evening's breast;

"Almost as pale as moonbeams are,

"Or its companionable star,

"The evening primrose opens anew

"Its delicate blossoms to the dew;

"And, hermit-like, shunning the light..."[1]

The verses reverberated around them. Klein's tension nearly vanished, leaving him completely relaxed.

Fortunately, he had experienced a similar effect before and wasn't Aiur's intended target. He quickly gathered his thoughts and entered a partial state of Cogitation to resist the poem's influence.

Phew...

He breathed out in relief. He no longer doubted Dunn's or Aiur's ability to fight directly.

Because Klein had advanced only recently and still lacked a deep understanding of the Sequence system, he had forgotten that Sequence 7 Nightmare followed Sequence 8 Midnight Poet. Advancing didn't erase a Beyonder's former abilities; it preserved and even slightly strengthened them.

Most of Klein's impressions of Midnight Poets came from Leonard Mitchell. The profession inherited a Sleepless's distinctive traits, making its members skilled at combat, shooting, climbing, and perception. Through poetry, they could also influence the living creatures around them.

In simpler terms, they were poets with a talent for violence.

As Aiur continued his recitation, the stacked wooden crates nearby suddenly rippled like the surface of water.

A man in a black tuxedo and half top hat emerged from them.

His face had been painted in three pastel colors--red, yellow, and white. The corners of his lips curved sharply upward like a clown's, creating an absurd contrast with the formal evening attire.

Thud! Thud! Thud!

Lorotta, the black-haired woman introduced as a sharpshooter, charged forward. She held a gun in one hand and clenched the other into a fist. Within a few strides, she had closed the distance to the painted man.

The suited clown appeared affected by Aiur Harson's poem. His body swayed, his eyes looked peaceful, and he showed no desire to resist.

Lorotta shifted into a boxer's stance, drew back her fist, and drove it toward his face.

Bang!

The air cracked beneath the force of her punch.

The suited clown shattered like a mirror. His fragments rapidly evaporated and vanished.

At the same instant, another outline appeared in the shadows several steps away, beside a different stack of crates. The suited clown swiftly took shape once more.

The figure affected by the poem had been nothing but an illusion--part of a performance.

The clown grinned. With an exaggeratedly comic expression, he held down his half top hat in one hand and formed a pistol with the fingers of the other.

Bang!

An apparent gunshot rang out.

Lorotta threw herself left and rolled, evading the attack.

But nothing happened. The sound had been an illusion as well.

Bang! Bang! Bang!

Dunn and Aiur raised their revolvers and fired with steady precision.

The suited clown dodged with astonishing agility. He darted left and right, dropped low, and rolled across the ground like a circus acrobat.

Lorotta abruptly charged again. Despite being called a sharpshooter, she still chose to fight with her fists.

Bam!

The clown failed to evade in time and raised his left arm to block her blow.

The instant he stopped moving, Dunn and Aiur took aim and pulled their triggers.

Orange-yellow flame erupted from the clown's blocking arm.

Within a heartbeat, the fire engulfed his entire body and spread toward Lorotta.

Bang! Bang!

Dunn and Aiur's bullets struck the blazing figure.

The flames rapidly consumed it, leaving only black ash drifting through the air.

Yet the suited clown appeared once more behind a nearby stack of crates.

He raised his right hand and pointed another finger gun.

Bang!

At the illusory gunshot, Lorotta abruptly halted instead of charging.

Mud burst from the ground directly in front of her as a real bullet struck.

This time, the clown's attack hadn't been an illusion.

Real and false, reality and deception--they were almost impossible to distinguish.

Bang! Bang! Bang!

The clown repeatedly fired at Dunn and Aiur, vanishing into hiding and reappearing at unpredictable moments.

Lorotta narrowed her eyes and raised the dull-gold revolver in her left hand.

Bang!

The clown suddenly crouched, narrowly avoiding a fatal shot. His half top hat flew backward and landed on the ground with a scorched groove across it.

After rolling several times, he scrambled up the stacked crates with a monkey's agility. From the high ground, he launched invisible bullets from his finger gun.

Aiur Harson stepped back and lowered his revolver. Then he resumed his recitation.

"Wastes its fair bloom upon the night,

"Who, blindfold to its fond caresses,

"Knows not the beauty it possesses..."

The suited clown leaped repeatedly between the crates. Then he scratched one ear and gave Aiur an exaggerated smile.

Did he block his ears? The pathway controlled by the Secret Order truly is peculiar...

Klein observed the battle from a distance, silently forming theories.

Then a figure appeared atop the warehouse beside him.

It was running directly toward the building where Ray Bieber was hiding.

The newcomer wore the gray-white uniform of a dockworker. His face, too, was painted red, yellow, and white.

The suited clown is distracting the captain and the others while this second man retrieves the notebook?

Klein instinctively raised his revolver and aimed at the figure on the roof.

Before he could finish lining up the shot, the man suddenly dropped into a crouch and transitioned from running into a roll.

Bang!

Klein pulled the trigger without hesitation.

The figure abruptly stumbled as blood sprayed from his body.

He stared at Klein in shock. Enduring the pain, he continued toward the warehouse.

That felt like a lucky hit...

Klein's lips twitched as he fired again. This time, the bullet struck the wooden roof beside the target.

Bang! Bang! Bang!

Leonard and Borgia also opened fire, but neither hit him.

Klein was about to mock their shooting compared with his own when he suddenly stopped pulling the trigger.

That's right. Why do we need to stop him?

Didn't my divination indicate grave danger inside the warehouse? Wouldn't it be better to let him enter first and trigger whatever trap awaits us?

Leonard and Borgia must have reached the same conclusion.

With that realization, Klein raised his revolver and fired harmlessly into the air.

Bang! Bang! Bang!

Amid the gunfire, the figure reached the deepest section of the warehouse without further obstruction.

He threw himself downward, crashed through the roof, and disappeared inside amid a shower of collapsing timber.

At that moment, Lorotta's eyes turned completely black. Her left hand performed a strange pulling motion.

The suited clown froze in mid-leap, as though an invisible hand had closed around his ankle.

Dunn didn't fire immediately. Instead, he lowered his revolver.

Without moving his throat, he used his spirituality to resonate with the surrounding air, producing an eerie, distant, ethereal voice.

"Thus it blooms on while night is by;

"When day looks out with open eye,

"Bashed at the gaze it cannot shun,

"It faints and withers and is gone."

The suited clown's body went limp, as though he had lost all desire to live.

Aiur Harson raised his revolver, aimed, and began squeezing the trigger.

At that exact instant, a hideous scream erupted from inside the warehouse.

"Ahhh!"

The cry contained immeasurable terror, as though the intruder had witnessed something beyond imagination.

Every hair on Klein's body stood upright.

The scream ended abruptly. Silence returned to the deepest part of the warehouse--a silence that made the skin crawl.

Bang!

Distracted by the cry, Aiur struck the suited clown only in the abdomen.

Haa... Haa... Haa...

The silence within the warehouse broke again.

What should have been quiet breathing sounded unnaturally loud. It rose steadily toward a crescendo, tightening everyone's nerves.

Thump! Thump! Thump! Thump! Thump! Thump!

Inside the black chest, Sealed Artifact 2-049 had gone completely berserk.

[1] Adapted from John Clare's poem "Evening Primrose."

Chapter 74: Ray Bieber

Haa! Haa! Haa!

Thump! Thump! Thump!

The thunderous panting and violent pounding first alternated, then sounded together. The unholy rhythm stretched Klein's nerves taut, like the murmurs of some malevolent existence.

While Aiur, Dunn, and Lorotta were distracted, the suited clown pulled a long strip of paper from his pocket.

Pa!

He flicked it with his right hand. The paper ignited and transformed into a whip of black flame, which he lashed toward the invisible restraint around his ankle.

A brief but agonized scream rang out.

The suited clown broke free, executed a backward somersault, and landed beyond Lorotta's reach.

Bang! Bang! Bang!

Dunn, Aiur, and Lorotta opened fire, but their bullets struck only the wooden crates.

The clown didn't linger. Pressing one hand against the wound in his abdomen, he fled away from the warehouse. His speed was extraordinary; within a heartbeat, only the distant outline of his back remained.

Before he disappeared, he moved the right hand covering his abdomen to his left arm.

The wound in his stomach had vanished completely, leaving the area perfectly intact. At the same moment, the flesh beneath his hand on his left arm became horribly mangled, and a silver bullet emerged from the torn tissue.

Dunn and the others didn't pursue him. The deafening panting from the innermost warehouse filled them with unease.

Bang!

The warehouse door exploded outward, scattering fragments in every direction.

Something wrapped in torn cloth flew through the opening and landed near Klein.

When he looked down, he realized it was an arm. Much of its bloody flesh had been chewed away, and splintered white bones protruded at irregular angles.

Pa! Pa! Pa!

One object after another sailed from the darkness.

First came a spray of blood, then a dilated eyeball and an ear that had been brutally torn free. Finally, half of a still-beating heart and a length of intestine filled with yellow-brown matter struck the ground.

If Klein hadn't already witnessed the gruesome giant corpse in Ray Bieber's home, he would have vomited immediately.

His nerves hovered on the brink of collapse. With considerable effort, he resisted the urge to fire blindly into the pitch-black entrance. Instead, he ejected the empty casings from his revolver and loaded fresh demon-hunting bullets.

Bang!

Dunn approached with steady steps and fired into the warehouse.

The bullet vanished into the darkness like a stone dropped into the sea. No response followed.

Haa! Haa! Haa!

The panting accelerated as a gray-white mass filled the open doorway.

Two more shots rang out. Bullets from Aiur Harson and Borgia tore through the whiteness but failed to stop it from spilling outward. They left neither wounds nor leaking fluid behind.

Klein held his breath and refrained from wasting ammunition. Before his eyes, the pallid mass gradually resolved into a complete figure.

It was a humanoid creature more than two meters tall.

Every joint in its limbs had twisted into an unnatural angle, as though someone had violently snapped each one. White bones pierced its skin, while the entire gray-white surface of its body was covered in folds and grooves resembling a human brain stripped of its skull.

Rotten, viscous fluid flowed across it.

Only its head remained relatively human, with pale skin and deep wrinkles. When its mouth opened and closed, Klein saw a porcelain false tooth hanging loose, strands of bloody saliva, and fragments of minced bone and flesh.

Could this thing still be called Ray Bieber--or even human?

Klein drew a silent breath as his heart hammered violently.

Bang!

Leonard's demon-hunting bullet struck Ray Bieber in the forehead and punched straight through, leaving a deep cavity.

Gray-white liquid dripped from the wound. Upon reaching the ground, it writhed and transformed into plump, cream-colored maggots.

The monster appeared completely unaffected.

Moving at a pace neither fast nor slow, it lunged toward Borgia, the nearest person. Its true objective, however, seemed to be the black chest containing Sealed Artifact 2-049.

"A Beyonder who has lost control!" Dunn shouted. "Lorotta, it resembles an undead creature. Find its weakness!"

"Understood."

Lorotta said nothing more. She raised both hands and pressed them over her eyes.

Her pupils turned gray, then colorless, as though her sight had entered the spirit world and the kingdom of the dead. From that elevated perspective, she searched the monster for a critical node.

Seeing that ordinary gunfire had no effect, Klein decided not to waste more bullets. He tapped his glabella and activated Spiritual Vision, intending to assist the Gravedigger.

In his altered sight, Monster Bieber possessed only a single spiritual color: pure gray-white, saturated with madness.

There was nothing else.

Then Aiur Harson and Leonard Mitchell began singing together.

"Oh, the threats of horror, the hopes of crimson cries!

"One thing at least is certain--this Life flies;

"One thing is certain, and the rest is Lies;

"The Flower that once has bloomed forever dies..."

The power of peaceful sleep spread through the air.

The twisted gray-white monster gradually slowed, unable to resist the poem's charm.

Then it opened its mouth and released a piercing shriek inaudible to ordinary humans.

"Ahhh!"

Bang!

Agony stabbed through Klein's skull, forcibly knocking him out of Spiritual Vision.

Warm liquid ran from his nose. When he instinctively wiped it away, the back of his hand came away covered in blood.

Aiur and Leonard collapsed at the same time, blood tracing the corners of their mouths, noses, and eyes.

Borgia, Dunn, and Lorotta each staggered back one or two steps, their faces draining of color.

The monster had screamed only once, yet the attack had exceeded what all six Beyonders could withstand. Every one of them became severely weakened.

Bam!

Monster Bieber closed on Borgia and swung one grotesquely twisted limb.

Bang! Bang! Bang! Bang!

Borgia and Dunn each fired twice, but the bullets did no visible damage.

The monster's blow sent Borgia flying. His long-barreled revolver fell from his hand and struck the ground.

He tried several times to rise, but couldn't.

Stringy fluid dripped from the corner of Monster Bieber's mouth as it leaped toward the black chest.

Bang!

At the critical moment, Aiur Harson fired at the chest itself. The impact knocked it away before the monster could seize it, sending it sliding more than ten meters.

The black metal cracked, making the furious pounding within even louder and more distinct.

"I found it!" Lorotta finally cried. "Hold it still for at least three seconds!"

"All right."

Dunn didn't hesitate. He touched his glabella and closed his eyes.

He appeared to fall asleep. Invisible waves spread outward from him, one after another.

Monster Bieber abruptly froze. The madness in its eyes receded, and its thin, translucent eyelids began closing against its will.

Dunn's body trembled violently. Shapes bulged and writhed beneath his clothing, as though he had concealed a nest of slippery, scaleless snakes against his skin.

Lorotta charged forward, dropped into a roll, and arrived directly beneath the monster.

Supporting herself with one hand, she drove her clenched fist upward into its groin with the force of artillery.

Poof!

Ignoring the corrosive fluid burning her skin, she braced against the ground and forced herself higher, driving her fist deeper into its body.

Rip!

Lorotta tore her forearm free, dragging out a length of intestine smeared with yellow-brown blood.

Inside the intestine was an ancient notebook.

"Ahhh!"

Monster Bieber gave a bloodcurdling scream. Light suddenly shone from within its body as its flesh began to melt.

"Get down!"

Aiur Harson had barely shouted the warning when Klein saw the monster's body swell.

Boom!

The explosion hurled Klein through the air. He struck the ground heavily, his head spinning.

Struggling against the dizziness, he forced himself upright.

Monster Bieber had become a pile of disgusting, rotting flesh. Dunn and Lorotta lay unconscious more than ten meters away.

Aiur Harson, Borgia, and Leonard Mitchell were also on the ground. Some groaned in pain, while others repeatedly tried and failed to stand.

Klein was about to relax when he noticed a familiar object only two or three meters away.

The black chest had stopped rolling with its cracked surface facing upward.

A thin brown arm extended from within.

Sealed Artifact 2-049... Damn it!

Klein's heart clenched. He immediately threw himself in the opposite direction, desperate to escape the artifact's effective range.

The explosion had sent the chest tumbling directly toward him.

At that instant, a buzzing filled Klein's head.

His thoughts became sluggish.

Chapter 75: Saving Himself

Oh no! The puppet has me!

Captain Dunn and the others are either unconscious... or too badly injured to rise. They can't... wake me in time...

No. I have to... save myself!

Everything before Klein unfolded in slow motion. His mind and every joint in his body seemed to be coated in a layer of glue that grew thicker by the second.

He had no intention of becoming a human puppet. While he still retained a fragment of control, he desperately searched for a way to save himself.

I can't... strike myself. It has to be... an external force...

An external force... I'll try it. There is no time to hesitate.

Without the luxury of thinking through every consequence, Klein arrived at an idea within three seconds. He forced his "rusted" knee to move and took one step counterclockwise.

He didn't attempt to overcome the invisible rope constricting his throat. Instead, he recited the incantation silently.

The... Immortal Lord of... Heaven and Earth... for Blessings...

He intended to use the mysterious realm above the gray fog to awaken himself and escape Sealed Artifact 2-049's assimilation.

Creak! Creak! Creak!

Klein's knees and ankles produced piercing sounds as he forced his distorted body through a second counterclockwise step.

The Sky Lord... of Heaven and Earth... for Blessings...

His thoughts became increasingly sluggish. He felt like a computer burdened with every kind of useless software and half a dozen conflicting antivirus programs.

With jerky effort, he lifted his left foot and placed it in the required position.

The... Exalted Thearch...

His mind grew more rigid with every instant. Only ingrained instinct allowed him to complete the final step.

By then, Klein knew the puppet had almost completely seized him. Even if Aiur Harson recovered in time, he might already be beyond awakening.

Yet his fierce desire to live drove him to finish the final line.

The... Celestial... Worthy...

The instant the incantation ended, chaotic, hysterical howls and murmurs erupted around him. They flooded every corner of his sluggish mind, shattered the paralysis, and reduced his thoughts to fragments beyond his control.

Klein's brain became a boiling mixture of confusion. His stiff body turned weightless as his spirituality rose.

Endless gray-white fog filled his vision. Dark-red stars floated at varying distances, vast, mysterious, hazy, and indistinct.

His chaotic mind swiftly calmed. The magnificent palace appeared, and his ability to think returned.

"Phew... Thank goodness it worked," Klein whispered, still shaken.

From his observations, anyone fully assimilated by Sealed Artifact 2-049 was effectively dead. Under normal circumstances, no medicine or treatment could save the victim.

Fortunately, the luck-enhancement ritual and the realm above the gray fog were anything but normal.

After pacing several times, Klein considered his predicament.

I can't remain here indefinitely.

If Captain Dunn and the others wake up and gather around my body, how will I explain any of this?

Right now, my body is nothing but an empty shell--more lifeless than a zombie.

But if I return immediately, I can't guarantee my safety. What if 2-049 takes control of me again?

Caught in the dilemma, Klein suddenly slapped his forehead and chuckled.

"It seems I still haven't adjusted to being a Seer."

Before the words finished echoing, he appeared in the seat of honor at the long bronze table, seated in the high-backed chair bearing the strange symbol.

Klein extended a hand, and a fountain pen materialized from the fog.

On an illusory sheet of paper, he wrote:

Returning to the real world is safe.

He then drew out the projection of his spirit pendulum. After several Tarot Gatherings, he had discovered that the objects carried on his body appeared above the gray fog as relatively insubstantial projections.

Holding the silver chain in his left hand, Klein allowed the topaz to hang just above the paper.

He steadied his breathing, half closed his eyes, and repeated the statement calmly.

"Returning to the real world is safe."

"Returning to the real world is safe."

After seven repetitions, he completed the spirit-dowsing divination.

Klein opened his eyes.

The topaz was slowly drawing the silver chain clockwise.

Clockwise means yes, while counterclockwise means no... Returning to the real world is safe.

He sighed in relief and habitually put away the chain. Then he released his spirituality, wrapped it around himself, and simulated the sensation of falling.

The hazy fog and dark-red stars became ethereal and rushed upward.

Soon, Klein saw his body standing dazed in its original position. The brown puppet had emerged halfway from the chest, but Sealed Artifact 2-049 had apparently stopped moving.

His physical senses reconnected with his mind. Just as he prepared to move an arm and test his condition, a voice hidden within the wind reached him.

"Do you wish to be awakened? I can save you if you agree to one condition.

"Help me obtain the Antigonus family's notebook.

"Nod if you agree. I know you still retain enough control to do that."

Who is speaking?

And 2-049 no longer appears to be controlling me... That's right. It doesn't repeatedly target the same person without an interval.

Klein was startled, but allowed none of it to show on his face.

The hidden voice quickly continued.

"You will receive an additional reward if you succeed. I know you're a Seer, and I know the Church of the Evernight Goddess doesn't possess the Sequence 8 formula that follows it. The Secret Order does.

"To be honest, I was once a Seer myself. Otherwise, I wouldn't have dared return. As proof of my sincerity, I'll tell you the name now. The Sequence 8 following Seer is Clown."

Clown? The Secret Order?

Klein nearly failed to maintain his puppet-like stillness.

He had never imagined any connection between Seer and Clown.

Was the entire pathway leading toward management of a circus?

"Make your choice," the voice urged through the wind. "Believe me, you don't have much time left."

In the distance, Dunn and Lorotta remained unconscious. Borgia lay heavily injured, moaning without moving. Aiur Harson and Leonard Mitchell were in somewhat better condition and were attempting to sit up.

Why did he choose me?

The Secret Order... Is this the suited clown from before? Did he return after escaping so he could take advantage of the aftermath?

Questions flashed rapidly through Klein's mind.

Since the man claimed to have been a Seer, Klein approached the situation through a Seer's reasoning.

He dared return because his divination revealed "hope." He expected Monster Bieber to die and our group to suffer severe injuries.

He won't retrieve the notebook himself or attack us directly because his divination probably warned of enormous danger. He suspects Captain Dunn and Madam Lorotta may be pretending to be unconscious, or that the entire scene is a trap.

He hasn't divined my present condition because, first, he has little time. If he waits much longer, Aiur Harson and the others will recover enough combat strength to resist him. Second, he underestimates me and considers another divination unnecessary.

He understands Seers and is certain I can't escape the puppet's control. He intends to use me as expendable bait to test for traps.

Viewed another way, this confirms that the luck-enhancement ritual produces no visible abnormality in my body.

With his mind no longer impaired, Klein's analysis unfolded smoothly. He felt confident that he understood the suited clown's motives and plan.

As for the promised reward, he didn't believe a word of it.

Expendable bait had no rights.

While those thoughts raced through him, Klein forced his neck to move and nodded with apparent difficulty.

The motion confirmed that he had fully escaped Sealed Artifact 2-049's control.

A transparent "curtain" stirred two or three meters to his side, revealing the suited clown with his face painted in pastel colors. It was indeed the Secret Order member who had fled earlier.

When Klein had attempted to leap out of 2-049's range, he had turned his back to both the chest and puppet. The suited clown now stood diagonally in front of him. That position kept the man away from the artifact while also avoiding the barrel of Klein's revolver.

He was exceedingly cautious.

The clown removed a long strip of paper from his pocket and snapped it outward until it became as rigid as a wooden pole.

Remaining two or three meters away, he extended the pole and prepared to prod Klein's shoulder awake.

He knows 2-049 extremely well. He knows the puppet becomes agitated by the scent of an Antigonus descendant and can control two people simultaneously. He also knows that throwing a stone apparently doesn't work--Captain Dunn and the others must have tested similar methods before.

Although Klein didn't understand why 2-049 had temporarily stopped assimilating him, he had no intention of remaining within five meters of it. He held his breath and waited.

The instant the wooden pole was about to touch his shoulder, Klein's left hand shot up, seized its end, and yanked backward.

Caught completely off guard, the suited clown lurched forward. He staggered several steps, reducing the distance between them to less than two meters.

At the same moment, Klein squeezed the trigger of the revolver in his right hand.

Bang! Bang!

He didn't aim at the clown.

Instead, he fired behind him, toward the area beside Sealed Artifact 2-049.

Even before the shots sounded, the clown reacted instinctively. From his stumbling position, he dropped into a roll and retreated away from Klein.

Klein released the paper pole, rapidly stepped back, and rushed beyond the artifact's dangerous range.

After rolling twice, the suited clown prepared to spring backward.

Then his head buzzed, and his thoughts abruptly slowed.

No!

He forced me to dodge toward the Antigonus puppet!

I'm inside... five meters...

Why isn't he... under the puppet's control?

The clown's roll came to a stop. With joints that seemed rusted, he struggled to crawl away.

By then, Klein had already turned around.

He gripped his revolver in both hands and aimed at the slowly moving target.

To him, it was no different from firing at a stationary mark.

After watching the clown fight Dunn, Aiur, and Lorotta, Klein knew how agile he was and how skillfully he could roll and evade. Even at a distance of only one or two meters, Klein had deliberately refrained from shooting him directly.

Instead, he had forced the man to dodge into the "kill zone" he had selected--the area controlled by Sealed Artifact 2-049.

If the puppet had failed to affect him, the clown would have realized he had entered a trap and escaped by leaping backward. Even then, he would no longer pose an immediate threat.

Bang!

Reflected in the suited clown's indescribable eyes, Klein calmly pulled the trigger.

Chapter 76: Dealing with the Aftermath

Bang!

The silver bullet crossed the few meters between them and buried itself precisely in the suited clown's neck.

Blood erupted from the wound, staining his skin and bow tie crimson.

Unable to cry out, the clown produced only a strangled gasp. He tried to raise an arm and transfer the fatal injury elsewhere, but his joints seemed packed with glue. Every movement was slow and jerky.

Bang!

In a partial state of Cogitation, Klein remained calm despite the blood. He squeezed the trigger again with the composure of someone practicing at the shooting range.

A grisly hole appeared in the clown's forehead, spraying red behind him. The ornate revolver's power was far greater than Klein had imagined.

The light faded from the man's eyes. His knees buckled, his arms dropped, and he slowly collapsed with bewilderment frozen across his face.

His body convulsed several times before relaxing into stillness.

After delivering the fatal headshot, Klein turned away with apparent composure. He rotated the revolver's cylinder and let the spent casings fall.

Still wearing his black formal suit and half top hat, he walked toward Aiur Harson. He removed the final silver demon-hunting bullet from his pocket and inserted it into an empty chamber.

Klein hadn't turned away because he wanted to look impressive. This was the first person he had killed, and the sight made him deeply uncomfortable.

Nevertheless, the second shot had been necessary. He didn't know what might happen if the suited clown fell completely under 2-049's control.

Nor had Klein dared reenter the artifact's effective range. No one could guarantee that some unexpected condition wouldn't prevent the luck-enhancement ritual from saving him a second time.

As for the clown's belongings, Klein cared only whether they contained the Clown formula or clues leading to it. There was no need to search the body immediately. He could do so later with Dunn, Aiur, and the others.

If the Nighthawks obtained the formula, that was nearly the same as Klein obtaining it. They had no reason to conceal a Sequence 8 formula from him forever. At most, he would need to accumulate sufficient merit before receiving it.

Besides, he had only recently become a Seer. Fully digesting the potion would still take considerable time.

As those thoughts turned through his mind, Klein reached Aiur Harson.

The gentleman in the gray trench coat was struggling to sit up, but every attempt failed. Dust and mud from the explosion covered his clothes.

Klein crouched beside him and kept his revolver pointed safely toward the ground.

"Mr. Harson, what should I do?"

Aiur gasped and sighed.

"That monster was too powerful. If we hadn't discovered its weakness..."

He pointed toward a sky-blue metal bottle lying nearby and laughed self-deprecatingly.

"I tried to take some medicine, but my hand was shaking."

The bottle was scarcely larger than Klein's finger and no more than five centimeters long. Its cap, engraved internally with spiral grooves, lay beside it. Nearly all the liquid had spilled.

Klein picked up the container and squinted inside.

"Mr. Harson, only a few drops remain."

"Search Borgia's inner pockets," Aiur said between breaths. "He has another bottle."

"All right." Klein stood and casually asked, "Is it a healing medicine?"

Was it some kind of mysticism item?

"It offers a degree of restoration, but its primary purpose is to stimulate the mind and force the body to release its remaining potential. It allows us to function temporarily until we return for proper treatment." Aiur attempted to sit up and failed again. "It's called the Goddess's Gaze. Remember to give Borgia half the bottle."

Klein wasted no time. He hurried to the groaning Borgia and found an identical sky-blue bottle in the Nighthawk's inner pocket.

After removing the cap, he carefully held it to Borgia's mouth.

Sensing what Klein was doing, Borgia forced his lips apart.

Dark-red liquid flowed into his mouth.

Klein estimated the quantity and stopped at half, then screwed the cap securely into place.

The medicine worked rapidly. Within seconds, vitality returned to Borgia's eyes.

"Thank you," he whispered.

He braced one hand against the ground and slowly sat up. After briefly tending to his own wounds, he walked to the unconscious Dunn and Lorotta and retrieved another bottle of the Goddess's Gaze from the captain's inner pocket.

Klein returned to Aiur and fed him the remaining half of Borgia's bottle.

After several deep breaths, Aiur's movements abruptly became agile. He rose as though he had never been injured.

"I'll assist Borgia. You help your teammate." The charming middle-aged gentleman gestured toward Leonard Mitchell.

Klein had no objection. He turned and jogged toward the poet.

"That won't be necessary. I can take it myself."

With his hair in disarray, Leonard smiled and raised a sky-blue bottle.

When Leonard pushed himself smoothly upright with one hand, Klein froze before he could make a joke.

Leonard's injuries are much lighter than I thought...

He was capable of drinking the medicine from the beginning.

That means he could have seen me take four counterclockwise steps and perform the luck-enhancement ritual.

No, that part should be fine. I recited the incantation silently, and the ritual produces no visible anomaly. Otherwise, the suited clown wouldn't have been deceived.

Even so, Leonard recovered long ago and chose to watch from the sidelines. He saw quite a lot--including my immunity to 2-049's continued control and my ambush of the suited clown.

Klein's eyes narrowed slightly.

Leonard approached, stopped beside him, and chuckled softly.

"I intended to save you, but then I discovered that you didn't need my help.

"Don't worry about it. This world contains many unusual people capable of things others cannot accomplish. People like you..."

He smiled as he walked past Klein toward the awakening Dunn and Lorotta.

"...and me."

Narcissist...

Klein silently mocked him, though the exchange left him considerably more relaxed.

It appears Leonard Mitchell is hiding a great many secrets of his own.

Deep in thought, Klein rejoined the others and saw Captain Dunn put on a glove before picking up the Antigonus family's notebook, still smeared with yellow-brown blood.

Its cover consisted entirely of thick black paper. Despite its age, it showed no sign of softening or decay and radiated an aura of remote antiquity.

It was almost identical to the notebook Klein had seen in his dream. For an instant, he even suspected that opening it would reveal The Fool dressed in splendid robes and an elaborate headdress.

He soon realized he was imagining things.

Dunn opened the notebook to perform a final confirmation. From his poor angle, Klein couldn't make out the writing, but he was certain there was no illustration of The Fool in magnificent attire.

"Ahem. There is nothing wrong with it."

Dunn closed the notebook and held it securely. Then he looked toward Aiur and the others.

"We'll store both the notebook and Sealed Artifact 2-049 behind Tingen's Chanis Gate. They can remain there until all of you recover or Backlund sends someone to retrieve them."

Klein felt both disappointment and relief.

He wanted to examine the Antigonus notebook and discover why the original Klein, Welch, and Naya had died. Yet the ancient object was clearly saturated with misfortune and repeatedly brought catastrophe to those around it. He didn't dare touch it.

Sending it to Church headquarters and sealing it away is probably the best possible outcome.

He quietly released a breath.

"Agreed."

Aiur Harson, Borgia, and Lorotta nodded together. They approached 2-049, strictly monitored one another, and repeatedly woke anyone whose movements became sluggish.

Then they forced the puppet, which had resumed moving, through the opening in the damaged black chest.

"Everything has returned to normal," Aiur said, sounding more relaxed.

Within the dim chest, the puppet wrapped in oil-stained cloth rolled over with a creak. Its clown-painted face aligned itself with the opening and the light beyond.

Beneath its black, pupilless eyes, two nearly imperceptible crimson cracks had appeared.

Meanwhile, Dunn, Leonard, and Klein--who had finally mustered the courage--searched the suited clown's body.

They found paper flowers, handkerchiefs, playing cards, shards of glass, and a variety of other strange props.

Apart from those, he appeared to possess nothing of practical value and no useful clues.

Well, except for a wallet containing seventy or eighty pounds and more than ten soli...

Klein sighed inwardly.

The thought of money prompted him to look down at himself. His expression nearly collapsed.

Rolling across the ground had torn his several-pound formal suit in five or six places. It was also covered in dust, mud, and stains.

Dunn glanced at him, and the corners of his mouth rose.

"Property damaged during a mission can be reimbursed."

Reimbursed!

Hearing the term introduced by Emperor Roselle instantly lifted Klein's spirits.

This suit only needs a thorough cleaning and some careful mending before I can wear it again. It will still look respectable.

When the reimbursement arrives, I can purchase another suit and alternate between them.

Of course, I would never use reimbursement money for anything beyond its intended purpose...

Still, I should consider purchasing dedicated combat clothes--perhaps a black trench coat like the captain's. Less expensive material would cost far less than a tuxedo.

Tsk. Is that why the bastard Leonard avoids formal suits?

"Let Frye examine the body," Dunn said, touching the clown's painted face with his gloved hand. "He may be able to reconstruct the man's original appearance or discover useful clues."

They then searched the innermost warehouse.

The floor was littered with lumps of bloody flesh that looked as though they had been crushed beneath boulders. White bones lay scattered everywhere.

After inspecting the remains, Dunn offered a theory.

"Ray Bieber was using an ancient ritual to absorb power from the notebook, much as we consume a higher-Sequence potion. Such a ritual is extremely dangerous. It must be performed somewhere isolated from all disturbance, and it requires the participant to enter a deep sleep for a period of time. That is probably why he hadn't left Tingen."

Lorotta laughed. Her black hair formed a sharp contrast with her pale face.

"How unfortunate. We woke him before he finished. His displeasure at being disturbed certainly left an impression."

"This is another form of losing control," Dunn told Klein, turning the observation into a lesson.

"Why didn't he leave Tingen and perform the ritual elsewhere?" Klein asked, puzzled.

Aiur laughed and pointed toward his temple.

"People influenced by ancient or malevolent powers are often deficient in this area."

Dunn inhaled, concealing his pain.

"Leonard, you're still in good condition. Remain here and keep ordinary people away. The rest of us will search Ray Bieber's remains for anything important, then return with the recovered items, the Sealed Artifact, and the Antigonus notebook. After that, we'll send Frye, Royale, and the police to handle the site."

Chapter 77: Remnant Items

"All right," Leonard replied casually to Dunn's instruction.

The others left the warehouse and approached the place where Monster Bieber had exploded. Starting from the center, they spread out and searched in widening circles.

Klein looked at the rotting flesh and blood scattered everywhere and suppressed the urge to retch.

"Captain, what exactly are we searching for?"

Dunn didn't look up. His deep gray eyes continued sweeping the ground.

"Ray Bieber awakened before the ritual was complete, lost control, and became a monster. That means he failed to absorb all the Beyonder power supplied by the notebook. A portion of his body should therefore have become an extraordinary material.

"Whenever you encounter something similar, remember to search the remains. You may find something extremely valuable."

So that's how it works...

Klein nodded in understanding.

Then another thought occurred to him.

What if Monster Bieber concentrated the Beyonder power in some indescribable organ? Wouldn't retrieving it be unbelievably awkward? And then someone would have to brew that into a potion...

While Klein's imagination wandered in an unpleasant direction, Borgia's eagle-sharp eyes found something.

"Here. Ahem."

Dunn and the others immediately converged on him. Driven by curiosity, Klein quickened his pace and joined them.

On the ground before Borgia lay a gray-white object about the size of a fist. Its surface was covered in folds and grooves. Though soft in appearance, it seemed strangely resilient, resembling a brain freshly removed from a living creature.

Klein couldn't identify anything extraordinary about it, but Borgia was unlikely to be mistaken. The fact that it had survived the violent explosion intact was evidence enough.

Dunn studied the object carefully and crouched. While continuing to bend and extend his right arm, he reached out with his black-gloved left hand.

The instant he touched it, the gray-white mass collapsed into an extremely viscous liquid.

Aiur Harson removed a square tin case from his pocket, took out the cigarettes inside, and transferred them to another pocket.

He handed the empty case to Dunn with a smile.

"I know. You only smoke a pipe."

Dunn chuckled, accepted the container, and "poured" the sticky gray-white liquid into it for temporary storage.

Once it was sealed, everyone made another cursory sweep of the area.

After confirming that nothing important had been overlooked, they left the scene.

Outside, the horses pawed anxiously at the ground. Clearly terrified, they had nearly torn free of their restraints.

"I'll drive." Borgia covered his mouth and coughed softly.

Aiur smiled and nodded. "You're skilled at calming animals."

Once they boarded the carriage, Dunn, Lorotta, Aiur Harson, and Klein resumed their arm exercises. For a time, none of them spoke.

The horses began trotting, and the carriage rolled away.

Dunn looked at Klein and carefully chose his words.

"I know you're curious about the Antigonus family's notebook. You want to understand what happened."

No, not at all...

Klein instinctively denied it inwardly.

That ancient relic was saturated with misfortune.

Before he could answer, Dunn continued.

"However, I must first report the matter to the Holy Cathedral. Only after they assign the notebook a confidentiality level can we determine whether you're permitted to read it."

"I understand," Klein replied simply.

Dunn continued rhythmically moving his arm while thinking.

"I once promised that if we confirmed Ray Bieber was a descendant of the Antigonus family, I would apply to make you a formal Nighthawk. We have now done more than confirm his identity. We eliminated the monster and disrupted the Secret Order's plan.

"Your performance throughout the operation was exceptional. You personally killed a member of an evil organization. Therefore, I intend to fulfill my promise and submit the application to the Holy Cathedral immediately. Then we'll wait for their approval.

"Ah, I nearly forgot the most important part. I still need to ask whether you agree.

"Mr. Klein Moretti, are you willing to become a formal member of the Tingen Nighthawks? Your salary will increase several times over, to six pounds per week, with further raises each year.

"The Church and the Awwa County Police Department will each pay half. You will also receive the identity of a probationary inspector, which can be extremely useful.

"As a support-oriented Beyonder, you won't always be required to confront enemies directly. You will, however, need to guard Chanis Gate once each week.

"You may not leave Tingen without the squad's permission, and you must keep your true duties secret from your family..."

After Dunn finished explaining the benefits and restrictions, Klein considered the offer for more than ten seconds.

"I wish to become a formal Nighthawk."

Only by doing so could he gain access to deeper mysteries, including more information about the Secret Order.

Reading Emperor Roselle's diaries had altered Klein's thinking.

His unchanging goal remained to master mysticism and discover a path home. But he had added another objective: increase his own strength, gain safer and more complete control over the mysterious realm above the gray fog, and use that power to return.

As Emperor Roselle had written, relying entirely on an external existence was extraordinarily dangerous.

Since becoming a Seer and obtaining Beyonder abilities, Klein had sensed his control over the gray-fog realm increasing. For example, he could now draw another person into the Tarot Gathering.

That naturally made him wonder what further changes might occur after he reached Sequence 8, Sequence 7, or even higher.

Of course, all of that depended on completely resolving the Seer potion's side effects. He couldn't afford to become impatient or reckless.

"Very good. Once the Holy Cathedral approves the application, you'll be one of us." A trace of pleasure entered Dunn's gray eyes.

Aiur Harson, who had listened in silence, spoke next.

"Klein--I hope you don't mind my addressing you by your first name--your performance today was truly remarkable. You killed a Beyonder from the Secret Order, one I suspect may have reached Sequence 7. How did you accomplish it? I find the result almost unbelievable."

At last, the question has come.

Klein had prepared for it long ago. He adopted the expression of someone organizing his memories.

He understood how extraordinary the achievement appeared. The suited clown had run circles around Dunn, Aiur, and Lorotta, yet Klein had killed him alone. None of them were blind or foolish; asking for an explanation had only been a matter of time.

Still, Klein hadn't expected them to wait until now.

Of course. The captain and Mr. Harson were badly injured before, and their condition might have deteriorated at any moment. They postponed any subject that could provoke conflict, lest I react desperately after believing my secret exposed.

Only after I declared my willingness to become a Nighthawk did they feel safe enough to ask. How shrewd. Without visibly communicating, they reached the same tacit decision.

Klein answered as though still reviewing the incident.

"I was extraordinarily lucky. The clown made a fatal error in judgment.

"The explosion threw Sealed Artifact 2-049 near me. It appeared to be five or six meters away, but that was only a rough estimate. Anyone observing the scene could easily conclude that I lay within its effective range.

"I was also dizzy from the explosion. My movements became slow, making it appear as though the artifact had taken control of me.

"At some point, the suited clown approached while invisible. He offered to awaken me if I helped him obtain the Antigonus notebook. As further temptation, he revealed that the Sequence 8 following Seer is Clown.

"He claimed the Secret Order controls the corresponding pathway and said he had once been a Seer himself."

Klein recounted the entire encounter in detail, including his theories about the man's motives. He explained that the clown had probably divined enormous danger in retrieving the notebook personally and therefore changed his plan.

Every detail was true except the foundation supporting them all: Klein had actually fallen under 2-049's control and escaped through his ritual.

"He divined that retrieving the notebook was extremely dangerous?" Lorotta covered her mouth and chuckled. "It certainly was. But the danger turned out to be you.

"His divination was accurate, yet his interpretation led him directly into a fatal situation. What an interesting story."

Klein paused, then nodded solemnly.

"Indeed. Divination never provides perfect clarity. Its ambiguity leaves room for an incorrect interpretation."

I must remember that lesson myself.

Still bending and extending his arm, Dunn leaned back.

"What happened next?"

Klein smiled.

"I pretended to accept the offer and waited for him to awaken me. He didn't dare enter the artifact's effective range, so he remained two or three meters away and tried to prod me with a strange strip of paper.

"I seized it and pulled him toward 2-049. Once the artifact slowed him, I fired repeatedly and killed him.

"It's rather embarrassing, actually. Even from only two or three meters away, I wasn't confident that I could hit him directly."

Aiur nodded slowly.

"Given his evasive ability, such a short distance still offered no guarantee. You might have wounded him without striking a vital point, which would only have made the situation worse.

"Your decision was flawless--exceptional, even. In your position, I may not have handled it any better."

He asked no further questions. Once the suited clown entered 2-049's range, his fate had effectively been sealed. He was little more than a stationary target.

"The Sequence following Seer is Clown..." Dunn suddenly sighed. "How strange."

Chapter 78: Trauma

Aiur Harson nodded in agreement.

"Exactly. It's difficult to imagine that the Sequence following Seer would be Clown. Under ordinary logic, no one would associate the two."

"Is it really that unusual?" Lorotta covered her mouth and yawned. "Quite a few pathways appear to have little connection between their different Sequences."

Her injuries were clearly more severe than the others'. Even the Goddess's Gaze could no longer preserve her usual energy.

"No, Lorotta, this is different," Aiur said with a sigh and shake of his head. "Even when other potion names appear unrelated, one can usually discover a common principle by considering them from another angle. I can't find any connection whatsoever between Seer and Clown."

Klein listened to the discussion and laughed.

"There is one common point."

"What is it?" Aiur asked curiously.

Even Dunn's rhythmic arm movements slowed slightly.

Klein answered without hesitation.

"Both Seers and clowns can be found in a circus."

"..."

Aiur, Dunn, and Lorotta stared at him in silence.

"Pfft!" Lorotta recovered first and burst into laughter. "An excellent answer. I like young men with your sense of humor."

Aiur smiled and shook his head.

"Gentlemen capable of self-deprecation are becoming increasingly rare in this age. Fortunately, we met one today."

Do you think I enjoy mocking myself? I simply can't find any genuine connection between the two.

Klein smiled bitterly.

"I only hope the later potions aren't named Beast Tamer, Acrobat, or Magician. At that point, the pathway really would become a circus."

And a one-man circus at that...

"Haha!"

Dunn and the others laughed, filling the carriage with a cheerful atmosphere.

The carriage traveled directly to Zouteland Street. Since Klein was uninjured, he entered the Blackthorn Security Company first.

"Goddess! What happened to you? Why do you look like that?" Rozanne exclaimed when she saw him.

Klein looked down at his torn, filthy suit and felt another pang of distress.

"Unexpected things always happen during a mission. Fortunately, the Goddess watched over us, and everything ended well."

"Praise the Lady!"

Rozanne reverently traced the crimson moon across her chest.

Before Klein could continue, she asked, "Do we need to hide on the third floor again? Is the Sealed Artifact truly that dangerous?"

"Believe me, it's far more dangerous than you can imagine," Klein replied, still shaken by the memory.

Without his even more mysterious luck-enhancement ritual, he would have died at the proverbial hands of 2-049.

"Goddess..."

Rozanne's lips moved as though she had a million questions. Remembering that the captain and the others were waiting below, she restrained herself and hurried to tell Mrs. Orianna and the other civilian employees to move upstairs.

The neighboring offices were either Church property or occupied by devout clergy who possessed a vague understanding of Blackthorn's true function.

Once every civilian had cleared the area, Klein didn't stop at the recreation room to notify the other Nighthawks. He immediately went back and helped escort Sealed Artifact 2-049, Monster Bieber's remnant material, and the Antigonus notebook to the second floor.

Dunn pushed through the partition and opened the recreation-room door. Two Nighthawks were playing Gwent inside.

"Frye, Royale, proceed immediately to Tyrell Warehouse at the harbor and help Leonard handle the aftermath."

"All right."

Royale, with her raven-black hair and cold expression, rose first.

Corpse Collector Frye stood after her. His black hair, blue eyes, and pale skin gave him an aura of coldness and gloom.

They put down their cards and left the recreation room. Both paused briefly after passing through the partition.

"Wait," Dunn called, meeting their expectations.

Sleepless Royale turned her head without expression.

"What else?"

"Notify the police. Have them close the road and prevent anyone from approaching until you've processed the scene and transported the body," Dunn said, smacking his forehead.

"All right."

Royale turned and took two steps, then stopped again.

She looked back, blinked, and asked coldly, "Captain, is that everything?"

"Yes," Dunn replied categorically.

Royale gave an almost imperceptible nod and continued toward the entrance.

Frye maintained his measured pace beside her.

Then Dunn added, "Remember to tell Rozanne, Mrs. Orianna, and the others that they may come downstairs."

"No problem," Frye replied calmly, displaying no visible emotion.

Klein watched them leave and go upstairs before quietly sighing in relief. Then he followed Dunn and the others underground toward Chanis Gate.

As Dunn signaled Sleepless Kenley to open the gate, he instructed Klein, "Go to the armory and bring Old Neil. We need his ritualistic magic to treat our injuries."

The medicine's effects were already fading, and Dunn's energy was visibly declining.

"All right," Klein replied. Before the captain could continue, he added, "I'll watch the armory in Old Neil's place, requisition at least twenty demon-hunting bullets, and wait patiently for the Holy Cathedral's approval instead of trying to satisfy my curiosity about the Antigonus notebook."

"..."

Dunn was momentarily speechless.

Klein smiled. "Captain, is there anything else?"

Dunn shook his head without saying a word.

Klein retrieved his cane, turned, and walked away. At the intersection, he entered the armory and gave Old Neil, who was drinking plain water, a general account of what had happened.

"He lost control and became a monster... and you killed a Beyonder?"

Old Neil rapidly cleared his desk.

"It sounds like the script of a stage play."

Still muttering, he rounded the desk and headed directly for the corridor without waiting for Klein's response.

Klein asked curiously, "Mr. Neil, doesn't the Church possess genuine restorative medicine? Why is ritualistic magic necessary?"

"No medicine made from ordinary ingredients can provide the lasting restorative effects of a ritual. Extraordinary materials are exceedingly rare, and most aren't suitable for medicine," Old Neil explained casually.

"You know about the Goddess's Gaze, yes? Immediately after it is created through a ritual, it is a genuine and highly effective restorative medicine. But its power diminishes with every passing minute until very little remains."

"I see..." Klein nodded, disappointed.

As a former keyboard warrior and enthusiastic gamer, he naturally longed for a magical potion capable of instantly restoring injuries.

He watched Old Neil leave, then sat in the armory and savored a tranquility he hadn't experienced for a long time.

Within that silence, however, the suited clown's death returned to him.

He remembered pulling the trigger without emotion, the terrible wounds, and the blood spraying through the air.

Klein shuddered with discomfort. He stood, sat down again, then repeated the process. Between attempts, he paced restlessly back and forth.

Phew...

He breathed out and decided to occupy himself so he wouldn't continue dwelling on those images.

Klein removed his silk top hat and formal coat, then used a handkerchief and brush to clean away the dirt and mud.

After an uncertain amount of time, he heard Old Neil's distinctive footsteps. The old man's habit of landing heavily on his heels produced an unmistakable sound as he ambled down the corridor.

"How exhausting..." Old Neil complained as he entered.

He glanced at Klein. "Tell everyone not to come in for the next hour. I need to rest."

"Why not rest upstairs while I continue watching the armory?" Klein kindly suggested.

Old Neil shook his head.

"It's too noisy up there. Rozanne is incapable of remaining silent."

"All right."

Klein didn't insist. He put on his coat and hat, picked up his cane, and returned to the corridor, leaving the armory door slightly ajar.

Tap. Tap. Tap.

He walked slowly along the empty passage and suddenly noticed several rooms he had never seen before.

"There's a concealed door here..."

Klein stopped around a bend and looked into one of the rooms.

Corpse Collector Frye had already returned. He was carefully examining a completely dissected body.

A corpse?

Klein's heart stirred. Gathering his courage, he approached and knocked lightly on the open door.

Knock! Knock! Knock!

Frye stopped working and turned his icy blue eyes toward him.

"Sorry to disturb you. I only wanted to know whether that is the body of a Beyonder," Klein asked, controlling his voice.

"Yes."

Frye's lips opened and closed, producing only the single word.

Klein looked past him and saw the familiar terrible wound in the corpse's forehead.

It's the suited clown...

He quietly exhaled.

"Did you discover anything?"

"No," Frye replied with abnormal brevity.

The atmosphere immediately became awkward.

Just as Klein prepared to excuse himself, Frye spoke voluntarily.

"If the experience troubles you, come inside and look. You'll discover that it is only a corpse."

Is he worried that my first kill traumatized me?

Klein considered the suggestion and nodded.

"All right."

He entered and approached the long table draped in white cloth.

The red, yellow, and white paint had been cleaned from the suited clown's face, revealing an unfamiliar and unremarkable man in his thirties, with black hair and a high nose bridge.

Frye walked to a square table against the wall, picked up a pencil and sheet of paper, then returned to the body and began drawing.

Klein watched curiously. Frye was sketching the dead man's head.

It didn't take him long to finish. A lifelike portrait filled the page. Compared with the corpse, it differed only in having intact skin and blue eyes.

What remarkable talent...

Klein marveled aloud.

"I never expected you to be such a skilled artist."

"Before becoming a Nighthawk, I dreamed of being a painter," Frye replied with complete calm.

"Then why didn't you pursue it?" Klein asked curiously.

Frye set down the pencil and lifted the portrait.

"My father was a priest of the Goddess. He wanted me to enter the clergy. It was a respectable occupation."

"You became a priest?" Klein asked in surprise.

Given Frye's personality and the cold, gloomy aura surrounding him, Klein found the idea difficult to imagine.

"Yes. I performed the role reasonably well." The corners of Frye's mouth lifted slightly despite his chilly expression. "Later, I encountered certain events and eventually became a Nighthawk."

Klein had no intention of prying into his private history.

"Since you were once a priest of the Goddess, why didn't you choose Sleepless?"

"For personal reasons," Frye answered frankly. "And Madam Daly provided an excellent example."

Klein nodded and was about to change the subject when Frye said, "Watch this room for me. I need to deliver the portrait to the captain immediately. Closing and reopening the concealed door is troublesome."

"All right."

Although Klein felt uneasy about remaining alone with a corpse, he forced himself to agree.

After Frye left, silence filled the room. The corpse lay motionless, and Klein's heart felt heavy.

He took several breaths. Determined to overcome his fear, he approached the long table.

The suited clown lay silently with his pale face and tightly closed eyes. He displayed no sign of breathing. Apart from the gruesome wound, he radiated the distinctive coldness of the dead.

Klein observed him for a while, and his emotions gradually settled.

Then his gaze caught a peculiar mark on the man's wrist.

Summoning his courage, Klein reached out and attempted to turn the arm for a clearer look.

The corpse's icy skin had barely touched his fingertips when its lifeless hand shot upward and clamped around his wrist.

It gripped him with terrifying force.

Chapter 79: Another Murmuring

Every hair on Klein's body stood upright as the icy hand tightened around his wrist.

He instinctively jerked backward, desperately trying to break free.

The grip felt impossibly heavy. Klein summoned every ounce of strength in his body and wrenched his arm toward himself.

Bam!

The naked, pallid corpse was dragged sideways and toppled from the autopsy table.

Yet its cold white fingers remained locked around Klein's wrist.

For a moment, he lost the ability to think. His only impulse was to draw his revolver and fill the body with bullets.

His dominant hand was trapped, however. He threw aside his black cane and fumbled desperately for the revolver in his holster, but couldn't pull it free.

Then the corpse's eyelids rose, revealing a pair of calm blue eyes.

Its mouth moved.

"Hornacis... Hornacis... Hornacis..."

The repeated word completely unnerved Klein.

The fingers around his wrist gradually loosened and fell limp.

The suited clown's eyes closed again, as though nothing had happened.

If the pale corpse weren't lying on the stone floor, Klein might have believed he had suffered an illusion.

He staggered several steps backward, most of his body trembling from shock and terror.

Phew... Phew...

Klein gasped until his thoughts slowly returned to normal. He stared fearfully at the corpse on the floor.

Finally, he drew his revolver and retreated from the room one careful step at a time. Only after confirming that the body remained motionless did he glance at the wrist of the hand holding the weapon.

Five deep red finger marks had been imprinted on his skin, silent proof of what had occurred.

As Klein recovered, profanity flooded his thoughts.

Damn it! That nearly frightened me to death!

After panting for more than ten seconds, he began mentally arranging the facts to regain his composure.

He carefully reviewed every detail of the encounter.

Although he didn't understand why the suited clown had briefly "returned to life," one fact stood out clearly: the corpse had repeated the word Hornacis.

"Hornacis again..."

Klein frowned.

"The Antigonus family's notebook records a Nation of the Evernight in the Hornacis mountain range. During Cogitation and Spiritual Vision, I sometimes hear things I shouldn't be able to hear, and one of those murmurs is 'Hornacis.'

"Does the answer to all these mysteries lie in that mountain range? There may also be an enormous danger hidden there--perhaps an evil god sealed within, using various forms of attraction to secure its freedom."

While considering the possibility, Klein cautiously reentered the room and prodded the corpse several times to confirm that it was truly dead.

He didn't want Frye to return and find the room in chaos, so he gathered his courage and lifted the body back onto the autopsy table.

His heart remained in his throat throughout the process. The slightest movement threatened to snap his taut nerves, and the corpse's icy touch was especially revolting.

After completing the task with great difficulty, Klein remembered why he had approached in the first place. He focused on the strange mark on the suited clown's wrist.

At some unknown point, the brand had detached from the skin and contracted into a spherical mass of blood tinged with blue.

The blood sphere was about the size of a thumb and floated silently in midair, openly defying the laws of physics.

"What is that?" Klein murmured, not daring to touch it.

He had no intention of concealing the object. First, he didn't know whether it was beneficial or dangerous. Second, Frye had already examined the body and must have noticed the mark on the wrist. He might even know what the floating sphere was.

And if Frye doesn't recognize it, reporting it to the captain and allowing the Nighthawks to investigate is still much wiser than experimenting blindly.

That was one of the advantages of belonging to an organization: he could make full use of its collective strength.

Klein waited nervously for several minutes before Frye returned, black-haired, blue-eyed, and thin-lipped.

He noticed the floating blood immediately and asked the same question Klein had asked himself.

"What is that?"

"I have no idea."

Klein shook his head honestly and recounted everything that had happened without concealing a detail.

"The mark detached and became a sphere of blood..."

Frye nodded thoughtfully.

"The corpses of Beyonders frequently undergo unusual transformations."

He looked up at Klein.

"Bring the captain here. Tell him what the corpse murmured as well."

"All right."

Klein was already eager to leave.

"You don't need to return with him," Frye added. "I don't believe you'll enjoy what comes next."

As he spoke, he picked up a silver surgical knife.

Still shaken, Klein nodded.

"I was hoping you would say that."

He retrieved his cane, put on his hat, and made his way toward Chanis Gate on slightly unsteady legs. He found Captain Dunn, who no longer appeared weak, in the guardroom.

After hearing Klein's account, Dunn gave an almost imperceptible nod.

"I'll report this to our superiors and allow the Holy Cathedral to decide how to proceed. Perhaps they will send someone to investigate the highest peak of the Hornacis mountain range."

Klein briefly acknowledged the answer. Seeing only Dunn and Sleepless Kenley in the room, he casually asked, "Are Mr. Aiur and the others resting?"

Dunn nodded.

"Aiur and Borgia are at Saint Selena Cathedral. Lorotta is probably searching for a coffee shop."

"A coffee shop? Madam Lorotta hasn't recovered from her injuries, has she?" Klein asked in surprise.

Dunn rubbed the bridge of his nose and laughed.

"Lorotta has three great interests: coffee, desserts, and maidservants. She claims all three accelerate her recovery."

"Maidservants?" Klein asked, perplexed.

Does Madam Lorotta have some particular preference?

Dunn shook his head helplessly.

"She likes maidservants. Yes, that's correct. More specifically, she prefers those with large breasts."

"She is certainly unusual."

Klein had no idea what expression he ought to make.

Dunn wasted no more time and left the guardroom. Klein watched his back and silently waited for him to turn around.

From the corner of his eye, he saw Kenley take out his pocket watch and open it.

Three, two, one...

The instant Klein finished counting, Dunn stopped and looked back.

"One more thing I forgot. You experienced a great deal today, Klein. Once you relax, the exhaustion will strike. There's no need to remain here this afternoon. Go home and rest. Tomorrow, I'll submit the application detailing your losses.

"And don't torment yourself over killing a Beyonder. By killing him, you saved many future victims."

"Actually, I'm already feeling much better," Klein replied with a quiet breath.

Dunn nodded and turned away, then immediately slapped his forehead.

"I also gave the Beyonder's portrait to Leonard. He and the police will conduct the follow-up investigation. That man must have hired carriages, purchased food, and found somewhere to stay.

"Wherever a person goes, whatever he touches, and whatever he leaves behind--even unknowingly--will become a silent witness against him. Emperor Roselle's words are exceptionally sensible."

"Yes," Klein replied, slightly dazed.

After Dunn disappeared down the corridor, Klein left the guardroom and slowly climbed toward the second floor.

Halfway up the stairs, he remembered something that sent another wave of fear through him.

The suited clown claimed that the Secret Order controls the pathway following Seer. Even if he exaggerated and the organization lacks the higher-Sequence formulas, it must at least possess the lower ones.

That means the Secret Order has multiple Seers.

Could they divine that I killed the clown and secretly seek revenge?

Even if they can't defeat the Nighthawks, they could easily target me--a Seer with no direct combat abilities.

Klein stopped in the stairwell and considered the danger seriously.

Before long, he realized he was worrying unnecessarily.

First, the Secret Order doesn't know the identities of every Nighthawk.

Second, even if it knows one or two, a civilian employee like me would never appear on its list.

Third, under the present circumstances, only a true Prophet could divine the murderer's identity from nothing.

Relieved, Klein left the Blackthorn Security Company and took a public carriage back to Daffodil Street.

Although he had missed lunch, he possessed no appetite.

Once inside his bedroom, he removed his damaged suit and half top hat, climbed into bed, and attempted to sleep.

His mind remained painfully active, and his entire body refused to relax. Yet he no longer replayed the moment he shot the suited clown. Instead, he remembered moving the corpse--and the horrifying instant when it grabbed him.

His discomfort over killing had faded into something closer to disgust.

"That was probably Frye's intention. He wanted me to approach the body, face it directly, and overcome my trauma.

"The original trauma is gone, but now I have an entirely new one..."

Klein laughed self-deprecatingly as his nerves gradually settled.

He didn't know when he finally fell asleep. When he woke, his stomach was growling in protest.

"I feel as though I could eat an entire horse."

Klein looked toward the setting sun, which had set the western sky aflame.

After changing into old but comfortable casual clothes, he hurried downstairs. Before he could decide what to cook, he heard a key enter the front door.

Melissa...

The corners of his mouth rose.

Since she had begun taking public transportation, his sister no longer returned home late.

The lock turned, and Melissa entered carrying a bag filled with books and stationery.

She glanced toward the kitchen.

"Klein, a letter arrived for you. It's from your mentor."

A letter from my mentor?

Klein was briefly confused before remembering.

Right. I wrote to him asking about the history of the highest peak in the Hornacis mountain range.

Chapter 80: A Banquet Invitation

After dinner, a comfortably full Klein lounged on the living-room sofa and opened his mentor's letter with a small letter opener.

Melissa sat at the dining table beneath the gas lamp, working diligently through a problem in her textbook. Benson occupied an armchair, reading Accountancy for Beginners.

The letter contained three pages. Klein read them with a mixture of apprehension and anticipation.

"I was delighted to receive your letter. It reminded me of the pleasant years we recently spent together. Unfortunately, Welch and Naya have left us forever...

"I attended their funerals separately and witnessed the anguish of their parents. Both were young adults who should have enjoyed bright and beautiful futures...

"Fate is eternally unpredictable. No one knows what the next moment will bring. The older I become and the more I experience, the more keenly I perceive humanity's weakness and helplessness.

"Regarding the history of the highest peak in the Hornacis mountain range, I recall that the archaeologist Mr. John Joseph once published a monograph on the subject. It includes an account of his expedition to the peak, where he discovered several ancient structures more than a thousand years old.

"To the shame of historians and archaeologists alike, we have been unable to date the ruins precisely. We can offer only a rough estimate based on the architectural style, characteristics of the murals, and the few surviving texts we have deciphered.

"It is difficult to imagine human beings living atop such a high mountain. Yet Mr. Joseph provides ample evidence that they developed a civilization of their own.

"The details are too extensive to describe in a letter. I suggest borrowing the monograph from Deweyville Library. Sir Deweyville's generous donations have given it a larger collection than the municipal library.

"The title is Research into the Ruins of the Hornacis Main Peak, published by Loen Publishing House.

"Several relevant papers have also appeared in the journals New Archaeology and Archaeological Review. The specific issues and volume numbers are..."

Klein read every word, silently repeating the titles of the monograph and papers until he had memorized them.

Then he found a sheet of paper, an envelope, and a fountain pen and wrote a letter expressing his gratitude.

"Melissa, please mail this for me. Here is the postage."

Klein placed the sealed envelope and more than enough money on the table beside his sister.

Melissa glanced at the coins and pursed her lips.

"Klein, stamps don't cost that much."

"No, they don't. But a young woman ought to have some spending money," he replied with a smile. "I believe Selena has mentioned that before."

When Melissa appeared ready to object, he quickly added, "You can use it to buy materials and tools."

"Tools..."

Melissa repeated the word several times, then returned her gaze to the textbook.

"All right."

She gave an almost imperceptible nod.

The corners of Klein's mouth rose as he walked briskly back to the sofa.

"Excellent persuasion," Benson said, giving him a thumbs-up while suppressing a laugh. "You identified Melissa's weakness precisely."

Klein cleared his throat and adopted a solemn expression.

"How, then, should I persuade you? Your independent studies ought to emphasize language and classical literature, though basic mathematics and logic are equally important."

Based on the curricula of public and grammar schools, along with existing university entrance examinations, Klein felt confident about the subjects any future civil-service examination would emphasize.

Benson touched his receding hairline and smiled self-deprecatingly.

"Those books make me feel like a curly-haired baboon."

"But they're useful," Klein insisted with a firm smile.

At that moment, Melissa put down her pen, rose, and approached the sofa.

"Benson, Klein, this Sunday is Selena's birthday. She and her parents have invited all three of us to a banquet at their home. Are you free?"

"I should be," Klein replied after thinking.

The occasion would give him an opportunity to meet his sister's friends. If something ever happened to Melissa, he wouldn't be completely ignorant about the people around her.

"So am I." Benson combed his thinning hair with his fingers. "It seems we'll need to choose a birthday present for Miss Selena."

Klein smiled.

"We should leave that to Melissa. She knows Miss Selena far better than we do. Our duty as gentlemen is simply to pay for it."

"That is the first time I've heard anyone describe laziness so elegantly," Benson said, shaking his head with a chuckle.

Klein returned his smile.

"That is the purpose of studying language and classical literature."

"..."

Benson hadn't expected the conversation to circle back so quickly and was momentarily speechless.

...

The following morning, Klein wore his inexpensive formal suit and carried his black, silver-inlaid cane up the stairs to the Blackthorn Security Company. His tuxedo was already at the tailor's for repairs.

He was about to greet Rozanne when Captain Dunn emerged from behind the partition.

"Good morning, Klein. Did you sleep well?" Dunn asked with concern.

Klein answered honestly.

"Better than I expected. I didn't have any nightmares. The memory still leaves me feeling heavy and somewhat disgusted, though."

"Very good. I'm relieved to hear that." Dunn nodded and smiled.

After a brief exchange about the weather, he raised a more important matter.

"The Holy Cathedral has answered my telegram. Aiur, Lorotta, and the others are to escort Sealed Artifact 2-049 and the Antigonus family's notebook back to Backlund immediately. Another Nighthawk traveled here by steam train yesterday afternoon to reinforce them.

"I believe they have already departed."

They've already left?

Does that mean I'm finally free of the terrifying Antigonus notebook?

Klein was stunned. The resolution felt almost unreal, as though he were dreaming.

That was far easier than I expected...

Surely there won't be any further consequences.

After several seconds of silence, Klein traced the crimson moon over his chest.

"May the Goddess bless them with a safe journey."

Dunn put on his hat and gestured toward the entrance.

"I need to patrol Raphael Cemetery. Ah, I nearly forgot. Leonard and the police made progress in their investigation. They found the carriage driver who transported the Secret Order members and identified their temporary residence in Tingen.

"Unfortunately, they were extremely cautious and left no useful clues."

"As one would expect from an ancient secret organization," Klein replied ruefully.

Dunn nodded and turned toward the door.

Three seconds later, he stopped and looked back.

"One more thing. The Holy Cathedral will need another two or three days to respond to your application for formal membership. That matter is handled by a different department from the one responsible for the Antigonus notebook. Their standards of efficiency are not the same."

"I understand," Klein replied sincerely.

At the same time, he silently supplemented the captain's reminder.

Remember to submit my reimbursement request today!

After Dunn left, brown-haired Rozanne exclaimed, "Goddess! Klein, are you becoming a formal member? You haven't even worked here for a month!"

Klein smiled.

"Once I consumed the Seer potion, it became only a matter of time."

"That does make sense..."

Rozanne stared blankly for several seconds, then suddenly sighed.

"I was praying you would finish your mysticism lessons so we could add you to the armory-duty schedule, but now... Goddess, I have to take a shift every other day! I'm not a Sleepless. Think of my skin, my health, my state of mind. Goddess, save me!"

"Shouldn't you already be accustomed to that schedule? Before I arrived, it was always you, Bredt, and Old Neil taking turns, wasn't it?" Klein asked, puzzled.

Rozanne shook her head dejectedly.

"There used to be four of us, and five before that. Unfortunately, Kenley chose to become a Sleepless. Viola didn't renew her contract last month and joined the Khoy Noel Machinery Company.
