"She's exceptionally gifted at inventing things. She only lacked money and opportunity, and five years as a civilian employee allowed her to save enough."

Rozanne suddenly glanced at Klein and covered her mouth as she laughed.

"I've thought of the perfect solution. Marry as soon as possible, then accidentally reveal the existence of Beyonders to your wife. That would be only a minor breach, so the punishment couldn't be too severe. After all, no one can lie forever to the person who shares their bed.

"Once she knows, introduce her to us and have her become a civilian employee. It's a perfect plan!"

The corners of Klein's mouth twitched.

"Miss Rozanne, you could find a husband yourself. That would be even easier. I'm sure you possess adequate means to reveal the secret to him."

Rozanne's eyes widened, and her mouth fell open.

"How could I possibly do that? Marriage is a serious matter. I would need to choose carefully and observe him for a long time to make certain he's suitable."

That isn't what you said a moment ago...

Klein didn't bother debating the contradiction. He exchanged a little more casual conversation, said goodbye, and headed underground.

In the armory, he found Old Neil struggling with his hand-ground coffee and sat down to wait patiently.

"You'll be a formal member soon, won't you?" Old Neil asked casually while filtering the drink.

"The captain said it will take another two or three days. We still don't know whether the Holy Cathedral will approve the request," Klein replied frankly.

"Heh." Old Neil chuckled. "The Holy Cathedral almost never rejects an application like this, particularly when the candidate is already a Beyonder."

He turned toward Klein with an amused expression.

"You should prepare yourself. Every new Nighthawk must complete one ritual of initiation: an independent mission.

"Of course, Dunn will choose the easiest and simplest assignment available for a novice--especially since you're a support-oriented Seer."

Chapter 81: Finally Meeting

"I have to complete a mission alone before becoming a formal member?" Klein asked in surprise. "We might not receive any assignments this week, and the next one may not be simple."

Does that mean becoming an official Nighthawk could take another month or two? And only then would my salary increase...

Old Neil sniffed his coffee and glanced sideways at him.

"It's merely an initiation ritual among the Nighthawks. We stand at the forefront of Beyonder danger and don't want teammates who behave like children requiring constant care. It won't affect the salary or privileges you receive as a formal member."

So it's simply a ritual for earning the other Nighthawks' recognition.

But Mr. Neil, why did you specifically emphasize that it wouldn't delay my formal salary? Was my concern really that obvious?

Klein touched his face and smiled awkwardly.

"Must it be a Beyonder-related mission?"

"Normally, yes. But your performance yesterday was exceptional. You used your wits to kill a Beyonder who was at least Sequence 8. I'm certain Frye, Royale, and the others already recognize your abilities. Dunn may therefore assign you an ordinary mission instead."

Old Neil suddenly sighed.

"Your salary will multiply several times over. I'll never experience an increase like that again in my life."

Klein chuckled and changed the subject to his pathway.

"Mr. Neil, do you think Clown truly is the Sequence 8 following Seer?"

In retrospect, the confidential description did seem to fit.

A profession skilled at fighting through cunning and artifice...

"I can't guarantee it, but I believe it's highly probable. First, it matches the archived description: agile movement and a deceptive style of combat. Second, other pathways contain similarly unexpected transitions. Do you know which Sequence 8 follows Mystery Pryer?" Old Neil asked with a chuckle.

"No. The Church records available to me didn't include it," Klein replied honestly.

Old Neil laughed.

"I'm close friends with two old fellows in the Machinery Hivemind. They once mentioned it as a joke. Mystery Pryer's Sequence 8 is Melee Scholar.

"Did you hear that? Melee Scholar! Goddess, I dislike close combat. It doesn't suit the image of a Mystery Pryer at all."

Klein considered it.

"I can understand the logic. Mystery Pryers pursue the mysteries underlying all things, and close combat is evidently one of those mysteries."

Old Neil finished his hand-ground coffee.

"All right, enough wasted time. Let's continue your mysticism education. You still need to master many forms of ritualistic magic, along with the creation of amulets and charms."

"All right."

Klein sat down and organized his schedule for the day.

This morning, I'll study mysticism, review historical records, and submit my reimbursement claim. After lunch, I'll practice at the Shooting Club. Then I'll visit Deweyville Library in Golden Indus Borough and try to borrow the monograph and journals concerning the Hornacis main peak.

If time remains after that, I'll visit the Divination Club. I can't neglect my acting.

Once the reimbursement is approved, I can buy a new suit on the way home.

Yes... Tomorrow morning, I'll requisition the materials needed to make protective amulets for Benson and Melissa.

...

In an elegantly decorated dining room beneath a chandelier, several friends were celebrating Joyce Meyer's survival and return to Tingen.

"We all read the newspaper. The written account alone was terrifying," said a man with short stubble along his chin. "Joyce, I can't believe you endured such an ordeal.

"A toast. The tragedy is behind us, and sunlight shines upon us once more. Praise the Exalted Steam."

Joyce and his fiancee, Anna, raised their glasses and clinked them against those of their friends. Everyone swallowed the small amount of champagne remaining.

A young woman with a petite nose and coiled brown hair looked toward Joyce, then glanced at Anna.

"Anna was terribly worried. I suspect she cried every night. Whenever I invited her to afternoon tea, she was absentminded and distracted. Thank goodness you've returned. Otherwise, I believe she might have simply wasted away."

"If Anna experienced anything similar, I would be the same--perhaps worse."

Joyce, with his aquiline nose, gave his fiancee a tender look.

Anna wasn't accustomed to displaying emotion in public. She shifted her gaze to the opposite end of the table.

"Bogda, why have you kept your head lowered all evening? I can tell you're in a terrible mood."

The young woman with the petite nose answered for him.

"Bogda is ill. His physician found a serious problem with his liver. Medicine can reduce the pain but won't cure the condition. He needs surgery."

"Lord! When did this happen?" Anna and Joyce asked with surprise and concern.

Bogda was a short-haired young man with a sallow complexion. The usual brilliance of his red eyes had faded to a dim glow.

"Last week. Since Joyce hadn't returned, I asked Irene not to tell you," he explained with a bitter smile.

Joyce asked solemnly, "Have you chosen a date for the operation?"

Bogda's expression shifted several times.

"No. I still haven't decided. You know what surgeons are like--practically butchers. The patient becomes a slab of meat on a chopping block, and they cut however they please.

"I've read the reports. They even use axes for amputations! Lord, I may very well die on the operating table."

"If you delay too long, surgery may no longer be able to save you," the stubbled man tried to persuade him.

Anna suddenly interjected.

"Bogda, perhaps you should have a divination. If the result says the operation will go smoothly, undergo it as soon as possible. If the result is unfavorable, seek another solution with the fortune-teller's guidance.

"I know a genuine and mysterious fortune-teller--no, I should call him a Seer. I believe he can help you."

"Truly?" Bogda asked, openly doubtful.

Their other friends wore similar expressions.

"Yes." Anna nodded without hesitation. "I hired him to divine Joyce's circumstances. Afterward, he told me to return home because my fiance was already waiting there.

"I was just as doubtful as all of you. But when I arrived, Joyce truly had returned."

"I can confirm every word," Joyce added.

He didn't mention asking Klein to interpret his dream. The police had informed him that Tris remained at large, and he needed to keep the matter secret to avoid retaliation.

"Lord, that's unbelievable!"

"Can divination truly be so miraculous?"

Amid their exclamations, Bogda thought deeply for a moment.

"Perhaps I should try it. Anna, Joyce, can you tell me the Seer's name and where to find him?"

Anna sighed in relief.

"You've made a wise choice.

"The Seer works at the Divination Club on Howes Street.

"His name is Klein Moretti."

...

Golden Indus Borough, Deweyville Library.

Using the letter of introduction from his mentor, Klein successfully obtained a borrowing card.

Turning the little card over in his hand, he approached the librarians.

"Do you have Research into the Ruins of the Hornacis Main Peak, published by Loen Publishing House?"

One librarian responded immediately.

"Please wait while I check the catalog."

He turned toward a cabinet of drawers, opened the one corresponding to Hornacis, and searched through an ordered series of index cards.

After a careful inspection, he shook his head.

"I'm sorry, sir. The library doesn't possess that book."

"That's unfortunate," Klein replied, unable to hide his disappointment.

It seems I'll need to write to Loen Publishing House or visit Khoy University.

At the same time, he silently lamented the antiquated state of library management in this world.

What you need is a computer. Unfortunately, I can't build one...

Mocking himself, Klein asked, "Do you have the relevant issues of New Archaeology and Archaeological Review?"

"We do," the librarian confirmed. "A gentleman returned them recently."

He consulted the corresponding cards and pointed Klein toward the appropriate shelves.

Klein found the journals, selected the issues mentioned by his mentor, and took a seat beside a window.

In the bright afternoon sunlight, he quietly began to read.

"Ancient ruins exist not only on the highest peak of the Hornacis range, but throughout the surrounding forests, valleys, and gentle slopes...

"These sites consist of lofty domes and enormous stone pillars. In truth, they can only be described as magnificent...

"I cannot help wondering how the original inhabitants quarried and shaped such stones. For the sake of argument, let us suppose the material was extracted locally and didn't need to be transported up the mountain...

"A peculiar pattern emerges: the ruins increase in scale as one approaches the main peak. Yet, surprisingly, no structures stand upon the summit itself. According to our hypothesis, that location should contain palaces surpassing ordinary human construction--divine halls intended for sacrifice..."

Palaces beyond human construction... Divine halls used for sacrifice...

Could that be the palace I saw in my dream?

While Klein considered the possibility, footsteps approached from the distance.

He raised his head and saw a familiar face, one frequently printed in the newspapers.

The man had a square face, thick eyebrows, a firm nose, short dark-blond hair, azure eyes, and tightly pressed lips. He was one of Tingen's most prominent citizens--a philanthropist, entrepreneur, and the owner of the library.

Sir Deweyville.

Beside him walked the middle-aged butler Klein had met before.

Klein watched them approach from more than ten meters away. Driven by curiosity, he raised his right hand and lightly tapped his glabella twice.

Chapter 82: Herb Store

Colors surfaced around the people in the library as Klein activated Spiritual Vision. He casually examined Sir Deweyville.

He's physically healthy, with almost no hidden ailments... But his emotional state is terrible. Beneath the dullness, there is a profound weakness.

Is his mind exhausted? Does he suffer from poor sleep? Yet the purple aura around his head appears perfectly normal...

Klein silently analyzed the sight until Sir Deweyville walked away and left the library.

He withdrew his gaze, pinched his forehead, and sighed inwardly.

Being a wealthy magnate isn't easy either...

He paid the matter no further attention and returned to the journals before him.

After reading every relevant article, Klein found few useful clues. He could confirm only several broad facts.

First, an ancient kingdom had once existed in and around the Hornacis mountain range, dating back at least fifteen hundred years.

Second, its architecture emphasized enormous scale and grandeur. The inhabitants left numerous murals, from which archaeologists inferred a belief that the Evernight protected the loved ones of the dead.

Finally, symbols representing the Evernight appeared throughout the ruins, but they differed clearly from the modern Sacred Emblem of the Evernight Goddess.

"If I ever have the opportunity--no, even if the opportunity presents itself, I will never go there."

Klein clenched his teeth and vowed not to court death.

He organized the journals, returned them to their proper shelves, put on his hat, picked up his cane, and left Deweyville Library.

...

At the Divination Club, Bogda approached the attractive receptionist.

"I would like a divination."

Angelica smiled politely.

"Do you have a particular fortune-teller in mind, or would you prefer to review our album and select whoever seems best suited to your needs?"

Bogda pressed a hand against the right side of his abdomen and silently endured a spasm of pain.

"I would like Mr. Klein Moretti to perform the divination."

"Unfortunately, Mr. Moretti isn't here today," Angelica replied uncertainly.

Bogda fell silent. After pacing several steps, he asked, "When will he be available?"

"No one knows. He has his own affairs to manage. From what I've observed, he usually visits on Monday afternoons," Angelica said after some thought.

"I see."

Bogda's face darkened, and he turned to leave.

"Sir, you could choose another fortune-teller," Angelica hurriedly suggested, trying not to lose the business. "Mr. Hanass Vincent, for example, is famous throughout Tingen."

Bogda stopped and considered it.

"No. I trust only Mr. Moretti. May I wait here for a while? Perhaps he'll come after finishing his other business."

"Of course." Angelica gave him a warm smile.

Bogda sat on the sofa. Sometimes he stroked his cane; at others, he stared out the window. His impatience was obvious.

Seconds became minutes. Just as his thoughts descended into confusion and he began wondering whether to remain or leave, the receptionist exclaimed in delight.

"Good afternoon, Mr. Moretti!"

Upon seeing the familiar Angelica, Klein nearly asked why she was always working. Didn't she ever rest or receive a day off?

Then he remembered that a Seer shouldn't ask such ordinary questions. He ought to speak like a mysterious charlatan instead.

How marvelous that fate compels us to meet once more, Madam Angelica.

No, that sounds as though I'm flirting with her...

His thoughts spun before he finally settled on a simple smile.

"Good afternoon, Madam Angelica."

"A client has been waiting for you." Angelica pointed toward Bogda, who had hurriedly risen from the sofa.

Someone specifically requested me?

Pleasantly surprised, Klein removed his half top hat and used the motion to conceal two taps at his glabella.

"Good afternoon, sir..."

His voice trailed off as he looked at the client.

Through Spiritual Vision, Bogda's liver appeared dim and nearly black. The condition had disrupted his entire body, thinning his aura in several places.

Klein carefully chose his words and adopted a serious expression.

"Sir, you should consult a physician, not a fortune-teller."

Bogda froze, then his face brightened with startled delight.

"How extraordinary... Anna wasn't lying..."

He looked earnestly at Klein.

"Mr. Moretti, I've already consulted a physician. He says I may require surgery, but the operation terrifies me. I want to divine its outcome."

Surgery in this age truly is dangerous. Emperor Roselle may have accelerated its development, but medicine still lacks most of the necessary technology...

Klein didn't reject the request. He nodded slightly.

"My fee is eight pence. Is that acceptable?"

"Eight pence?" Bogda exclaimed. "You charge only eight pence?"

Based on Anna's description and what Mr. Moretti just demonstrated, I would willingly pay at least a pound!

Haven't you heard of making a profit through volume?

Klein felt briefly embarrassed. After several seconds, he smiled with deliberate serenity.

"The privilege of receiving divine revelations and glimpsing fate is already a blessing. We must therefore remain humble and restrain our greed. Only then will such gifts continue to be bestowed upon us."

"You are a true Seer."

Bogda placed a hand over his chest and bowed sincerely.

Receiving the man's trust and praise caused Klein's spirituality to relax. Even the "principle" he had invented gave him a new insight into acting.

He turned toward Angelica.

"Miss Angelica, is the Topaz Room available?"

Angelica sighed in relief for Bogda and smiled sweetly.

"Yes."

After entering the room, Klein asked Bogda to lock the door. Then he sat behind the table and pinched his forehead.

"Shall we use tarot cards?" he asked with a smile.

Spirit dowsing was best suited to matters connected to the diviner himself, while constructing an astrolabe would take too long.

"I'll leave the method to you," Bogda replied.

Klein helped him shuffle and cut the deck, then arranged the cards in an Intis spread.

Relying on the instincts granted by the Seer potion, he ignored the other cards and turned over only the one representing the final outcome.

"The Wheel of Fortune, reversed. Events will develop unfavorably," he said solemnly after a glance.

All color drained from Bogda's face. His lips trembled.

"Is there no hope?"

While searching for another solution, Klein replied, "Let me attempt a different form of divination. Leave your ring with me and write your date of birth on this paper. Then wait quietly outside."

His gentle, reassuring tone calmed Bogda enough to follow the instructions. He wrote down the information, left his ring, and departed.

Once the door closed, Klein added a sentence to the paper.

Outcome of Bogda Jones's surgery.

He took the ring and paper, leaned back, and performed a dream divination.

Within the blurred and distorted dreamscape, he gradually regained awareness. He saw Bogda being wheeled out of a trembling operating room beneath a white sheet, his visible face ashen and lifeless.

This time, Klein encountered no strange palace, no sense of being watched, and no other anomaly. He quickly awakened.

Frowning deeply, he considered how to explain the result.

The operation is very likely to kill him. I could attempt the restorative ritualistic magic I learned today, but that would expose the existence of Beyonders. I would also need the captain's approval first, and the ritual might not cure such a severe illness anyway...

As Klein racked his brain, a memory surfaced.

Mr. Glacis's lung disease was cured by an apothecary. He said the medicine was miraculous.

What was the man's name? Right--Lawson Darkwill. Number 18, Vlad Street, East Borough. Lawson's Folk Herb Store.

Because Klein had deliberately memorized the information, the details returned immediately.

He tapped the corner of the table and made a decision.

After using spirit dowsing to determine whether the idea was favorable, Klein opened the door.

Bogda sprang anxiously to his feet. Klein returned the ring and smiled warmly.

"I have found hope for you."

"Truly?" Bogda asked with delighted surprise.

Rather than answer directly, Klein continued.

"Your hope lies in East Borough, on Vlad Street, and is connected to the name Lawson.

"If you fail to find it, return here at four o'clock on Monday afternoon."

"Yes. Of course."

Bogda nodded repeatedly, excitedly opened his wallet, and produced five pence and three pennies.

He followed Klein's stated fee exactly, unwilling to corrupt a genuine Seer with a tip.

The corners of Klein's mouth twitched, but his smile remained brilliant as he accepted the coins.

"I hope you find that hope quickly."

After Bogda left, Klein handed over the club's commission and again tipped Angelica, pretending he had received a full soli.

...

East Borough, Vlad Street.

Bogda walked from one end of the street to the other three times, continuing until his liver began aching intensely.

At last, he determined that only one place had any connection to the name Lawson: Lawson's Folk Herb Store at number 18.

Gathering his courage, he entered.

The mingled smell of countless herbs filled the shop. Behind the counter stood a round-faced man of thirty or forty with extremely short black hair.

The proprietor's clothing resembled that of a village witch doctor: a deep-black robe embroidered with peculiar symbols.

"Good afternoon. Do you have medicine capable of treating my illness?" Bogda asked politely.

The man raised his head. His deep-blue eyes swept across Bogda, and he smiled.

"Your liver disease is extremely serious. The first question, however, is whether you possess enough money. Can you afford the treatment?"

He could tell at a glance?

Bogda's confidence immediately increased, and he nodded rapidly.

"How much does the medicine cost?"

"Ten pounds. A very fair price."

The proprietor took a packet of herbs from beneath the counter.

"Add enough water and boil this into a medicinal brew. Once it is ready, add ten drops of blood from a freshly killed rooster and drink it immediately. The herbs can be brewed three times. After the third dose, you will recover."

As he spoke, he opened the yellow-brown paper packet and added several strange herbs.

That sounds extraordinarily suspicious...

Bogda swallowed.

"Is that all?"

The proprietor stared at him, then smiled broadly.

"Did you want something else? What about this packet? Once your liver recovers, I guarantee your wife will be extremely satisfied."

Chuckling, he lowered his voice and produced another packet wrapped in black paper.

"It contains mummy powder. Trust me, many aristocrats consume it. They add it to tea or boil it into soup."

...

Bogda's confidence in the apothecary plummeted, replaced by disgust.

I believe in Mr. Moretti...

He took a deep breath, opened his wallet, and withdrew the two largest notes remaining among his gold pounds.

Chapter 83: Carving

Clutching the yellow-brown packet of herbs, Bogda staggered out of Lawson's Folk Herb Store.

While waiting for a public carriage, realization suddenly struck him.

He had just spent ten pounds on a bag of plants.

That was nearly an entire month's salary!

If he hadn't trusted Anna and Joyce, he would never have brought so much cash to the Divination Club.

Could Mr. Moretti charge only eight pence because he is secretly working with the owner of Lawson's Folk Herb Store? Perhaps the insignificant divination fee merely directs customers toward the true expense. The newspapers describe scams exactly like this!

Once Bogda made the connection, he began doubting Klein. For a moment, he even suspected Joyce and Anna.

A carriage stopped before him. Bogda looked down at the herbs, unable to bring himself to return them, and climbed aboard with a heavy heart.

...

Inside Lawson's Folk Herb Store, the proprietor watched Bogda leave, then turned toward a doorway partially concealed by piles of herbs.

"Scharmaine, stop purchasing supplies after today."

"W-why, Master?"

A handsome young man with disheveled hair emerged.

The proprietor smiled.

"That was the sixteenth customer to seek me out because of my growing reputation. If this continues, the Nighthawks, Machinery Hivemind, or Mandated Punishers will notice me. When that happens, I'll have to consider moving to another city."

Scharmaine nodded in understanding, then asked with concern, "Should we sublet the shop?"

The proprietor chuckled.

"If you wish to remain, you may take over as owner. You can already identify herbs and prepare ordinary medicines. Naturally, you must deposit half the monthly profit into my anonymous account at Backlund Bank."

"But you haven't taught me your true specialty."

Scharmaine was tired of never remaining in one city for more than a year, but he was equally unwilling to abandon the miraculous formulas his master knew.

The proprietor rocked leisurely in his chair.

"That isn't something you can learn merely because you wish to."

...

A bubbling black-green liquid filled Bogda's vision. It smelled like filthy socks, and its nauseating color made him question every decision he had made that day.

When drops of rooster blood disappeared into the boiling brew, Bogda's father looked at him anxiously.

"I still believe surgery is the best choice."

The blood bubbled briefly, then vanished into the liquid.

Bogda drew a deep breath.

"If this medicine does nothing, I'll consider the operation."

"May the Lord watch over you."

His father traced a triangular Sacred Emblem across his chest.

Once the brew had cooled, Bogda refused to let ten pounds go to waste. He raised the bowl, closed his eyes, tilted back his head, and swallowed the medicine in one continuous gulp.

Its pungent flavor, mixed with the foul smell of blood, flooded his mouth. He nearly spat everything out.

That night, Bogda suffered an upset stomach and visited the bathroom six times. By the time the crimson moon disappeared, he fell into an exhausted sleep.

After an unknown period, he jolted awake from a dream in which his employer was reprimanding him.

"Thank goodness I took three days of annual leave. I don't need to rush to work."

Bogda sighed in relief--then realized he felt far more energetic.

The difference from his lethargy of the previous few weeks was startling.

He instinctively pressed the right side of his abdomen. Previously, even the lightest pressure had caused pain. Now he felt only the ordinary discomfort of pressing flesh.

"Could it truly have worked? That apothecary was obviously deceiving me..."

Both surprised and doubtful, Bogda climbed out of bed and stretched. Vitality seemed to be returning to his body.

After a long silence, he muttered, "The apothecary said I must drink it twice more. Once I've finished all three doses, I'll visit the hospital for an examination.

"He never said how many doses I could take in a day...

"I still think he's a fraud."

...

At the Blackthorn Security Company, Klein had obtained an undisturbed room in the civilian offices through an earlier request.

He held an engraving knife and channeled spirituality through its tip, carefully carving incantations and symbols into two silver ornaments.

The Hermes incantation requested protection from misfortune. The two principal mystical symbols represented the Evernight Goddess and Her title as the Empress of Misfortune and Horror.

Klein also included the Goddess's corresponding Path Number, seven, and the spell's magical characteristic.

Charms and amulets required engravings on both sides. The exact position and format of every symbol, incantation, and characteristic belonged to the domain of mysticism. Versions circulated among ordinary people were generally riddled with errors.

A pile of ruined materials lay to Klein's right. Only after repeated practice and confirming that his technique was sufficiently steady did he dare begin the amulets intended for Benson and Melissa.

Calming his mind, he allowed spirituality to flow from the knife. The number seven slowly appeared on the silver surface.

He had already engraved the incantation and symbols on the other side. Only the final face remained.

When Klein completed the last stroke and put down the knife, every thread of spirituality within the object linked together.

A strange, majestic, and terrifying energy briefly surged through the room.

The disturbance vanished almost immediately. Through Spiritual Vision, Klein saw serene darkness radiating from the now-complete engravings on both sides.

He gently polished the silver ornament, formed from a circle crossed by a vertical line. Its smooth surface felt faintly cool beneath his fingers.

"It's finished!"

Delighted, Klein placed it and the amulet he had completed earlier into his pocket. He would find an appropriate opportunity to give them to Benson and Melissa.

Amulets crafted by Beyonders possessed genuine effects. To a certain extent, they could subtly help their wearers avoid disaster, though their power was hardly miraculous.

Their spirituality also diminished gradually. Unless created through advanced ritualistic magic with a complete prayer structure, they would remain effective for no more than a year.

Advanced rituals demanded a terrifying amount of spirituality--far more than Klein could presently endure.

When the time comes, I can simply make replacements.

He nodded and began cleaning the cluttered table.

Klein hadn't made an amulet for himself because something of this level would offer him only limited protection. He wanted to deepen his understanding of incantations and combine them with ritualistic magic, eventually creating defensive charms activated by a spoken command.

After finishing, Klein left the office to turn in the ruined materials. In the corridor, he encountered Captain Dunn in his black trench coat.

Dunn's deep gray eyes swept over him, and he smiled.

"Klein, the Holy Cathedral has approved your application. You are now a formal member."

"Truly? That's wonderful!" Klein exclaimed with genuine delight.

Dunn nodded.

"You'll receive three pounds in back pay for this week. Afterward, you'll be paid four pounds and ten soli per week until your advance has been fully deducted.

"Did I tell you about the Nighthawks' initiation ritual?

"Every formal member must complete a mission independently. Only then will the rest of the team fully recognize you. In view of your exceptional performance, I can assign you an ordinary case instead of a Beyonder mission.

"After you complete it, I'll formally introduce you to every Nighthawk in Tingen."

"All right!" Klein replied without hesitation.

Three pounds, plus seven pounds in reimbursement. Buying a new suit would present no problem.

And I would still have plenty left over.

Though who knows when this independent mission will appear...

...

Klein waited until Sunday, the day of Selena's birthday banquet.

He changed into his formal suit, brushed his half top hat, polished it with a handkerchief, and studied himself approvingly in the mirror before going downstairs.

Melissa was examining Benson's clothes.

"Is something wrong?" Benson raised his cane, suddenly self-conscious beneath his sister's gaze.

His own inspection had revealed no problem. He considered himself quite presentable.

Melissa finally stopped staring and said solemnly, "Benson, that suit is very old.

"Many respectable ladies and gentlemen will attend today's banquet. Wearing it might appear disrespectful."

Klein had initially been confused, but Melissa's emphasis immediately revealed her intention.

He approached with a chuckle.

"Benson and I have similar builds. He can wear my other tuxedo."

Klein had already told his siblings about purchasing a new suit. He explained that the old one had torn while he inspected certain goods and that the company had generously reimbursed him.

He concealed his promotion and salary increase, worried that the sudden change would alarm them. He planned to reveal it after approximately six months.

His explanation left Benson and Melissa deeply impressed by the Blackthorn Security Company, which they now considered an impeccable employer.

"That isn't really necessary, is it?" Benson objected, failing to grasp the gravity of the situation.

"It is extremely necessary."

Klein pushed him by the shoulders toward the stairs.

"My tuxedo is hanging on the clothes rack."

After watching Benson climb upstairs in a daze, Klein turned and smiled at Melissa.

"Are you hoping Selena's birthday banquet will give Benson an opportunity to begin a beautiful romance?"

His recent reading of newspapers and magazines had taught him that aristocratic and middle-class banquets frequently doubled as matchmaking events.

Melissa nodded solemnly.

"Yes. Benson sacrificed too much because of us."

Little sister, why do you sound like our mother?

Klein looked at Melissa, shook his head, and laughed helplessly.

Chapter 84: Elizabeth

Seeing that his sister remained unconvinced, Klein decided the moment presented a useful opportunity. He looked her over and adopted a solemn expression.

"Melissa, I don't believe you're showing sufficient respect for today's banquet either."

"What?" Melissa looked completely puzzled.

Klein pointed toward her neck.

"As a young lady, you lack a necklace to accentuate that area."

Before she could respond, he smiled and took a silver amulet shaped like encircling angel wings from his pocket.

"Fortunately, I prepared one for you."

"..."

Melissa stared at it, then asked, "How much did it cost?"

Little sister, your priorities are seriously misplaced...

Klein silently complained before explaining with a chuckle.

"It wasn't particularly expensive because I purchased it unfinished. I imitated an object I'd seen before and engraved the blessing and decorative patterns myself."

"You engraved it?"

Melissa was successfully distracted.

"What do you think of my craftsmanship?"

Klein seized the opportunity to hand her the amulet.

Melissa studied it and gently bit her lip.

"I like the angel feathers around the edge."

If you think the symbols and incantation I carved are ugly, you can say so directly. There's no need to be diplomatic. An amulet's value lies in its effect!

The corner of Klein's mouth twitched.

Before he could urge her to accept it, Melissa put on the necklace with a strained expression and carefully adjusted the amulet's position.

"Perfect," Klein declared after examining her, offering deliberately exaggerated praise.

Melissa glanced at him and looked down at the silver charm.

"Klein, you weren't like this before. The way you behave..."

"Perhaps my good job is responsible," he interrupted, offering a preemptive explanation. "A respectable income has made me more confident."

Even though I inherited the original Klein's fragmented memories and can imitate him naturally in most important respects, subtle differences remain. I'm still accustomed to expressing my real personality--especially as I grow closer to Benson and Melissa.

Klein sighed inwardly.

Melissa appeared to accept the explanation and pursed her lips.

"It's good that you're like this... truly."

After they chatted briefly, Benson came downstairs in his borrowed clothes.

He wore a white shirt beneath a black waistcoat and tuxedo, paired with a black bow tie and straight trousers. The transformation made him resemble a successful businessman who had spent years building his career.

The receding hairline supports the impression too...

Klein chuckled inwardly.

"Excellent, Benson. It suits you remarkably well." He spread his hands and smiled brightly.

Melissa nodded in agreement.

"The evidence suggests my clothing is more important than I am," Benson commented self-deprecatingly.

Klein took out the remaining amulet, repeated his earlier explanation, and said, "I made one for you as well."

"Not bad. I'll carry it with me."

Benson accepted it without fuss, then quipped, "Klein, at this point I wouldn't be surprised if you suddenly learned to style hair, tailor clothes, repair watches, or feed curly-haired baboons."

"Life is full of surprises," Klein replied with a smile.

The three siblings made their final preparations and left home. They took a trackless public carriage to Fania Street in North Borough, where Selena lived.

The Wood family also occupied a terraced house, but unlike the Morettis' residence, theirs had a porch and a small front lawn that lent it an elegant appearance.

After they rang the bell, only ten seconds passed before the guest of honor herself opened the door.

Selena Wood, her head covered in wine-red hair, embraced Melissa delightedly.

"I love your dress. It makes you look exceptionally beautiful."

Beside Selena stood her father, Mr. Wood, a senior employee at the Tingen branch of Backlund Bank.

"Welcome, our esteemed elder brother. And welcome, our young historian," he said, addressing Benson and Klein with deliberate exaggeration.

Young historian... Why didn't he add "with a conscience"?

Klein removed his hat and smiled.

"Mr. Wood, you look much younger and more energetic than I imagined."

Without realizing it, his style of flattery had begun leaning toward that of the Foodaholic Empire.

Benson extended his hand to Wood.

"I've met many bank employees, but they're uniformly arrogant and rigid, like newly manufactured machines. None possesses your civility."

"If you encountered me at the bank, you might revise that opinion," Wood replied with a cheerful laugh.

After the greetings, Selena led the siblings inside with a spring in her step. One moment, she announced in her normal voice that Elizabeth had already arrived; the next, she whispered to Melissa that her brothers were more handsome than expected.

I can hear you perfectly well... even if you are flattering me.

Klein helplessly watched the two sixteen-year-old girls walking ahead.

Wait. I'm nowhere near handsome enough to justify that reaction. Miss Selena, exactly how ugly did you imagine Benson and me? A gloomy, balding fat man with pale skin and lifeless eyes?

Klein casually pinched his glabella, diligently practicing Spiritual Vision as he surveyed the gathering.

Miss Selena Wood is healthy, excited, and extremely happy. Mr. Wood has a minor problem with his lungs--yes, I see his pipe...

"Elizabeth, Melissa is here," Selena announced brightly.

A girl in a blue, frilled dress approached. She had naturally curly brown hair and an adorable face softened by baby fat.

Klein froze when he saw her.

He knew this girl.

He had helped her select an amulet in the underground market.

Elizabeth greeted Melissa first, then looked toward Benson and Klein.

She paused, knitting her brows as though trying to recall something.

Soon, she smiled and greeted them politely, behaving as though nothing unusual had happened.

Klein likewise pretended not to recognize her. Under Mr. Wood's guidance, they joined the guests around the living-room sofa and were introduced to Selena's brother, Chris, and the others.

Klein watched Benson converse effortlessly with Chris and several solicitors about their neighbor, Mr. Shaud, and couldn't help feeling envious.

I don't possess that kind of social skill...

He picked up a cocktail from a table in the corner and listened quietly, occasionally nodding or offering a polite smile.

Before long, every guest had arrived, and the banquet formally began.

The Wood family's dining table couldn't accommodate so many people, so the meal was served as a buffet. A maid brought out steak, roast chicken, fried fish, mashed potatoes, and other dishes, arranging them on separate tables. A footman carved the meat so guests could take whatever they wished.

Klein clicked his tongue at the sight of elegant enamel plates and silver cutlery. For a middle-class household, the Woods appeared extraordinarily extravagant.

If they're so wealthy, why did Chris need years to prepare for his wedding?

Then he remembered something Melissa had mentioned.

Perhaps it took them years simply to save enough for all this tableware. For families like theirs, maintaining a respectable appearance is essential.

Filled with mixed feelings, Klein picked up a plate, approached one of the tables, and speared a piece of honey-glazed roast meat.

Elizabeth came to stand beside him. Looking down at the food, she whispered, "So you're Melissa's brother. Thank you. Selena loves the amulet I gave her. She said she felt healthier the moment she put it on."

Selena... the amulet...

Klein suddenly remembered why Elizabeth had been selecting one.

It was a birthday gift for a friend interested in mysticism.

That friend was Selena? Selena is interested in mysticism?

Klein frowned slightly but maintained a polite smile.

"It may simply be a placebo effect."

After saying it, he waited for her to praise Emperor Roselle for introducing the concept.

Instead, Elizabeth looked confused.

"What is a placebo effect?"

"It means the improvement is psychological. Sometimes, believing we'll recover can genuinely make us feel better," Klein explained crudely.

"No. She said it feels different from the amulets she bought before," Elizabeth insisted.

She tilted her head and regarded Klein curiously.

"I never expected Melissa's brother to be an expert in mysticism."

"I studied history, as you know. It's common for historians to encounter related subjects."

Klein skillfully redirected the conversation.

"Do you attend Tingen Technical School as well?"

"No. Selena and I were Melissa's classmates before she transferred there. I now attend nearby Ivos Public School," Elizabeth explained seriously.

Despite the name, a public school wasn't established or maintained by the government. Such institutions had evolved from prestigious grammar schools, admitted students from the general population, and focused on preparing them for university.

Their fees were high, and family background played a role in admission. Even many ordinary middle-class families couldn't afford them.

Elizabeth wasn't especially talkative. After selecting her food, she returned to Selena.

Once everyone had congratulated the birthday girl, the banquet gradually shifted into its later stage.

Klein and Benson were invited to play Texas hold'em. The small blind was half a penny and the big blind one penny. Melissa, Elizabeth, Selena, and their friends went upstairs, whether to talk or play games Klein didn't know.

Klein's luck was terrible. After nearly twenty hands, he hadn't received a single good combination and could do little but fold and observe.

He lifted the corners of his cards again and found the Two of Hearts and Five of Spades.

Should I attempt a bluff?

Klein considered it but couldn't summon the courage. He also resisted the temptation to cheat with divination.

He covered his cards and tapped the table to indicate that he was folding. Then he rose and headed toward the bathroom.

Roselle must also have suffered from obsessive-compulsive tendencies. He actually contrived a bizarre reason to preserve the name Texas for this style of poker...

Klein shook his head as he walked.

Then he abruptly stopped, his pupils contracting.

His spiritual perception had detected a strange fluctuation upstairs.

Chapter 85: Urgency

The strange, distorted, and obscure fluctuation lasted only an instant. A moment later, Klein almost wondered whether he had imagined it.

If his spiritual perception weren't unusually sharp, he probably would have dismissed the anomaly entirely.

Klein frowned. Remembering that his sister was upstairs, he tightened his grip on his cane, passed the bathroom, and hurried toward the Wood family's staircase.

Following the residual traces with his spiritual perception, he climbed quickly and reached the sitting room beside the balcony.

It should have come from here...

Klein raised a hand and tapped his glabella twice.

Through Spiritual Vision, auras penetrated the wall and heavy wooden door. Most appeared ordinary, their outlines indistinct.

One, however, rippled with a sinister dark-green color that was slowly corroding inward.

Just as I thought. Something is wrong.

Klein's expression became stern. He removed the silver chain wrapped around his left wrist and held it in that hand, allowing the topaz to hang freely.

Once the pendant stopped swaying, he visualized the sphere of light and silently recited a statement.

"The room before me contains supernatural danger."

Spirit dowsing was normally most effective when divining matters related to the diviner or specific circumstances within a small nearby area. Klein therefore made the statement precise: the room was directly before him, and the danger could potentially affect him.

"The room before me contains supernatural danger."

After repeating the words seven times, Klein opened his eyes.

The topaz was spinning rapidly clockwise.

There was indeed a supernatural danger inside, and it was considerable.

Selena is interested in mysticism. Did she make a serious mistake while experimenting with some ritual?

What should I do?

Klein rubbed his brow, rewound the chain around his wrist, and knocked on the door.

Thump! Thump! Thump!

He knocked three times in an even rhythm and arranged an amiable smile upon his face.

The door creaked open. Melissa stood before him in her new dress.

"Klein? Is something wrong?"

She clearly hadn't expected to see her brother and looked briefly surprised.

Klein smiled, displaying no sign of alarm.

"I heard all of you enjoying yourselves and became curious."

"I'm sorry if we disturbed you."

Embarrassed, Melissa lowered her head.

"We're performing mirror divination. Selena knows a great deal about it, and it's very entertaining."

Mirror divination... Little sister, couldn't you play the Charlie Charlie challenge[1] or use a Ouija board[2] instead?

Feeling both irritated and helpless, Klein looked past Melissa into the room.

He saw Selena's bright smile and deep dimples.

Through Spiritual Vision, however, the sinister dark-green color was steadily invading the wine-red-haired girl holding a silver-plated mirror.

Klein's thoughts raced as he carefully selected an excuse.

"I won't interrupt your game. Ah, where is Elizabeth? We spoke earlier about Feysac grammar, and she mentioned having several questions for me."

"Elizabeth?"

Melissa examined her brother and emphasized strangely, "She's only sixteen."

Don't let your imagination run wild!

Klein immediately explained, "It is a perfectly ordinary academic discussion. Elizabeth is interested in history and ancient languages."

Melissa gave him a long look.

"She's inside. I'll tell her."

"Thank you."

Klein stepped back from the doorway.

As his sister turned away, he quietly breathed in relief. The situation remained dangerous, but at least the affected person wasn't Melissa.

Only ten seconds later, a puzzled Elizabeth emerged.

"Mr. Moretti, what is the matter? I never said I was interested in history or ancient languages..."

Her sentence died when she saw Klein's grave expression. Her nerves tightened as she realized something was wrong.

Klein moved several steps diagonally and gestured for her to stand partly concealed behind the door.

Influenced by the sudden seriousness, the round-cheeked girl obeyed without thinking.

"As you know, I am interested in mysticism," Klein said directly.

Elizabeth nodded.

"Yes. In fact, I believe you're an expert."

"No, merely an enthusiast. But even I can tell that something has gone wrong with your mirror divination," Klein said heavily.

"Gone wrong?"

Elizabeth nearly cried out, then hurriedly covered her mouth.

Klein thought briefly.

"Words alone may not convince you. Return to the room. When Selena isn't paying attention, look at the front of the mirror she has kept hidden from the rest of you."

"How do you know she's hiding the front?" Elizabeth blurted out.

According to Nighthawk records, more than ninety percent of evil incidents involving mirror divination share that feature.

Klein smiled.

"General knowledge."

Elizabeth returned to the room, doubtful and frightened.

Klein's composed smile disappeared the instant she was gone, replaced by worry.

Even though we're still in North Borough, traveling from Fania Street to Zouteland Street takes at least fifteen minutes by public carriage. By the time I summon the captain and return, the situation may have deteriorated beyond recovery.

If only Benson and Melissa weren't here...

But I can't directly confront a hidden and unknown existence. Do I possess any means of containing it?

Right. Selena is a mysticism enthusiast. Her bedroom should contain extracts, essential oils, herbs, and other materials.

While Klein desperately considered a solution, Elizabeth returned to Selena's side under the pretense of discussing something.

A girl sitting opposite them took a sip of red wine. Under the teasing gazes of her companions, she blushed and summoned the courage to ask, "Can you divine when I'll meet a handsome and romantic gentleman?"

Selena coughed lightly twice and stroked the mirror's back.

"Mirror, mirror, tell me: when will the gentleman in Yonina's heart appear?"

After repeating the question three times, she lifted the mirror before her.

Elizabeth seized the opportunity, abruptly leaned sideways, and glanced into its surface.

She expected to see Selena's face along with half of her own.

Instead, she saw only Selena.

The tiny mirror contained Selena's entire body.

Its background was utterly black, and Selena stood at the center with a cold expression.

Elizabeth trembled violently and recoiled against the sofa. For a moment, she forgot to breathe.

She shuddered uncontrollably, rose without offering an excuse, and stumbled toward the door. She didn't dare look back at the smiling Selena.

"Yonina will meet her gentleman on the Sunday of the second week, six months from now..."

Amid the other girls' laughter, Elizabeth opened the door and stepped into the hall.

Klein stood beneath the wall lamps in his tuxedo and half top hat, partially concealed by shadow.

"Mr. Moretti, I-I..." she stammered, dazed.

Klein smiled calmly.

"Don't alarm the girls inside."

His composure helped Elizabeth settle. She closed the door and hurried toward him.

"I saw it. Only Selena appeared in the mirror--a devil-like Selena," she whispered hoarsely.

Just as expected...

Klein's expression became solemn.

"Do you know which room belongs to Selena? And do you know where she keeps her mysticism supplies?"

"There. She keeps the supplies inside as well."

Elizabeth immediately pointed toward a door diagonally across the hall.

Holding his cane, Klein crossed over and opened the unlocked door. In the mingled light of streetlamps and crimson moon, he turned a valve and lit the gas lamp.

Pale-yellow light filled the bedroom.

Klein surveyed bottles of extracts and floral essences, boxes of powdered herbs, candles, charms, and related objects. They stood on tables or were neatly arranged on shelves, each labeled with its name.

After confirming what was available, he turned to Elizabeth, who had followed him.

"Do you want to save Selena?"

"Yes!" Elizabeth nodded instinctively, then asked with uncertainty, "Will it be dangerous?"

"To some extent. After all, I am only a mysticism enthusiast," Klein answered honestly.

"Some danger..."

Elizabeth pressed her lips together for several seconds.

"What do you need me to do?"

Klein gave her a warm, reassuring smile.

"Don't be nervous. For now, return as though nothing happened and sit beside Selena.

"Five minutes later--remember, exactly five minutes--tell her you have a pleasant surprise and bring her here. Knock softly: one long knock followed by two short ones. After that, leave everything to me."

Elizabeth considered the instructions in silence, then nodded solemnly.

"All right."

After watching her return to the sitting room, Klein checked his pocket watch, closed Selena's bedroom door, and rapidly cleared the desk.

He selected the necessary materials and placed them on a chair.

Then he took two lightly scented candles and positioned them at the upper-left and upper-right corners of the desk.

They represented the Lady of Crimson and the Empress of Misfortune and Horror.

Klein intended to perform a ritual and borrow the Evernight Goddess's power to drive away the mysterious existence affecting Selena.

As a mere Sequence 9, however, his ritualistic magic wasn't especially powerful. For the attempt to succeed, Elizabeth needed to lure Selena within the sealing circle surrounding the altar.

Klein also had to account for the possibility that Selena would detect the plan and resist.

For those reasons, he decided to use a suspended ritual--one prepared in advance and completed only after the target entered its range.

[1] A paper-and-pencil divination game in which balanced pencils supposedly move to answer questions.

[2] A board bearing letters, numbers, and symbols, used with a movable pointer that supposedly answers questions during a seance.

Chapter 86: Prayer

A suspended ritual allowed a Beyonder to pause the ceremony at a carefully chosen point, attend to other matters, and later return to complete it without losing the intended effect.

The technique had emerged over more than a thousand years of development in ritualistic magic. Many advanced rituals involved multiple stages and could require anywhere from an hour to half a day. It was nearly impossible to guarantee that no interruption or accident would occur throughout such a long period.

After generations of practitioners paid for their failures in blood and tears, methods for safely suspending rituals became standard at higher levels. Those discoveries gradually influenced simpler ritualistic magic as well.

However, a ritual couldn't be paused at an arbitrary moment. The practitioner had to follow mystical principles and understand the corresponding technique. Otherwise, failure was inevitable, and the resulting backlash could be terrifying.

As Klein understood it, once a deity's attention had been attracted and the existence was waiting to hear the request, suddenly saying, "Please wait while I use the bathroom," might ensure that the ritualist never needed a bathroom again.

Phew...

Klein breathed out and composed himself.

He had performed the luck-enhancement ritual many times and had even designed a ritual for Justice and The Hanged Man. Nevertheless, this was the first genuine ritualistic magic he had conducted in accordance with established rules.

After glancing at his silver-inlaid cane beside the bed, he picked up a third candle and placed it at the center of the desk to represent himself.

He set Selena's silver ritual bowl before that candle and replaced the ritual ax with a Sacred Emblem. To the left, he arranged extracts and essential oils made from moon flowers, slumber flowers, and other plants. To the right, he placed a dish of coarse salt, a small silver dagger, a piece of imitation goatskin, and a quill dipped in ink.

Fortunately, Selena possessed a comprehensive collection. Otherwise, Klein couldn't have completed the preparations. The faster and simpler rituals performed by Old Neil weren't techniques a Seer could imitate.

Selena is clearly an experienced mysticism enthusiast. Of course, if she weren't experienced, she probably couldn't have caused this much trouble.

She's only sixteen, yet she must have studied these subjects for at least a year. Who taught her?

Ideas flashed through Klein's mind as he picked up Selena's cup, filled it with plain water, and placed it beside the salt.

He took out his pocket watch and snapped it open. After checking the time, he delayed no longer.

Klein visualized layer after layer of spherical light and rapidly entered Cogitation.

A formless whirlwind passed through the flower-scented room.

He put away the watch. His eyes darkened from brown to black, as though they could see directly into the soul.

Klein extended one palm toward the candle at the upper-right corner and silently chanted:

"Evernight Goddess, You are the Lady of Crimson."

As he recited the line, he projected his spirituality and rubbed the wick.

After several seconds, the candle ignited. Tranquil blue burned at the heart of its dim-yellow flame.

"Evernight Goddess, You are the Empress of Misfortune and Horror."

Using the same method, Klein lit the candle at the upper-left corner.

"I am Your loyal guardian, the shield that defends against danger in the dark night and the spear that strikes evil in silence."

Whoosh!

The third candle, representing Klein, burst alight.

Its flame remained perfectly still.

He picked up the small silver dagger and imitated Old Neil's practiced movements, using the incantation, salt, and water to complete the purification.

Then he gathered his spirituality and sent it flowing from the dagger's tip, naturally merging the energy with the surrounding space.

Holding the silver blade, Klein circled the bedroom. When he reached the bed, he climbed over it on his knees, completing the circuit and enclosing the entire room within an invisible barrier.

The streetlamp's glow disappeared from the windows, while crimson moonlight continued shining quietly through.

Klein returned to the desk and picked up the quill. Using ink infused with spirituality, he drew the incantations and symbols for warding off misfortune.

Once finished, he put down the quill and added one drop of extract, floral essence, and essential oil to each of the three candles.

Sizzle!

A faint mist spread through the room, lending it an additional layer of mystery.

Next, Klein burned several kinds of herbs. He stepped back from the intermingled fragrances and began reciting the incantation that would suspend the ritual.

"Higher than the cosmos and more eternal than eternity, the Evernight Goddess.

"I pray for Your loving grace.

"I pray that You show Your loving grace to Your devout believer.

"I pray for the power of the Crimson.

"I pray for the power of Misfortune and Horror.

"I pray that You cleanse Your devout believer, Selena Wood, of evil corruption and protect her from danger.

"I pray that You wait a moment--one moment for this unfortunate girl.

"Moon flower, herb of the red moon, bestow your power upon my incantation!

"Slumber flower, herb of the red moon, bestow your power upon my incantation!"

After completing the prayer, Klein closed his eyes and repeated it seven times inwardly.

Nothing unusual occurred at the altar.

He raised the silver dagger and retreated toward Selena's bedroom door.

Klein touched four points over his chest to form the crimson moon, then turned and lifted the blade.

Spirituality flowed from its tip and cut a door-shaped opening through the invisible wall.

He knew that opening the physical door now wouldn't disturb the altar's sacred tranquility.

Klein took out his intricately patterned silver pocket watch, checked the time, and mentally rehearsed the next stages.

...

In the second-floor sitting room, Elizabeth trembled as she repeatedly glanced at the wall clock beneath the light of two gas lamps. She silently counted down the minutes.

"It's nearly time..."

She looked sideways at the lively girl with long wine-red hair. Selena's dimples were deep, her smile radiant, and she chatted animatedly with the friends around her.

Yet the more normal Selena appeared, the more frightened Elizabeth became. She couldn't erase the image of the cold, terrifying Selena standing inside the mirror.

I can't wait any longer. I have to act now!

Elizabeth abruptly rose. Under everyone's startled gaze, she forced a smile and stammered, "Selena, I-I have a surprise for y-you. Come outside with me."

"Really? Didn't you already give me a birthday present?"

Selena turned the mirror face down and stood in surprise.

"A surprise wouldn't be a surprise if there were signs beforehand," Elizabeth replied, painfully aware that she possessed no talent for acting.

Without further explanation, she walked toward the bedroom first. Selena followed with a confused smile.

Melissa watched her two closest friends leave and unconsciously furrowed her brow.

Elizabeth has behaved so strangely today.

She became even stranger after speaking with Klein.

Earlier, she suddenly ran out claiming she needed the bathroom, but why did she look so anxious?

...

Outside Selena's bedroom, Elizabeth drew a deep breath.

"Let's go into your room."

"Elizabeth, you're trembling. You look frightened. Why?" Selena asked, puzzled by her friend's condition.

"Excitement! Yes, I'm excited!"

Elizabeth glanced at the mirror in Selena's hand, half turned, and knocked on the door: one long knock followed by two short ones.

"Why are you knocking on my own bedroom door?" Selena asked, even more baffled.

Creak.

The door opened. Klein stood before them in his black tuxedo and half top hat.

"This is the surprise?" Selena's mouth fell open. "Why is this a pleasant surprise?"

Klein suddenly seized her wrist and pulled her into the room, leaving Elizabeth rooted in place.

At the same time, he swept the silver dagger through the air. Spirituality poured from its tip and instantly repaired the door-shaped gap.

The invisible spiritual wall sealed the room, trapping Selena's screams inside.

Bang!

Klein shut the physical door. Without looking at Selena, he rushed toward the desk.

The wine-red-haired girl stopped screaming and raised her head to survey the room.

Her gaze rapidly turned cold. Mottled pallor spread across her skin, and her fingernails lengthened into sharp claws.

By then, Klein had already returned to Cogitation.

He added another drop of moon-flower extract and essential oil to each candle and chanted loudly:

"Supreme Lady of Crimson, great Empress of Misfortune and Horror.

"I pray that You bestow Your loving grace.

"Show Your loving grace to the lost lamb, Selena Wood!"

As he prayed, Klein picked up the imitation goatskin and thrust it into the flame of the candle representing the petitioner.

Whoosh!

A freezing wind swept against his back as an immense force assaulted him.

The goatskin ignited. Klein threw it into the silver bowl, then crouched according to his rehearsed plan, narrowly avoiding the lethal strike from behind.

Whoosh! Whoosh! Whoosh!

The wind howled. Klein felt his spirituality pouring uncontrollably from his body like a surging current.

The goatskin burned in the small silver bowl, its flames a silent darkness.

Heavy objects struck the floor behind him.

Bam! Bang!

The two impacts came almost without pause.

Wisps of dark-green gas plunged into the silver bowl and vanished into its illusory black flames.

Klein rolled aside, rose, and drew the revolver from beneath his arm.

He found Selena lying unconscious on the carpet. The silver-plated mirror had shattered into countless fragments around her.

The pieces no longer reflected Selena. They showed only the ceiling and Klein's silhouette.

Through the Spiritual Vision he had kept active, Klein saw that the sinister dark green had vanished completely from Selena's aura. Everything had returned to normal, though she appeared weakened.

Phew...

The instant Klein relaxed, sharp pain began throbbing in his glabella and skull.

It spread through his entire body, making him want to writhe on the floor.

He clenched his fists. Veins rose black beneath the skin of his hands, wriggling like living worms.

Silent screams and whispers tore at his mind.

The ordeal lasted nearly twenty seconds. By the end, cold sweat had soaked his forehead and waistcoat.

Did the ritual drain nearly all my spirituality and bring my Beyonder powers close to losing control?

Klein made a rough assessment.

The experience also revealed how much of the potion he had already digested. If his spirituality remained at the level it possessed immediately after he became a Seer, he could never have survived the ritual. He would likely have transformed into a monster on the spot.

Acting truly is effective...

Klein tapped his glabella and wiped away the sweat.

He turned toward the altar, touched four points over his chest, and declared aloud, "Praise the Lady!"

Then he extinguished the candles and rapidly dismantled the altar.

He returned each object to the desk and used the silver dagger to disperse the spiritual wall.

Whoosh!

Wind echoed through the room before subsiding.

Klein released a long breath, still frightened by how narrowly he had succeeded.

"If I hadn't rehearsed every step and completed the ritual correctly, this could have become disastrous. And I still don't know what the hidden existence actually was.

"Fortunately... yes, fortunately the room is carpeted. At least rolling across the floor didn't damage my clothes."

He shook his head, approached the door, and opened it.

Elizabeth took two nervous steps backward.

"How is she?"

Klein removed his half top hat and gave her terrified face a warm smile.

"I corrected the error in her mirror divination. The problem has been resolved."

Chapter 87: A Warning

"Is it truly over?" Elizabeth asked in disbelief.

Klein smiled and nodded casually.

"Yes. It wasn't especially difficult."

That last part was a lie, he added inwardly.

Perhaps Klein's unwavering composure convinced her, or perhaps he was simply the only hope available. Whatever the reason, Elizabeth no longer doubted him. She patted her chest and sighed in relief.

"Thank you. You truly are a trustworthy gentleman. I was terrified just now.

"How is Selena? Will she be all right?"

"She may remain unconscious for several minutes, but the danger has passed. Expect her to feel weak for two or three days."

Klein's expression abruptly became stern.

"Who taught her mysticism? Didn't that person explain the basic prohibitions?"

Elizabeth straightened like a student being reprimanded by a teacher.

After thinking, she replied, "Selena once said her teacher was Hanass Vincent. She met him at the Divination Club on Howes Street about a year ago."

Hanass Vincent...

On the surface, he never appeared to teach anything improper about mirror divination. But privately, he has been experimenting with dangerous forms of it.

If I had learned this earlier, I could have reported him to the captain and had the Nighthawks raid his home.

Klein felt a lingering regret and fear. The incident had nearly endangered his sister.

"Did Hanass Vincent teach Selena this particular form of mirror divination?" he asked deeply.

Elizabeth nodded cautiously.

"Yes, although Selena tried it several times before without success. Today she said she had secretly read an incantation her teacher kept hidden and that there wouldn't be any danger."

She was practically an expert at courting death...

Klein rubbed his temples to ease the headache.

"Do you remember the incantation?"

Although Hanass didn't deliberately give Selena the dangerous material, he was clearly using it to contact some hidden and unknown existence. Sooner or later, that would create another incident. The Nighthawks must deal with him before anyone else is harmed.

"I remember only part of it," Elizabeth said. "She recited the words in Hermes. As you know, I've only just begun studying that language. I recognized terms meaning 'revolve,' 'spirit,' 'Creator,' and 'grace.'"

Creator? The True Creator?

Many underground mysticism enthusiasts believe in that ancient entity, and numerous secret organizations worship Him. An existence that first appeared a thousand years ago, during the early Fifth Epoch.

Klein nodded thoughtfully.

"When Selena wakes, ask her for the entire incantation. Find an opportunity to tell me afterward."

"All right," Elizabeth agreed without hesitation.

Then confusion crossed her face.

"Mr. Moretti, why don't you ask her yourself?"

"I don't want Melissa to know that I study mysticism. Will you help me keep it secret?" Klein asked in return.

Elizabeth bit her lip, her eyes glinting.

"Of course. Melissa prefers machines to mysteries, and logic to instinct."

Klein placed his hat before his chest and bowed like a gentleman.

"Thank you for understanding. As for Selena, you know she isn't especially good at keeping secrets."

"More accurately, she enjoys sharing them," Elizabeth agreed.

Klein put his hat back on and considered the situation.

"When she wakes, tell her that she suddenly fainted and shattered the mirror. I suspect her memory ended when the divination began."

After Elizabeth nodded, his expression became severe once more.

"Remember this: whether performing divination or any other mystical ritual, never pray to an existence other than the seven orthodox deities. Burn any incantation of that kind immediately, and stay far away from anyone distributing such material.

"If I hadn't noticed in time, Selena would have become a monster or evil spirit within ten minutes. Everyone here would have died--including me."

Remembering the cold Selena inside the mirror, Elizabeth didn't doubt him.

"I understand. I'll remember, and I'll watch Selena carefully," she promised, still frightened.

"Good. Go take care of her."

Klein raised his black cane and walked toward the stairs.

As he went, his eyes darkened. He took a penny from his pocket and flicked it into the air.

"Selena is safe now.

"Selena is safe now."

Klein rapidly repeated the statement, then caught the falling coin.

The portrait of George III faced upward.

This wasn't a simplified form of spirit dowsing, but of dream divination. With Cogitation, Klein had forced himself into a momentary sleep and allowed his spirit to briefly wander the spirit world. The coin's two faces acted as symbolic manifestations of the answer.

Heads meant affirmation, while tails meant denial.

Excellent. Everything truly is fine.

Klein happily rolled the bronze coin between his fingers.

Only a Seer could reduce dream divination to such a simple act.

Elizabeth watched his departing back, saw the coin rise and fall, and waited until he vanished down the stairs before returning to the bedroom.

Selena lay unconscious on the floor, surrounded by fragments of the shattered mirror.

Holding her breath, Elizabeth tiptoed inside and examined the pieces. The cold, sinister Selena was gone. Every fragment reflected only the ceiling.

Phew.

Finally reassured, Elizabeth released a long breath.

She couldn't lift Selena onto the bed by herself, so she nudged her awake instead.

"Elizabeth... What happened? Did I drink too much?"

Selena's eyes were dull and confused, and her voice sounded weak.

After a moment of thought, Elizabeth answered solemnly.

"No, Selena. Something happened during your mirror divination. You attracted a malicious existence."

"Did I?"

With Elizabeth's support, Selena weakly made her way to the bed and rubbed her temple.

"I remember only beginning the divination."

Elizabeth offered a carefully constructed half-truth.

"You became a completely different person. The Selena inside the mirror was nothing like the Selena outside it. I was terrified.

"I told you I had a surprise and brought you here, then seized the mirror and smashed it against the carpet. After that... you fainted.

"Praise the Goddess, you're safe now."

"I-I don't remember any of it..." Selena muttered, her face pale.

The harder she tried to remember, the emptier her mind seemed, and the greater her fear became.

She glanced instinctively toward the desk and noticed that several objects were clearly out of place.

What actually happened?

Selena strained her memory and recovered only the faint image of a man in a black suit and hat. He wasn't particularly tall or strong, but he stood with a straight back.

"Selena," Elizabeth said seriously, "when I visited the underground market to purchase your amulet, I met an expert in mysticism. He warned me never to pray to any existence besides the seven orthodox deities. Doing so will inevitably invite disaster.

"Promise me you won't attempt anything like this again. I didn't even know whether breaking the mirror would save you!"

Selena was thoroughly frightened. She nodded dazedly.

"Never again. I will never try it again."

"What did the incantation mean? If I happen to meet that expert again, I can ask him about it for you," Elizabeth said with feigned casualness.

Still rubbing her temple, Selena answered, "Spirit that revolves this world, the grace of the True Creator, the eyes that look at fate."

...

Tap. Tap. Tap.

Klein smoothed the wrinkles from his clothes and brushed away the dust as he descended the stairs.

He removed his hat and returned leisurely to the long card table.

"Where were you? You were gone for nearly ten minutes," Selena's brother Chris asked while folding his hand.

Klein smiled.

"First the bathroom, then upstairs to become acquainted with the young ladies."

"I admire your honesty," Chris said with a laugh.

He possessed the short build and red hair common in the Wood family. Gold-rimmed spectacles and a lively manner completed the appearance of an accomplished solicitor.

You wouldn't say that if you knew I had knocked your sister unconscious upstairs.

Klein replied humbly, "We engaged only in academic discussion."

Academic discussion concerning mysticism...

He set down his hat, returned to his seat, and received two cards as a new hand began.

Peeking at their corners, he found the King of Spades and Ace of Diamonds.

My luck has improved. Is this a reward for doing a good deed?

Klein took out a coin and prepared to bet.

Since Hanass didn't intentionally give Selena the incantation, perhaps there is no need to report the matter to the captain with extreme urgency.

He maintained a cautious style throughout the remaining hands, betting only when his cards were strong. He never attempted a bluff and won only modestly.

When the game ended at half past ten, Klein had gained six pence.

"I won two soli and eight pence." Benson played with the notes and coins in his hand.

"I didn't realize you were an expert at poker," Klein praised with a laugh.

"I rarely play, but poker resembles negotiation. You conceal your own cards, infer what others are hiding, then use different methods to frighten or entice them..."

Benson stopped as Melissa and the other girls came downstairs.

"It's time to go home," Klein said, glancing toward his sister and her friends while rubbing his temples.

His head still throbbed from the ritual.

Klein visited the bathroom once more and used the opportunity to pass Elizabeth, who quietly gave him the full incantation.

When he rejoined his siblings, he smiled.

"I just remembered something important. I need to stop by the company. Shall we go to Zouteland Street first? It won't take long."

Chapter 88: Report

"What is it?" Benson asked casually.

Melissa regarded her brother with a grave expression. Klein had been acting strangely all evening. In fact, he had seemed only slightly more normal than Elizabeth and, later, Selena.

Klein chuckled. He had prepared an excuse in advance.

"There was an error in one of my documents. I already told my colleagues I'd give them the corrected version when I arrived early tomorrow morning. Since the office is on our way, I can either fix it now or wake up at least half an hour earlier tomorrow. Naturally, I chose the former."

"Ah, no wonder. I had a nagging feeling your mind wasn't on the game. So you were thinking about work." Enlightenment dawned on Benson's face. "No, forgive me. I should say the card game helped you think."

"All right. We'll wait for you." Melissa looked away and smoothed the ruffles of her engageantes.

The track and trackless public carriages had stopped running for the night, so the three siblings bid farewell to their hosts and hired a carriage nearby. Forty-five minutes cost two soli.

"I've heard that every driver who hires out his own carriage charges outrageous fees," Benson muttered. He had spent most of his winnings from the card game on their fare.

Klein smiled. "I think it's quite reasonable. It is nearly eleven, after all."

"I was only joking. I thought we might share the carriage with some of the other guests. Forty-five minutes would take us to plenty of places." Benson gazed through the window at the guests hiring carriages one after another.

I know. Ride-sharing... Klein rubbed the silver-inlaid top of his walking stick. "We wouldn't mind, but the other guests might. Benson, didn't you notice how much they care about appearances and looking respectable? I suspect that's common among the middle class."

"Hmm." Benson nodded solemnly. "The Wood family was far more extravagant than I expected, even though Mr. Wood earns only four pounds a week... Heh, perhaps 'looking respectable' is the greatest difference between the middle class and curly-haired baboons."

Do you have some sort of grudge against curly-haired baboons? Klein nearly burst out laughing.

Melissa did not join their conversation. She sat quietly and studied Klein from time to time, sending a chill down his spine.

The two-wheeled carriage sped through the dark, silent streets and reached Zouteland Street in only twelve minutes.

"Wait here for me. Five minutes. It won't take more than five," Klein emphasized. He put on his top hat, took up his cane, and climbed out.

Since the driver charged by time rather than distance, he had no objection to waiting.

Klein climbed the stairs to the Blackthorn Security Company and knocked on the door.

It opened within ten seconds, revealing Leonard Mitchell in a shirt and vest.

"You're not on duty tonight," Leonard observed, surprised to see him.

Klein stood guard at Chanis Gate only once a week and otherwise kept regular working hours. Nighttime emergencies were handled by the Sleepless, who thrived after dark.

Though sleeping only two or three hours a day can cause baldness and memory loss... Whenever that thought occurred to him, Klein couldn't help mocking Captain Dunn Smith in his heart.

"I have something to report," he said simply.

"A mission?" Leonard asked as he stepped aside.

Upon entering the reception hall, Klein saw Dunn emerge in his black trench coat. His gray eyes were as deep and somber as ever.

"Captain, I encountered a supernatural incident."

"Tell me the details," Dunn said at once.

Klein recounted everything that had happened and explained the measures he had taken.

"Therefore, I believe Hanass Vincent needs to be investigated."

At the time, Klein had reasoned that the evil entity summoned through the magic-mirror divination had not yet caused a catastrophe, nor had his spiritual intuition warned him of extreme danger. The entity probably needed more time and had no wish to awaken early or take complete possession of Selena. As long as its intentions remained concealed, it would prefer to observe. Under those circumstances, it hadn't been difficult for Elizabeth to lure Selena to the bedroom door.

"You handled it well. You acted before the evil spirit fully manifested and completely possessed her." Dunn gave a slight nod. "Leave the subsequent investigation to us. You may go home and rest."

Klein exhaled in relief and laughed.

"I thought you might treat this as my initiation mission and make me finish it alone."

Judging by the incantation Elizabeth had given him, Hanass Vincent was unquestionably dangerous.

"That's because you already have an initiation mission," Leonard remarked languidly from the side.

"What?" Klein stared at him in surprise.

Dunn smiled faintly and explained in his soothing voice, "At around seven this evening, the police station referred a case to us. Our preliminary assessment found no immediate danger or urgency, so we decided that you would handle it alone tomorrow."

"That's enough questions about the case. Get a good night's rest, and move your day off to Tuesday or Wednesday."
