...There's a remedy like that? Klein suddenly suspected the apothecary was a fraud and wondered whether he had sent the man before him straight into a pit of fire.

He examined Bogda and confirmed that nothing was wrong with his aura.

"Mummy powder?" Klein asked cautiously.

"Yes, mummy powder. I asked a friend, and he told me even the nobles of Backlund are frantically searching for it. It's made by grinding mummies into powder and supposedly gives men exceptional performance in bed. It sounds filthy and disgusting, but the aristocracy truly uses it..." Bogda explained in detail, eagerness burning in his eyes.

Mummies? Preserved corpses, ground into powder? Klein was dumbfounded and nearly retched in Bogda's face.

Those nobles are truly committed... Just as he was about to advise Bogda against the purchase, Glacis, who had previously suffered from lung disease, entered and overheard their conversation.

"Yes, it's extremely effective. I recommend Lawson's Folk Herb Store on Vlad Street. Mr. Darkwill's secret formula works wonderfully!" Glacis removed his spectacles and leaned closer with obvious interest. Lowering his voice, he added, "My experience was very, very, very satisfying."

"You know of it too? I was just about to visit Mr. Darkwill's shop." Bogda's final doubts vanished.

After a brief conversation, he hurried out of the Divination Club.

Klein remained somewhat stunned.

He waited until twenty past five before putting on his hat and taking up his black cane. Then he hired a carriage to Vlad Street, intending to observe the apothecary Lawson Darkwill before deciding whether to notify the captain.

At 18 Vlad Street, Klein stopped outside the herb store. Its door was closed, and a notice announced that the premises were available to let.

...What a cautious man, he thought.

With the apothecary gone, Klein no longer needed to wrestle with the decision or conduct an investigation.

Chapter 96: Daly's Theory

By the following morning, Klein had completely recovered from his exhaustion. He entered the Blackthorn Security Company with a steady stride.

"Good morning, Klein. The weather is so cool and pleasant today. I'm already looking forward to tonight's feast." Rozanne, dressed in pale green, greeted him with a smile from behind the reception desk.

Klein deliberately touched his stomach. "Miss Rozanne, you shouldn't mention that so early in the morning! I'm already tired of today's work before it has even begun. Now all I want is for evening to arrive."

"Me too." Rozanne chuckled.

She looked both ways, beckoned Klein closer, and lowered her voice. "I saw Madam Daly earlier."

"Madam Daly, the Spirit Medium?" Klein asked in surprise.

The most famous spirit medium in Awwa County had been stationed at Enmat Harbor, a considerable distance from Tingen.

"Yes." Rozanne nodded firmly. "But she's already left. Ah, she's my ideal kind of Beyonder. If I became a Spirit Medium, I would leave Tingen and travel the entire world alone--Intis, Feysac, Feynapotter, the Southern Continent, vast grasslands, primeval forests, and snow-covered plains!"

Young lady, remember the Nighthawks' rules... Klein shook his head in amusement.

"Even Madam Daly must apply for permission before leaving Enmat Harbor."

"I know, but you didn't have to remind me now and shatter my dream!" Rozanne complained. "In truth, I would never become a Beyonder. It's far too dangerous. You never know when a stray bullet might kill you. From what I've seen, Beyonders are people who turn themselves into monsters in order to fight monsters."

"Archbishop Chanis said that we are guardians, but also a group of miserable wretches constantly struggling against threats and madness," Klein replied with a sigh. The words had left a lasting impression on him.

To fight the abyss, we must endure its corruption.

They fell silent together. Rozanne was the first to recover. She pursed her lips toward the partition.

"The captain wants to see you when you arrive."

"All right." Carrying his hat and cane, Klein passed through the partition and knocked before entering Dunn's office.

The middle-aged gentleman with the high hairline and deep, tranquil gray eyes set down his coffee cup and smiled.

"Daly was here."

"I can't say I'm surprised. Rozanne just told me," Klein replied with a smile.

Dunn ignored the joke and sighed.

"Daly has been transferred to the Backlund diocese. It's the busiest, most crowded city in the world, with the largest concentration of Beyonders and the greatest number of opportunities... She has a better chance than I do of becoming an archbishop or senior deacon."

"Why?" Klein asked curiously as he took a seat.

Dunn considered his answer for nearly twenty seconds.

"She has an exceptional gift for mastering and exploiting Sequence potions... I've already told you about the Nighthawks' internal rules. Before consuming the next potion in a pathway, a member must wait three years and pass a rigorous examination designed to prevent loss of control. Yet three years is usually nowhere near enough.

"It took me three years to advance from Sleepless to Midnight Poet. Going from Midnight Poet to Nightmare took nine--a full nine years. I've now spent three years attempting to advance beyond Nightmare to Sequence 6, and I have no idea how many more years I'll need.

"Once our bodies begin aging and our vitality declines, we shouldn't attempt another advancement even if we've overcome the potion's latent dangers. The risk of losing control becomes so great that no one is willing to gamble on it.

"Daly is different from me and from most Beyonders. Only a year after becoming a Corpse Collector, she submitted a special application asking to consume the next potion immediately. To everyone's astonishment, she passed the stricter examination and received the Gravedigger potion.

"It took her only one more year to advance from Gravedigger to Spirit Medium. Heh. This is merely her fifth year as a Beyonder, and she's only twenty-four. She still has many opportunities ahead of her."

To the public, she's the most famous spirit medium in Awwa County, but she's also a genuine Spirit Medium... Isn't that acting? Old Neil seemed to mention that Madam Daly showed similar tendencies... Klein felt he had identified the key to Daly's rapid advancement.

"Captain, you're still young yourself. You're only in your thirties," Klein said consolingly, while adding inwardly, Your memory simply isn't very good...

Dunn took a sip of coffee, shook his head, and smiled bitterly.

"Why didn't you ask Madam Daly how she mastered and exploited her Sequence potions?" Klein asked deliberately.

Dunn set down his cup and massaged his temples.

"She told me to become a true Nightmare... I don't know what that means."

Act the role of a Nightmare. Though Nightmare certainly sounds sinister... Klein frowned and temporarily fell silent.

Dunn took out his pipe and inhaled the aroma of its tobacco.

"Daly and I discussed the possibility that Clown is the potion following Seer. Assuming the Secret Order member didn't deceive you, she proposed an interesting theory."

"What theory?" Klein asked eagerly, his eyes brightening.

He had once used divination to determine whether Clown followed Seer. The answer had been vague, but close to a confirmation.

Dunn's deep gray eyes rested on him as he organized his thoughts.

"An ordinary Sequence pathway progresses in stages, with each advancement building upon a common theme. Sleepless, Midnight Poet, and Nightmare, for example, are plainly associated with the darkness of night, peaceful sleep, and the tranquility that sleep brings. We can assume the later Sequences possess similar characteristics, only with greater power and broader scope. They might involve secrets, disaster, terror, the crimson moon, and so forth.

"Some pathways appear less closely related, but careful examination still reveals common ground. Consider Assassin and Instigator. Both bring calamity, pain, sorrow, and despair to others. Their subsequent Sequences should continue that pattern."

Klein listened attentively. "But no such connection exists between Seer and Clown?"

"Correct." Dunn nodded. "Daly believes some Sequence pathways may follow a different kind of relationship. After all, there is still much we don't know."

He paused before continuing.

"In such a pathway, each Low- or Mid-Sequence potion would grant the Beyonder an apparently new ability unrelated to the others. At the point of qualitative transformation, all those abilities would combine into an extraordinarily powerful 'profession' encompassing them all.

"In other words, the pathway doesn't advance step by step. Instead, its component parts are separated first and combined later."

Klein listened intently but remained puzzled. Dunn raised his right hand.

"A normal Sequence pathway advances gradually, like a child growing older. The child becomes taller, stronger, heavier, and more mature."

"A special Sequence pathway, however, is more like..."

Dunn raised his thumb.

"This is Sequence 9."

Then he extended his index finger.

"This is Sequence 8."

One after another, he raised the remaining fingers.

"Every finger is independent and appears unrelated to the rest. But in the end..."

On the word "end," Dunn clenched all five fingers into a fist.

"I understand." Enlightenment dawned on Klein. He found both Daly's theory and the captain's analogy convincing.

Could that truly be how it works? He nodded thoughtfully.

Sequence 8 Clown and Sequence 9 Seer possess completely different, entirely new abilities. According to the Nighthawks' records, the corresponding Sequence 7 also bears no obvious resemblance to Clown...

After a brief silence, Klein asked curiously, "At what Sequence do the separate abilities combine and produce a qualitative change?"

Dunn took another sip of coffee and chuckled.

"Daly and I guessed Sequence 4!"

"Why?" Klein blurted.

"Because under the Churches' classification, Sequence 4 marks the beginning of the High Sequences. Merely reaching that level is said to cause a qualitative transformation in vitality and energy. During the Fourth Epoch, Beyonders of Sequence 4 were qualified to call themselves demigods. Unfortunately, they are extremely rare in the present age," Dunn said wistfully.

"If Sequences 4 through 1 are High-Sequence Beyonders, which are considered Low Sequence?" Klein asked with interest.

"A thousand years ago, Sequences 9 through 7 were all regarded as Low Sequences. In recent centuries, however, Beyonders have become so scarce that the Churches now classify Sequence 7 as Mid Sequence." Dunn laughed self-deprecatingly.

Sequences 9 and 8 are Low Sequences. Sequences 7 through 5 are Mid Sequences, while Sequence 4 and above are High Sequences... Klein repeated the classification inwardly and inevitably felt a surge of longing.

Emperor Roselle had been a High-Sequence Beyonder!

Yet the higher the Sequence, the greater the danger of losing control... The thought filled Klein with apprehension.

In an apparently casual tone, he asked, "What is the Church of the Goddess's Sequence 4 potion called?"

"I honestly don't know. My security clearance isn't high enough. I'll be able to read that information only if I become a diocesan bishop or a Nighthawk deacon." Dunn shook his head with a smile. "In fact, at least half of the Church's thirteen archbishops and nine senior deacons are below Sequence 4--and that may be an optimistic estimate. Archbishop Ince Zangwill, the wanted fugitive, lost control while attempting to advance to Sequence 4."

The man who stole Sealed Artifact 0-08? His potion was apparently called Gatekeeper... Klein tested another possibility. "Is Gatekeeper Sequence 5 of the Sleepless pathway?"

"No, it belongs to the Spirit Medium pathway. You'll be permitted to access that information when you reach Sequence 7 and become either a bishop or the captain of a Nighthawk team."

Gatekeeper is Sequence 5 of the Spirit Medium pathway? Does the name mean guarding the gates of Hell, or perhaps the gates of the Spirit World? Klein speculated.

"All right. Go see Old Neil and continue your studies." Dunn smiled. "And don't forget dinner tonight at Old Will's Restaurant. The reservation has already been made. I'll formally introduce you to the rest of the Nighthawks."

"All right. I've already prepared the money." Klein forced a smile.

"There's no need. Have you forgotten our additional bonuses? This one is for completing an assigned mission." Dunn waved dismissively.

Klein blinked, then broke into a delighted smile. "All right, Captain."

He turned and walked toward the door, counting inwardly.

Three, two, one... Huh? Why hasn't the captain called me back?

Klein stretched the final count out for as long as possible, astonished that Captain Dunn Smith had not forgotten to add anything.

A miracle...

In the armory, Old Neil glanced sideways at the cheerful Klein.

"Don't become so preoccupied with tonight's dinner that you neglect your studies. You still have a great deal to learn: more ritualistic magic, ancient Hermes, Dragonese, Elvish, and much else besides.

"And every afternoon except your day off, you need at least two hours of combat training with an instructor."

"Combat training? The captain never mentioned it..." Klein stared at him in surprise.

Old Neil nodded and answered without hesitation.

"He forgot."

Chapter 97: Combat Instructor

At two in the afternoon, Klein stood outside a plain, dilapidated two-story house on the outskirts of North Borough.

Dressed in his probationary inspector's uniform, he surveyed the weed-choked garden and the vines creeping up the walls, then turned in surprise.

"My combat instructor lives here?"

Shouldn't an instructor chosen by the Nighthawks be someone exceptional...?

Leonard Mitchell, who had guided him there, snickered. "Don't judge Mr. Gawain by the condition of his home. He was never granted a noble title, but in his day, he was a true knight."

The poetic Nighthawk, dressed in a white shirt, black trousers, and laceless leather boots, abruptly grew melancholy.

"He served during the twilight of the knights. Warriors clad in armor would charge through rifle and cannon fire, break the enemy lines, and reshape the battlefield. But then came high-pressure steam rifles and six-barreled machine guns. From that point onward, knights were gradually forced to withdraw from history.

"Mr. Gawain suffered the same fate. More than twenty years ago, Awwa's Order of Knights faced the most advanced weapons fielded by the Intis Republic... Sigh. Whenever I remember it, I feel as though I'm brushing dust from the bones of history. The poet in me stirs at the thought of that inevitable fate, yet regrettably, I don't know how to write the poem."

...Then what was the point of saying all that? Pretending not to notice Leonard's self-mockery, Klein offered a serious suggestion. "One of my university classmates told me that poetry requires a certain measure of talent. You should probably begin by reading *The Anthology of Classical Loen Poetry*."

Leonard's mood changed in an instant, and he answered lightly, "I bought that book long ago, along with others such as *Selected Poems of Roselle*. I'll work hard to become a true Midnight Poet, Mr. Seer."

Is he hinting at the acting method? Feigning incomprehension, Klein replied, "You'll also need a few books on grammar."

"All right, let's go inside." Leonard pushed open the half-closed iron gate, and they followed the path toward the house.

Before they reached the entrance, Klein saw a tall man step through the front door.

His blond hair was cut short, and white threaded his eyebrows. Age seemed to have ravaged his features, carving deep lines across his face.

"Why are you here?" the older man asked in a deep voice.

"Mr. Gawain, in accordance with your contract with the police department, this probationary inspector will be studying combat under your instruction," Leonard explained with a smile.

"Combat? No one needs to study combat in this age." Gawain looked at Klein through clouded eyes, his voice lifeless. "Learn to draw and fire a gun. Master the most advanced weapons."

Was this the trauma inflicted by six-barreled machine guns and high-pressure steam rifles? Klein wisely refrained from answering and turned to Leonard with a smile.

"Combat remains an essential skill for a police officer," Leonard said, plainly prepared for this objection. "Most criminals we encounter aren't people we can execute on sight, and some aren't armed at all. In those circumstances, we must rely on fighting techniques."

Gawain's expression darkened. He was silent for more than ten seconds before saying, "Throw a punch."

The command was directed at Klein.

Without his cane in hand, Klein recalled the boxing matches he had watched in his previous life. He raised an arm and punched forward.

Gawain's lips twitched almost imperceptibly. After a moment's thought, he said, "Kick."

Klein turned slightly, rotated his hips, tightened the muscles in his thigh, and kicked forward with his right leg.

"Ahem..." Gawain covered his mouth and cleared his throat. Looking at Leonard, he said, "I will honor my contract. But with a foundation like his, he must come four times a week and train for three hours at a time during the first month."

"You're the expert. I'll leave it to you." Leonard nodded without hesitation, then smiled at Klein. "See you at dinner."

After Leonard had passed through the iron gate, Klein asked curiously, "Instructor, where do we begin? Punches or footwork?"

As a seasoned keyboard warrior, he understood the importance of footwork in combat.

Gawain planted his hands on his hips and shook his head wearily.

"What you need is strength training.

"See those? Two solid-steel dumbbells. They'll be your companions today.

"You'll also perform squats, run, and jump rope. We'll take them one set at a time."

While Klein still stared blankly, Gawain's voice suddenly rose into a stern bark.

"Understood?"

"Understood!" For a moment, Klein felt as though he had returned to military training and stood once again before a merciless instructor.

"Change your clothes. There's a knight's training outfit on the sofa." Gawain abruptly sighed, turned, and walked toward the black steel dumbbells.

At six that evening, the Blackthorn Security Company's staff gathered around a corner table at Old Will's Restaurant.

Apart from Frye, who was guarding Chanis Gate, everyone was present: six Nighthawks and five civilian employees.

A white cloth covered the long table. Waiters brought platter after platter, divided the food, and served each guest individually.

Klein saw steaks covered in black-pepper sauce; bacon and sausages with mashed potatoes; egg pudding, asparagus, and specialty cheeses; even rose-colored champagne. Yet he had no appetite. That afternoon's training had nearly made him vomit.

Noticing the newly inducted Nighthawk's pale face and unfocused eyes, Dunn raised his glass of red wine and laughed.

"Let us welcome our newest official member, Klein Moretti. Cheers!"

Royale Reideen, the cold, introverted woman with black hair; Kenley White, the Sleepless; the disheveled Leonard Mitchell; and Seeka Tron, the white-haired, dark-eyed Midnight Poet, all raised their glasses and looked toward their team's newest member.

Suppressing his discomfort, Klein lifted his glass of amber champagne and stood.

"Thank you."

He clinked glasses with each Nighthawk, tilted back his head, and drained the small serving.

"Does our Miss Author have nothing to say for the occasion?" Dunn asked Seeka Tron with a smile.

Seeka was a woman in her thirties. Her features were ordinary, but she possessed an exceptional air of quiet serenity. Combined with the few silver strands in her hair, it gave her a distinctive charm.

Old Neil had told Klein that the Midnight Poet worked as an author on the side and regularly submitted stories to newspapers and magazines. Unfortunately, only a few minor publications had accepted them.

Seeka smiled at Dunn.

"Captain, if you want 'Miss Author' to become an accurate title, you should provide the funds for me to publish my work myself."

Dunn laughed.

"You should learn from Old Neil and invent a more convincing justification."

"Mr. Neil is the finest expert in that particular field!" Rozanne agreed between mouthfuls of roast mutton.

Amid the laughter and conversation, Leonard looked at Klein and chuckled.

"Are you too exhausted to eat?"

"Yes." Klein sighed.

"If you don't intend to touch your food, I can assist you." Leonard spoke as though his sole concern was preventing waste.

Klein did not mind. "Go ahead."

Thus, Leonard and the others consumed a substantial portion of the food served to him.

Near the end of dinner, the waiters brought out plates of beef pudding and ice cream.

Klein tasted the ice cream. It was cold, sweet, and surprisingly appetizing.

Before he realized it, he had finished the entire serving drizzled with blueberry sauce.

That, in turn, awakened his hunger--the ravenous need for nourishment that followed intense exertion.

Swallowing, Klein looked across the table, only to discover that every plate was empty. Not a scrap remained.

"Let us conclude the dinner with one final toast to Klein," Dunn proposed.

Before the captain could continue, Klein interrupted. "Captain, may I order another plate of food?"

The group fell silent, then burst into laughter.

"Haha! Your appetite has finally returned. Of course. Order two plates if you'd like." Dunn shook his head, laughing.

During the unbearably long wait that followed, Klein's stomach growled.

At last, a freshly prepared steak with black-pepper sauce was placed before him.

His knife and fork flew. Klein finished the medium-cooked steak in ninety seconds, nearly moved to tears as the flavor of meat juices and pepper sauce lingered in his mouth.

A moment later, he gazed at the empty plate and sighed contentedly. He set down his utensils and took a sip of champagne.

"Waiter, the bill, please," Dunn called.

The waiter went to the counter and returned with the check, which he itemized carefully.

"Five bottles of Desi champagne at twelve soli and three pence each; one small glass of Southville red wine at ten pence; each black-pepper steak at one soli and two pence; each serving of beef pudding at sixpence; and each serving of ice cream at one soli. The total is five pounds, nine soli, and sixpence."

Five pounds, nine soli, and sixpence? That's nearly my weekly salary! Eating at a restaurant truly is much more expensive than dining at home. Klein clicked his tongue inwardly. Fortunately, the captain had said he would not need to pay. The team's mission bonuses provided a fund for such expenses.

After calculating the cost, Klein realized that the alcohol accounted for most of it. Five bottles of champagne alone had cost more than three pounds.

So this world is no different from Earth in that respect... Klein discreetly rubbed his stomach and forced down the last of his champagne.

The next morning, Klein awoke feeling bloated. Still half asleep, he attempted to climb out of bed.

The instant he exerted himself, the pain in his muscles shocked him fully awake. His body seemed no longer to obey him.

"What a familiar feeling... It's just like the morning after we were punished with frog jumps. Today may be my day off, but I still need to visit my mentor and ask whether I can borrow the monograph about the Hornacis main peak from the university library..." Klein's lips twitched as he struggled out of the room.

Every step made him want to gasp.

"Klein, what happened to you?" Melissa had just emerged from the bathroom. She studied her brother's stiff posture and slow movements with suspicion.

Chapter 98: Mr. Azik

Confronted by his sister's question, Klein could only offer a rueful smile.

"Muscle soreness."

He had assumed that consuming a Sequence potion and becoming a Beyonder would improve his constitution. Harsh reality had informed him otherwise. A Seer's attribute points were evidently allocated to spirituality, intellect, intuition, and interpretation. The potion did nothing to help him adapt quickly to combat training.

The original Klein had devoted himself to his studies and suffered from malnutrition, leaving him in below-average physical condition. Lingering aftereffects from strenuous exercise were only to be expected.

"Muscle soreness? You came home after dinner last night and didn't do anything else... Does alcohol make your muscles ache?" Melissa asked inquisitively.

Does alcohol cause muscle soreness...? Little sister, that question can't help inspiring some inappropriate thoughts... Klein laughed dryly. "No, this has nothing to do with alcohol. It's from yesterday afternoon. I started the company's combat training."

"Combat?" Melissa grew even more astonished.

Klein organized his explanation. "This is the situation. After giving it some thought, I realized that as the historical and relic consultant for a security company, I can't remain forever in an office or a warehouse at the docks. One day, I may need to accompany my colleagues to a village, an ancient castle, or the site of some ruins. That might require hiking, crossing rivers, and walking great distances. I'll have to endure all manner of natural challenges, so I need to be in sufficiently good health."

"So you joined combat training to improve your endurance?" Melissa seemed to understand.

"Exactly," Klein confirmed emphatically.

Melissa frowned. "But that isn't very gentlemanly... Don't you always hold yourself to the standards of a professor? A professor needs only the ability to interpret historical documents, contemplate difficult questions, and maintain a polite, gentlemanly manner.

"Of course, I'm not saying those aren't useful skills. I prefer men who can solve their own problems, whether the solution requires intellect or physical strength."

Melissa smiled.

"No, no, no, Melissa. Your definition of a professor contains a fundamental misconception," Klein replied. "A true professor can communicate gently and courteously. But when communication fails, he can also raise his cane and persuade the other person through the principles of physics."

"The principles of physics..." Melissa stared blankly for a moment, then understood her brother's meaning and found herself unable to refute him.

Klein said nothing more. With great difficulty, he lengthened his stride and headed toward the bathroom.

Melissa watched him for several seconds, shook her head, and caught up.

"Do you need help?"

She raised her arms as though preparing to support him.

"No, there's no need. I was exaggerating a little before." Embarrassed, Klein abruptly straightened and began walking normally.

Melissa watched her brother stride steadily into the bathroom and close the door. She pursed her lips and muttered, "Klein is becoming more and more theatrical... I actually believed his muscles hurt that badly."

Behind the tightly closed bathroom door, Klein's face twisted in agony.

Ow, ow, ow... He held his breath, stiffened his body, and remained motionless for seven or eight seconds.

By the time he had struggled downstairs, eaten breakfast, and seen Benson and Melissa off, the soreness had finally begun to subside.

After resting a while longer, Klein took up his cane, donned his top hat, and left the house, strolling toward the public carriage stop.

In summer, Khoy University was shaded by leafy trees and filled with birdsong and abundant flowers. The campus seemed peaceful and serene.

Klein followed the river, turned toward the history department, and located the weathered three-story building that housed his mentor Cohen Quentin's office.

He knocked and entered, only to discover that the man seated behind his mentor's desk was the scholar Azik.

"Good morning, Mr. Azik. Where is my mentor? We arranged by letter to meet here at ten," Klein asked in confusion.

Azik, Cohen Quentin's closest friend and frequent opponent in academic debates, smiled.

"Cohen was summoned to an unexpected meeting at Tingen University. He asked me to wait here for you."

Azik had bronze skin, an average height and build, black hair, brown eyes, and mild features. His presence inspired an indescribable feeling, as though the vicissitudes of countless years were reflected in his gaze. A tiny mole lay beneath his right ear, almost impossible to notice without careful examination.

After explaining, Azik suddenly frowned and studied Klein intently.

Confused by the scrutiny, Klein looked down at his clothes. "Have I committed some breach of etiquette?"

Tuxedo, black vest, white shirt, black bow tie, dark trousers, laceless leather boots... Everything appears normal.

Azik's brow relaxed, and he chuckled softly.

"Pay me no mind. I merely noticed that you look much more energetic than before--more like a gentleman as well."

"Thank you for the compliment." Klein accepted it calmly. "Mr. Azik, did my mentor find *A Study of the Ruins on the Main Peak of the Hornacis Mountain Range* in the university library?"

"He did, with my assistance." Azik smiled gently, opened a drawer, and removed a gray-covered volume. "You are no longer a student of Khoy University, so you may read it here, but you cannot take it home."

"All right." Klein accepted the academic monograph with mingled delight and apprehension.

The book followed contemporary design conventions. Its hardcover was made from stiff paper and bore an abstract image of the Hornacis mountain range's main peak.

Klein glanced at it, found a seat, opened the cover, and began reading carefully, line by line.

After becoming absorbed in the text, he suddenly realized that a cup of rich, fragrant coffee had appeared beside him.

"Help yourself to milk and sugar." Azik set down the silver saucer and indicated a milk pitcher and sugar bowl.

"Thank you." Klein nodded gratefully.

He added three sugar cubes and a spoonful of milk, then resumed reading.

*A Study of the Ruins on the Main Peak of the Hornacis Mountain Range* was not especially long. Klein finished it shortly before noon and recorded several noteworthy conclusions.

First, the settlement on and around the main peak of the Hornacis mountain range had clearly possessed an advanced civilization and belonged to an ancient nation.

Second, judging by the murals, its inhabitants understood life much as modern humans did. For the time being, they could be assumed to have been human.

Third, they revered the darkness of night while also fearing it. Accordingly, they called their god the Ruler of Evernight and the Mother of the Sky.

Fourth, and strangest of all, researchers had found no graves anywhere in the region. At first glance, this suggested that the inhabitants had no need for burial because they never died. Yet the murals contradicted that conclusion. The people believed death was not the end and that deceased relatives would protect them during the night. For that reason, they kept the bodies of their loved ones at home, lying beside them on beds, for three full days.

The murals showed nothing beyond that and contained no depiction of burial.

Klein took another sip of coffee and continued recording his reflections in his notebook.

Mother of the Sky--what an exalted title. And Ruler of Evernight plainly overlaps with the Evernight Goddess... Is there some fundamental contradiction here?

Throughout the ruins on and around the Hornacis main peak, every object and decoration was remarkably well preserved. Even the murals showed no damage. Before the site's discovery, nothing appeared to have disturbed it... Cutlery remained arranged upon the tables, and dried traces of decay stained the plates... Some rooms still held half-filled bottles of liquor whose contents had nearly turned to water...

What happened to the inhabitants? They seemed to have abandoned their homes in a great hurry, taking nothing with them, and never returned.

The complete absence of burial grounds only makes it stranger.

The author, John Joseph, had written that upon first discovering the ruins, he had felt as though every resident had vanished in a single instant.

Klein stopped writing and turned his attention to an illustration.

During John Joseph's third expedition to the main peak of the Hornacis mountain range, he had used a new model of camera to take a monochrome photograph.

It showed a lofty palace built in a grand style, one wall collapsed and weeds growing everywhere.

The instant Klein saw it, he thought of the palace from his dream.

Their architectural styles were identical. The only difference was that the palace in his dream had stood upon a peak and was far more magnificent. It had also contained a colossal chair--a place of honor clearly not intended for a human. Beneath it, countless translucent maggots had clustered together and slowly writhed.

I can now confirm that my dream is connected to the ancient ruins on the main peak of the Hornacis mountain range... This must be the Nation of the Evernight mentioned in the Antigonus family's notebook... Klein nodded slightly and closed the book.

Across from him, Azik touched the inconspicuous mole beneath his right ear.

"Well? Did you find anything?"

"Quite a lot. Look at how many pages of notes I've written." Klein pointed to the table with a smile.

"I don't understand why you've suddenly become so interested in the subject." Azik sighed. "Klein, while studying at Backlund University, I dabbled in divination and conducted a fair amount of research into it. Well... I've noticed a certain disharmony in your fate."

What? Divination? You're discussing divination with me? As a Seer, Klein regarded the scholar with amusement.

"In what way is it disharmonious?"

Azik considered his words.

"Have you encountered an unusual number of coincidences during the past two months?"

"Coincidences?" Klein owed Azik a debt of gratitude, so he did not dismiss the question and instead began thinking seriously.

If we're speaking of coincidences, the most obvious occurred while we were pursuing the kidnappers. We happened to find clues to the Antigonus family's notebook--missing for days--in the room directly opposite theirs.

Then Ray Bieber chose not to flee Tingen. Instead, he found a place to digest the notebook's power, allowing Sealed Artifact 2-049 to locate him with ease. That contradicts common sense. Aiur Harson offered a reasonable explanation, but I've always felt the circumstances were suspiciously convenient...

And Selena glimpsed Hanass Vincent's secret incantation, yet waited until her birthday banquet to attempt it--on the very night I was there to discover her. That's another remarkable coincidence. Otherwise, Hanass Vincent might not have been the only one to die so abruptly...

After several minutes of careful thought, Klein answered, "Three. Neither an excessive number nor especially frequent. Furthermore, there was no indication that anyone orchestrated or guided them."

Azik nodded slightly.

"As Emperor Roselle once said, anyone may encounter one coincidence. Two are still within reason. At three, one should begin considering what hidden factor is producing them."

"Can you tell me anything else?" Klein pressed.

Azik laughed and shook his head.

"I can sense only that something is out of harmony, nothing more. You must remember that I'm not a genuine seer."

Isn't that essentially saying nothing? Mr. Azik is rather strange... He's playing the charlatan in front of a charlatan like me... Klein exhaled. As Azik rose, Klein seized the opportunity to pinch his glabella and activate Spirit Vision.

Azik's aura appeared clearly before him, and everything seemed perfectly normal.

Unfortunately, I can see a person's Ether Body and Astral Projection only from above the gray fog... Klein thought idly as he stood and tapped his glabella again.

Chapter 99: The Red Chimney

Late that afternoon, Klein returned home and drew the curtains, plunging his bedroom into darkness.

He took out pen and paper and pondered for a long time before finally writing a sentence:

"Elliott's kidnapping was caused by Beyonder influences."

As a Seer, Klein had already attempted to determine whether the coincidences resulted from supernatural interference. His divinations had said they did not.

Influenced by Azik's warning, however, he decided to examine the incidents again. He had also learned from his encounter with the suited clown. This time, he carefully constructed an appropriate divination statement, eliminating every vague or potentially misleading phrase.

"Yes. I should separate the three coincidences and divine each one independently..." Klein nodded thoughtfully and slowly removed the topaz pendant from his wrist.

Holding the spirit pendulum in his left hand, he let it hang just above the sentence on the paper.

He composed himself and entered Cogitation. Closing his eyes, he repeated, "Elliott's kidnapping was caused by Beyonder influences."

After reciting the statement again and again, Klein opened his eyes. The topaz was slowly rotating counterclockwise.

"Still negative..." he murmured.

He devised several alternative statements, but every result remained the same: nothing supernatural had influenced the incident.

Next, he separately divined the reasons behind Ray Bieber's decision to remain in Tingen and Selena's magic-mirror divination. Both answers indicated that the events had been entirely normal.

Heh. Did I, a genuine Seer, allow a charlatan like Mr. Azik to frighten me? Besides, neither the captain nor the others sensed that anything was wrong... Klein laughed and shook his head. Nevertheless, caution compelled him to perform dream divinations for final confirmation.

After some thought, he adjusted the statement to suit the new method.

"The true reason for Elliott's kidnapping."

His fountain pen paused while he considered the wording.

After reading it several times, he tore off the slip of paper, walked to his bed, relaxed, and lay down.

With the statement in hand and the aid of Cogitation, Klein swiftly fell asleep.

He found himself within a distorted, fragmented world. Upon regaining awareness, he began moving through the blur.

Gradually, he saw the kidnappers. He watched them lose their last chips at a gambling table, acquire firearms through illicit channels, and survey the neighborhood. He saw them rent the apartment opposite Ray Bieber's as their hideout.

The events did not form a continuous scene, appearing instead as a succession of flashing images. Klein found nothing abnormal among them.

They also agreed with the statements given by the kidnappers.

After leaving the dream, Klein performed dream divinations on the other two incidents, but received the same answers. Every development followed an understandable chain of cause and effect. The coincidences truly seemed to be coincidences.

"I was indeed overthinking it. Mr. Azik is merely a divination enthusiast..." Klein steadied the pendulum and shook his head with a wry smile.

He was about to open the curtains and let sunlight into the room when he froze.

From the original Klein's memories, Mr. Azik is a dependable and trustworthy man. He never made baseless claims. Even when he argued with my mentor, their disputes were limited to academic questions, and both sides always had sound reasons... If he were merely a divination enthusiast, he wouldn't have approached me in that manner. And the original Klein's memories contain no indication that Azik was interested in divination... Of course, the relevant memories might simply be among those I've lost.

Klein frowned, unable to dismiss his unease. He needed some way to confirm the truth.

He suspected that Mr. Azik had accidentally learned privileged information and used divination as a pretext to warn him.

"How can I verify it?" Klein paced through the darkened room, reviewing every divination method he knew.

One step, two steps, three steps. He stopped abruptly as an idea occurred to him.

"Assume these coincidences truly are suspicious. Perhaps I can't obtain the correct answer because my Sequence is too low or some outside force is interfering. But I can change the environment. I can perform the divination somewhere even more mysterious and incomprehensible!"

Excitement surged through Klein. He opened a drawer and removed a silver dagger.

Concentrating, he extended his spirituality from its tip and merged it with his surroundings.

As he walked around the room, a wall of spirituality rose and sealed the space.

Klein intended to perform the divination above the gray fog, within that mysterious world.

In the majestic ancient palace above the endless gray fog, Klein sat in the place of honor at one end of the bronze table. Before him lay a sheet of goatskin parchment he had willed into existence.

He picked up a pen and wrote the same divination statement as before:

"Elliott's kidnapping was caused by Beyonder influences."

He raised the spirit pendulum and allowed it to hang low over the parchment. Klein quickly composed himself, becoming silent and ethereal.

With his eyes half closed, he repeated the statement seven times, using his spirituality to commune with the spiritual world that transcended all things.

Feeling the pull on the silver chain, Klein opened his eyes and looked at the pendulum.

The sight made him freeze.

The topaz was rotating clockwise!

That meant a Beyonder influence had indeed been involved in Elliott's kidnapping.

The result was the exact opposite of the answer he had received in the outside world.

There was no trace of interference... Such power and such methods are terrifying. What does the mastermind want? Is my fate entangled with the Antigonus family's notebook? Profoundly shaken, Klein lost his composure, and the pendulum's rotation dissolved into chaos.

He lowered the topaz and rubbed his glabella, his expression exceptionally grave.

After several seconds of thought, he did not immediately divine the other two events. Instead, he wrote a new statement:

"The true reason for Elliott's kidnapping."

Holding the paper, he recited the words seven times. Then Klein leaned back in his chair and fell asleep above the gray fog.

Soon, he saw a boundless, illusory expanse of gray-white mist.

The fog slowly parted, revealing a colorful lawn crowded with flowers.

Behind the flowers and grass, space folded in upon itself like a living monster.

Klein strained to see beyond it and barely discerned a dark-red chimney.

At that moment, the scene shattered, ending the dream.

Klein abruptly sat upright in the majestic palace, his heart pounding for no apparent reason.

Whew... It feels as though I just spied on something terrifying... He took two deep breaths and gradually calmed his chaotic emotions.

Tap. Tap. Tap. After some time, Klein began tapping the edge of the table and sank into thought.

A red chimney... a garden... a lawn... How are they connected to the person behind all this? The coincidences reveal neither the mastermind's objective nor whether that person bears me any malice...

As he considered the situation, Klein felt a growing sense of alarm--for himself, the captain, Frye, and the others.

We're like puppets dancing on strings. Worse still, we thought ourselves clever and capable...

Sigh... I don't know how to tell the captain. Old Neil obtained the same result I did in the outside world. If they ask me to demonstrate or confirm my findings, I have no way to do so... Klein rubbed his aching temples.

After nearly twenty seconds, he regained his composure and began divining Ray Bieber's decision to remain in Tingen. As before, he started with the spirit pendulum.

This time, the result astonished him. The topaz remained perfectly motionless, neither confirming nor denying the statement.

"Strange..." he muttered. As he considered possible explanations, an unsettling thought emerged. "Did the mastermind sense my divination and take countermeasures?"

He followed with dream divination, but saw nothing except fragmented pieces of fog. He made no further discoveries.

Selena's magic-mirror divination produced the same result.

By then, Klein was nearly certain of his conjecture. Unable to warn Captain Dunn Smith for the moment, he felt an unprecedented urgency to increase his strength.

I need to visit the Divination Club later and accelerate my "acting" so I can digest the Seer potion... I must also confirm whether Clown truly is the next Sequence and gather clues about its formula... And I need more contact with Mr. Azik, so I can uncover whatever privileged information he possesses...

Klein rested his forehead in his right palm as he swiftly formulated a plan and identified his priorities.

After further consideration, he conjured another sheet of goatskin parchment and wrote:

"Sequence 8 of the Seer pathway is Clown."

From his previous experiences, Klein was now completely convinced that the gray fog amplified and enhanced his divination abilities.

"It's like how obtaining rare items in games is usually a matter of luck... Is this the result of having good fortune?" he muttered, lifting his spirit pendulum.

A short while later, he received a definite answer.

Sequence 8 of the Seer pathway was indeed Clown!

He wrote another statement on the parchment:

"Sequences 8, 7, 6, and 5 of the Seer pathway will each grant at least one new and unrelated ability."

Klein exhaled and performed another pendulum divination.

The topaz remained motionless, showing no rotation at all.

"Is there insufficient information to complete the divination and receive a revelation?" he wondered aloud.

He lowered the silver chain and began considering the proper statement for a dream divination.

Nearly twenty seconds later, he raised his fountain pen and carefully wrote:

"Clues to the Clown potion."

Chapter 100: Interpreting the Symbols

"Clues to the Clown potion."

Seated in the place of honor at the ancient bronze table, Klein repeated the divination statement several times. Then he leaned back and sank into a deep sleep.

His surroundings swiftly became peaceful and still. A hazy vista appeared before him, countless blurred and distorted images flashing past like drops of morning dew upon tender petals.

Gradually, Klein gathered his spirituality and regained awareness.

A fireplace stood before him, with a rocking chair positioned in front of it. An old woman dressed in black and white sat in the chair.

Her head hung low, concealing her face, but Klein's intuition told him with considerable certainty that she was elderly.

The woman faced a desk covered with newspapers and silver-inlaid tin containers.

"This is..." The scene looked extremely familiar. Klein quickly recognized it.

This was the house where Ray Bieber had lived with his mother!

It was where Klein had seen a bloated corpse for the first time.

"There are clues to the Clown potion here?" As the thought flashed through his mind, the scene transformed.

He saw a gray-white warehouse hidden among rows of identical buildings.

White bones lay scattered everywhere, together with several masses of flesh that appeared to have been crushed beneath a boulder.

At the warehouse's center rested a gray-white object the size of a fist. Grooves covered its surface, and it looked simultaneously soft and resilient, like a brain freshly removed from a living creature.

Just as Klein recognized the place and recalled what had happened there, the image rippled like disturbed water and dissolved into another indistinct scene.

A naked corpse lay on a long table draped in white cloth. Bluish patches of discoloration marked its skin.

Klein frowned abruptly and murmured, "First Ray Bieber's hiding place and remains, and now something connected to the mark on the suited clown's wrist?"

As he tried to interpret the scenes, the vision changed once more.

He saw a marble coffee table, two leather sofas, and a chandelier suspended high overhead.

Three people occupied the room: Klein Moretti, with black hair, brown eyes, and a scholarly air; a wealthy, pale-skinned man with a plump figure; and a beautiful young woman wearing fishnet gloves.

Then the image gave way to three other people and a single object: a middle-aged man in a black robe with thick, spiky brown hair; the same plump, pale-skinned young man; an elderly gentleman in his fifties with untidy eyebrows, thinning brown hair, and gray-blue eyes; and, on the round table between them, a black notebook radiating an ancient, distant aura.

The Antigonus family's notebook!

Klein abruptly sat upright, and the dream vanished.

Gazing beyond the divine hall at the boundless gray fog and crimson stars, he thought in astonishment and confusion.

I was divining clues to the Clown potion... Why did the Antigonus family's notebook appear?

Let me think. That plump man was Welch. Yes, Welch--the unfortunate fellow who purchased the Antigonus family's notebook and set off the entire chain of events. The beautiful young woman in fishnet gloves was Naya...

I remember now. The marble coffee table and leather sofas were distinctive features of Welch's home. I saw Spirit Medium Daly there.

In other words, that was Welch's living room, and I witnessed the original Klein discussing the notebook with his two classmates.

Klein calmed himself and tapped the edge of the long bronze table in a steady rhythm.

Then what did the final scene represent? Welch and the notebook were both present. Could it have shown the moment he purchased the ancient artifact?

There were two other men, and one of them looked extremely familiar. I know I've seen the middle-aged man in the classical black robe somewhere before... That spiky brown hair, those severe dark circles beneath his eyes... Yes! Hanass Vincent from the Divination Club. The same Hanass Vincent who "died peacefully" after the captain entered his dream, once we learned that Selena had secretly copied an incantation from him!

Don't tell me he was the person who sold the notebook to Welch.

Everything has come full circle. The world is small--no, Tingen is small! On careful consideration, the possibility makes perfect sense. Hanass Vincent was no ordinary fortune-teller. He was deeply involved in mysticism and had attracted the attention of an ancient evil god. He possessed the contacts, ability, and opportunity to acquire the notebook accidentally released by the Secret Order...

No wonder the captain and the others never discovered where Welch obtained it. Their entire approach was wrong. They investigated the antique market... Then, after locating the notebook itself, they abandoned that line of inquiry.

What a pity Hanass Vincent died only recently. Otherwise, we might have learned something about the notebook from him. Given his involvement with mysticism, he surely studied it... His death was far too coincidental!

But another man was present--the gentleman in his fifties. He may know a great deal about what happened.

Klein stopped tapping the table and reviewed every scene from the dream divination.

Ray Bieber's house. Ray Bieber's hiding place. Ray Bieber's remains. The mark on the suited clown's wrist. Welch's home. The discussion between Welch, Naya, and the original Klein. And finally, Welch, Hanass Vincent, and the Antigonus family's notebook together... Heh. Apart from the clown's mark, every image is directly connected to the Antigonus family's notebook!

But I was divining clues to the Clown potion... This makes neither scientific nor mystical sense!

After becoming a Seer, Klein had attempted to divine where Welch acquired the Antigonus family's notebook. At the time, he had not thought to exploit the special properties of the world above the gray fog, so the divination had revealed nothing. Now, while searching for something else entirely, he had stumbled upon the truth.

It took him nearly twenty seconds to regain his calm. Then he combined the dream's context with the information from Roselle's diary and began interpreting its symbolism.

The first possibility: Zaratul--or rather, the Secret Order--is pursuing the Antigonus family's relics. The dream may be telling me to use something associated with the Antigonus family as bait, draw out the Secret Order, and obtain the Clown potion formula from them.

The second possibility: the Clown potion formula is recorded directly in the Antigonus family's notebook... The Zaratul family's pursuit of Antigonus relics implies a profound connection between them. They may have been allies or enemies. In either case, it would be natural for the Antigonus family to possess part of their Sequence pathway. If they were allies, the explanation is obvious; if they were enemies, then each would have studied the other more closely than anyone else...

But the second interpretation doesn't explain the mark on the suited clown's wrist. Sigh. I certainly hope it's correct. Once the Holy Cathedral finds an expert capable of interpreting the notebook, I could obtain the Clown potion without taking any risk.

The first interpretation still seems more plausible. Yet my intuition as a Seer tells me the dream may contain a deeper meaning.

With that thought, Klein rubbed his forehead and suddenly recognized the limitations of a Seer.

Unless a revelation was extraordinarily simple and direct, its interpretation demanded the utmost care. A Seer was forever walking along the edge of an abyss or crossing a lake on thin ice. The suited clown's fate was a vivid and bloody example of what could follow from a single mistaken interpretation or the failure to grasp one crucial point.

In that instant, Klein felt as though he had touched the true essence of being a Seer. Only one final step seemed to separate him from completely digesting the potion.

"Thank you for enlightening me with your life... Praise the Lady!" he murmured, tracing the crimson moon across his chest.

He then performed divinations to determine whether Azik meant him well and whether Azik was a powerful Beyonder. Both produced affirmative answers.

Eventually, the succession of divinations exhausted Klein. He had no choice but to halt his racing thoughts and identify the most urgent matters before him.

I must find the man who appeared with Welch, Hanass Vincent, and the Antigonus family's notebook as quickly as possible!

The Divination Club is the logical place to begin searching.

I can't simply confront Mr. Azik. He may be a Mid-Sequence Beyonder from the Life School of Thought, but I lack enough information to divine that possibility...

Whew. Klein exhaled and conjured a portrait of the gentleman in his fifties upon the goatskin parchment before him. The man had untidy eyebrows, thinning brown hair, and gray-blue eyes.

This was the third person present when Welch acquired the Antigonus family's notebook from Hanass Vincent.

Looking at the portrait, Klein suddenly faced a dilemma.

I can't draw. In elementary-school art class, I always received the harshest criticism from the teachers.

Should I use ritualistic magic like Old Neil? But that requires praying to the Goddess... If I combine it with the unique properties of the world above the gray fog, I'll be in serious trouble if the gods notice anything unusual!

Wait. Perhaps I can pray to myself! Transmitting an image should be similar to transmitting a voice... Although I can't yet access the mysterious power above the gray fog directly, something this minor ought to be possible!

The moment the idea occurred to him, Klein released his spirituality, enveloped himself, and simulated the sensation of falling.

Back in his bedroom, he lit the gas lamp and began to "pray."

"The Fool that doesn't belong to this era;

"You are the mysterious ruler above the gray fog;

"You are the King of Yellow and Black who wields good luck;

"I pray for your revelation. I pray that you allow me to draw what I have seen."

After reciting the incantation, Klein neither poured essential oils nor burned herbs to draw upon their power.

Praying to oneself was delightfully informal.

Whispers suddenly filled his ears, and the four black dots on the back of his hand emerged to form a square.

He took four counterclockwise steps while repeating the incantation, passed through the frenzied chaos, and returned above the gray fog.

This time, none of the crimson stars contracted or expanded. Instead, the strange symbol behind the place of honor at the long bronze table--part pupil-less eye, part contorted lines--shimmered faintly and produced illusory prayers.

Klein leaned close and listened. After confirming that everything was correct, he conjured the third man's portrait and cast it toward the flowing light in the same manner he would answer a prayer.

Once finished, he immediately left the mysterious world above the gray fog and returned to his bedroom.

As soon as his feet found the floor, the portrait surfaced clearly before his eyes. At the same time, he felt a faint, illusory force augmenting him.

He picked up a fountain pen, found a sheet of white paper, and focused on his intention.

To his astonishment, his right hand began moving beyond his control, swiftly laying down line after line.

Before long, a lifelike portrait of the third man appeared on the page.

After noting the man's hair and eye color along with his other distinguishing features, Klein sighed in relief despite the spasms in his right hand.

The illusory image before his eyes rapidly faded.
